The modifier load-bearing filter needed no edit either, it takes decorative rates from 0-96% with the budget disabled to 0% with it on, the one exception being rhombille (3/25 sonar, 6/25 compass), which is `TILING_LOAD_BEARING_BUDGET` expiring rather than the filter missing, since a rhombille attempt is the dearest of the six.
- **`wormSegmentSize` DID need one, and the way it was written is why.** It read "hex gets a pitch-wide segment, everything else gets the 4.8.8 clamp", so the four Laves lattices silently inherited a clamp sized for an interstitial square none of them has, and worms drew at 0.40 to 0.52 of the step they crawl, gaps of 27 to 44px at a 50px pitch, three to four times worse than the 2026-07-22 honeycomb bug that same function already carries a regression test for. It is inverted now: the 4.8.8 is the exception (its two cell sizes make the small diamond binding) and everything else gets the pitch, which for every isohedral tiling IS its inscribed diameter because `assembleTiling` normalizes to exactly that. The lesson generalizes past worms, **a per-tiling branch whose DEFAULT is the 4.8.8's special case will be wrong for the next lattice, silently**, and the new pin is written to match: it walks `TILING_TYPES`, measures each lattice's own mean center-to-center step from its geometry, and requires the segment to span it, so a seventh tiling is covered the day it is registered rather than the day someone remembers to extend a list. `worm` is in `TILING_SAFE_GIMMICKS`, so `?coastline=rhombille:worm` reaches this.
- **e2e:** `tests/e2e/tilingResize.spec.mjs` covers all six, and its assertion had to be corrected to do so. It used to pin "the pitch went DOWN on rotation to landscape", which passed only because both of the earlier tilings are tall, rhombille and deltoidal are WIDE AND SHORT, so landscape hands them more room and their pitch GROWS (measured 26 → 35 and 27 → 33). It now pins `boardW / pitch`, the tiling's own extent ratio, which is a constant of the tiling and survives every re-lay in either direction. The four new tilings matter to this spec beyond repeating the original frozen-geometry bug: each carries a per-cell clip-path, so a re-lay that recomputed the box but reused a stale clip-path would leave the polygon and its box disagreeing.

**Sonar and compass region geometry is single-sourced in `adjacency.js`** (`sonarScanCells` / `compassRayCells`), consumed by BOTH `recomputeDisplayedMines` (what the player is shown) and `buildStaticGimmickConstraints` (what the certifier proves from). Those were two hand-copied loops until Coastline; drift between them is a silent no-guess hole, since the certifier would deduce from a premise the board never displayed and no test would notice. `test/adjacency.test.mjs` pins that the region the player SEES equals the region the certifier PROVES from, on a walled board, and the guard was break-tested against a one-cell drift. The PLAYER-facing consumer of the same geometry is the region highlight (`showGimmickRegion` in boardRenderer.js: hover previews, click/tap/Enter PINS): its classes live on cell elements while `updateCell` rebuilds `className` wholesale, so `updateCell`'s shared tail re-applies them from a cached member set and a pinned region dies only by unpinning, before that, flagging a cell inside the region stripped the highlight from exactly the cell being counted, invisibly on desktop (the next hover repainted it) and permanently on touch (Christopher's report, 2026-08-01; the pin exists to ease flag counting). Pinned by `tests/e2e/regionHighlight.spec.mjs` on a frozen seed + frozen click policy, with the flag assertion deliberately made BEFORE any pointer movement so the hover fallback cannot mask the wipe.

**Sonar transfers to a tiling; it reads "within two steps" as graph distance** (a depth-2 breadth-first walk) when an explicit topology is present. That is the honest generalization rather than a convenience: on an UNWALLED rectangle the depth-2 closure of the 8-neighborhood is EXACTLY the 5×5 block, so the graph reading and the geometric one already agree wherever both are defined (pinned as its own test, if it ever fails, the generalization is the wrong one). Rectangular boards keep the literal 5×5 scan verbatim, walls severing only the inner ring. That inner-ring-only rule is NOT the same as graph distance once walls exist (the outer ring reaches through a wall the inner ring is blocked by) and looks more like an accident than a principle, but it is the rule players have been solving against and the help text says "a 5×5 area", so changing it is a design call rather than a refactor's side effect. **No board has ever combined sonar with walls** (measured across all 144 canonicals, 2026-07-19), so the quirk has never actually manifested in production. Byte-identical square-board behavior was verified by recomputing the number layers of every one of those 144 canonicals and diffing against a `main` control: zero `displayedMines` differences on both sides.
**Pressure plates follow the topology (2026-07-19), and the plate's demand region is single-sourced.** `plateDisarmCells` in `adjacency.js` answers "which cells must be revealed before this plate disarms", and is read by BOTH the live `checkPlateDisarmed` (gameActions.js) and the target set of `estimatePlateMovesToDisarm` (boardSolver.js). Those were two copies of one rule; drift between them prices the countdown for a different job than the player is actually being asked to finish, a mis-timed board rather than a broken one, and silently so. The estimator's other two loops (Pass-A deduction, cascade) now read `buildNeighborCache`. **The wall asymmetry is DELIBERATE and pinned:** the demand region ignores walls, while deduction and cascade respect them. That is coherent rather than a bug, the plate states what must end up revealed, walls constrain the reasoning and the flood that get you there, and a severed cell is still reachable from its own side, but it is exactly the kind of thing a well-meaning refactor "fixes", which is why a test asserts the walled neighbor is still demanded. On an explicit topology the distinction dissolves, since a severed link is just absent from the edge list. Plates are NOT in `DAILY_SAFE_GIMMICKS` (challenge/chaos only), so no canonical board includes one and there is no stored-board blast radius. Verified by a PLATE-PRICING differential (3840 estimates: 8 gimmick combinations × 20 seeds × 3 shapes × 4 partial reveal fractions, fingerprinting `{moves, steps, unsolved}` plus the derived `plateSeconds`) hashing identically to a `main` worktree. Reveal fractions matter, the Pass-A loop and cascade only fire on already-revealed cells, so a fully-fogged fingerprint would exercise almost nothing. Control-tested by making the target set wall-aware, which moves the hash and so also proves the asymmetry is a real distinction rather than a cosmetic one.

**Locked and mirror follow the topology (2026-07-19).** `isLockedCell`, `applyLocked`'s accessible-safe-neighbor check, and `applyMirrorPairs`' partner search each walked the 8-neighborhood by coordinate; all three now read `buildNeighborCache`. This was a TRUE no-op on rectangles rather than a judgment call like sonar's, because each already paired its coordinate walk with a `hasWallBetween` check, which is exactly what the cache computes, and the cache iterates `dr`/`dc` in the same order the hand-rolled loops did, so candidate and partner ORDER is preserved and the seeded shuffle consumes the RNG identically. `isLockedCell` gained an optional `neighborCache` param (pass one from a loop; both current callers are single-cell click checks). It must agree with the certifier's own unlock model (`canUnlock` in boardSolver.js, which has always read the cache): if the two disagree the solver certifies an unlock order the live game refuses. **`applyLocked`'s interior-only candidate rule (`r`/`c` never on the container edge) is skipped on an explicit topology**, the container's border is an arbitrary set of cells with respect to the graph, so it made ordinary tiling cells unplaceable, and on a degenerate N×1 container it would exclude every cell and silently place nothing. The accessible-safe-neighbor test is the constraint that actually matters and applies either way; rectangular boards keep the rule verbatim. Verified byte-identical on square boards by a GENERATION differential (`applyGimmicks` over 15 gimmick combinations × 24 seeds × 4 board sizes = 1440 boards, fingerprinting placement + walls + both number layers + the `applied` payload) hashing identically to a `main` worktree, and the differential was itself control-tested by perturbing candidate order, which moves the hash. A recompute-only check could not have caught this class, since placement runs at generation time.

**The par FEATURE VECTOR follows the topology too (2026-07-23).** `computeDailyFeatures` had the last two rectangular assumptions outside generation, and both produced a plausible number rather than an error. `wallEdgeCount` read `board._wallEdges`, but `applyWallsTiling` severs links out of `_cellNeighbors` and stores the removed pairs in a render-only `board._tilingWalls`, so **every walled tiling board reported ZERO walls** (measured: 9 and 10 severed edges reported as 0). It now asks whichever wall layer the board stores. `zeroClusterCount` flooded a hardcoded 8-neighborhood over `(row, col)`, which on a tiling is pure STORAGE, `containerFor` returns any exact factorization, so 63 hexagons ship as a 7×9 container and a prime cell count ships as 1×N, so it counted components of a grid the board is not (measured: 8 vs 5 on a plain 4.8.8, 3 vs 2 on a plain honeycomb, 2.6× off on the 1×113 container). It now floods `buildNeighborCache`. **The rectangular half was a real pre-existing bug too:** the old walk never consulted `_wallEdges`, so it merged zero-clusters that walls genuinely separate and disagreed with the cascade the player sees. Fixing both halves rather than branching was Christopher's call, taken against the measured blast radius, a differential over every canonical (148 daily + 13 weekly) moves par on **7 boards, all by +0.1s, largest +0.235%**, and all 7 carry walls. The two wall units are NOT identical and the model does not pretend otherwise: a rectangular wall edge blocks one orthogonal step plus up to two diagonals, a tiling wall edge blocks exactly one graph edge, and sharing `secPerWallEdge` across topologies is a modeling assumption each shape's own wallEdge DEVIATION can undo once earned (the per-shape equations of 2026-08-01; until then the shared slope stands). Pinned in `test/tilingFeatures.test.mjs`, break-tested against the un-fixed feature on both halves.
- **The 8 walled dailyMeta rows that disagree on `zeroClusterCount` are THAT change, and are deliberately left alone.** Making the count wall-aware changed the DEFINITION of a feature whose old value is already frozen in write-once `dailyMeta` rows, so `2026-04-29`, `05-08`, `05-11`, `05-12`, `05-16`, `05-23`, `05-27` and `06-06` store the old wall-BLIND count and recompute one cluster higher. Every one carries walls (18 walled canonicals exist; these are the 8 whose clusters a wall actually separates), every one is a PAST date, and every one was already played. It only became visible when issue #180 inverted the sweep to hard-fail by default and correctly promoted `zeroClusterCount` from warn-only to a real guard; the nightly sweeps FUTURE dates only, so it has never fired on them and cannot. **Left as-is on purpose:** the stored meta is what both the par fit and the daily-history chart read, so every client agrees with every other and the fit is self-consistent with what was stored, whereas rewriting write-once rows for boards people have played would perturb the fit's own inputs to buy agreement with a definition that postdates them. The cost of leaving it is +1 cluster ≈ +0.1s of par on those 8 boards, which is the same measurement that shipped with the fix. If they are ever regenerated for another reason, the mismatch resolves itself.

Also still rectangular but pre-existing and unrelated to topology: `redistributeMines`' mine-density ranking loop. **The power-up blast shapes CONVERTED 2026-08-03** (his petals report: "the sonar and scan for petals doesn't look right", Scan swept a container row/column, X-Ray/Magnet used container rectangles): on an explicit topology X-Ray reads the depth-2 CORNER-INCLUSIVE ball (`xRayScan` through `sonarScanCells`, the sonar-precedent port his challenge-250 ruling named, mine-information tools following mine-information adjacency; only the worm crawler is side-only), Magnet extracts over the depth-1 neighborhood, and **SCAN sweeps the true horizontal + vertical LINES through the target** (`scanLines`: every cell whose convex polygon the line strictly passes through, vertex-range spanning, exact and parameter-free; his correction 2026-08-03 rejecting the first-cut depth-2 ball: "Scan should move in some reasonable way horizontally and vertically, not a blob", Scan's identity is the crossing sweep, and on the 4.8.8 an octagon-centered sweep is EXACTLY its octagon row, test-pinned); rectangles keep row+column / 5×5 / 3×3 verbatim (goldens in `test/powerUpRegions.test.mjs`). `xRayScan` now RETURNS its area so the action layer highlights exactly what the logic read (its old inline ±2 highlight loop was a second copy of the geometry), and the power-up button tooltips go dynamic in `updatePowerUpBar` (a tiling board describes the ball, a rectangle the block). **The blast shapes were converted; the REPAIR after a blast was not, and that was issue #274 (fixed 2026-08-10).** `recalcAreaAdjacency`, the scoped recompute `shieldDefuse`/`defuseMine` run after removing a mine, walked the container 3x3, so on a lattice a shield break left 2 to 8 cells still counting a mine that was off the board, with the ✓ Certified chip still on screen and the stale value reaching the player directly (`recomputeDisplayedMines` only DERIVES from `adjacentMines`, it never recomputes it). It now derives its visit set from the neighbor cache it was already building, the removed mine plus exactly its neighbors, since adjacency is symmetric, which is the same "answer *which cells* once" rule `sonarScanCells` and `plateDisarmCells` follow. **The honeycomb's apparent immunity was the sample, not the lattice**: 38.5% of hex cells have a neighbor outside their container 3x3 (against 98.7-100% for the other five, and 0% for a rectangle), so a different patch size breaks it too, which is why `test/shieldDefuseTopology.test.mjs` asserts each fixture actually contains escaping cells before trusting a pass, and caught a vacuous hex patch doing it. With plates, the par features, the blast shapes and the post-blast repair converted, what remains rectangular is generation heuristics only.

**The clue-digit ceiling is GONE (2026-07-27), in both languages at once.** `clueShares` in dailyFeatures.js used to bin into a nine-wide histogram (`if (v >= 0 && v <= 8) hist[v]++`), and its R twin, `clue_histogram`, now `clue_digit_counts` in `scripts/refit-par-model.R`, did the same. That quietly assumed no cell can touch more than eight others: true on a rectangle and on both tilings shipped through 2026-07-22, and false the moment a higher-valence lattice arrives. A digit above 8 fell out of the NUMERATOR AND THE DENOMINATOR, so all four shares described a smaller board than the one that exists, nothing threw, the shares still summed to at most 10, and the wrong number would have landed in a write-once `dailyMeta` row (the `zeroClusterCount` precedent). It stopped being latent with tilings #5 and #6: **a rhombille rhombus has 10 corner-inclusive neighbors and a deltoidal kite 9**, and over 2000 random layouts each, rhombille drops a clue on 0.7% of boards at density 0.30 and 2.0% at 0.34, rare, and silent, which is the point. Both halves now count into the four reported buckets plus a running denominator with NO upper bound, so bucketing has no ceiling for the next lattice to reintroduce. **Byte-identical on history:** every rectangular board caps at 8, so all 148 daily + 13 weekly canonicals recompute unchanged, no `dailyMeta` row moves (unlike the `zeroClusterCount` change), and no `FEATURES_EPOCH` bump is needed, the bucket boundaries and the `5plus` lump are untouched, so no key was added or redefined for a rectangle. The two copies remain ONE definition in two languages and must keep changing together: the client scores a candidate board on the JS copy and the fit measures the same board on the R one, so drift is invisible until only the fit disagrees. What the widening does NOT settle is pooling, at a fixed density 0.22 rhombille's `clueShare5plus` runs **4.4× the 4.8.8's and 15× the hex's**, so routing a tiling into the rotation adds a SHAPE confound on top of the digit study's existing density one, and the pairwise `decorrelation_mission` takes exactly two features and cannot see a third axis. Decide that before a tiling date can feed the digit fit.

**A tiling board round-trips through the canonical path (2026-07-23).** `serializeBoard` carried `wallEdges` and nothing else topological, so a tiling canonical came back RECTANGULAR, the Phase 1 save-resume bug moved onto the path where the board IS the canonical, so every client would have recomputed both number layers against the 8-neighborhood and nobody would have held a correct copy to disagree with. The payload now carries `cellNeighbors` (post-wall-severing, so it IS the certified adjacency), `cellPos`, the `tiling` descriptor (`{type, M, N, wUnits, hUnits}`, M and N are load-bearing, `applyWallsTiling` rebuilds the wireframe through them), `tilingWalls`, and `firstClick`; `deserializeBoard` stamps them through `defineCellNeighbors` BEFORE anything reads the board, exactly as `tryResumeGame` does. A rectangular payload emits NONE of these and is byte-identical to before.
- **Stored, not rebuilt.** `buildTiling` is pure and reproduces `cellPos` / `adj` / the wall wireframe exactly (measured to 9 decimal places), for ~93 bytes against ~4 KB. It is stored anyway: re-deriving is precisely what the canonical architecture exists to prevent ("same seed + different code = different board" is its founding divergence source), `cellNeighbors` is the adjacency the CERTIFICATE was issued against so it must be frozen bytes rather than a function call whose definition can move, and the game save already stores `cellNeighbors` verbatim for the same reason. Measured payload: **6.1 KB (4.8.8) / 6.4 KB (hex)** with walls + compass, against ~1-3 KB for a rectangle, paid only on tiling days (the four Laves tilings were not measured; their per-cell adjacency lists are longer, so expect more). One thing the payload deliberately does NOT carry is the compass RAY ANCHOR: `serializeBoard` emits `cellPos` as `{cx, cy, shape}`, so cairo's and deltoidal's `ax`/`ay` are dropped. Harmless as the code stands, because the ray is computed once at generation and stamped per cell as `compassRay`, which `compassRayCells` reads back, but it means anything that ever RECOMPUTES a ray on a deserialized board would silently use the drawn center and get the short, plausible ray the anchor exists to avoid. Recompute the ray only from a board built by `buildTiling`, or add the anchor to `cellPos` and to the rules whitelist first.
- **`compassRay` was missing from `CELL_FIELDS`** and is now in it. On an explicit topology a compass is a geometry question the neighbor graph cannot answer, so the ray is computed once at generation and STAMPED, and both `recomputeDisplayedMines` and `buildStaticGimmickConstraints` read it back through `compassRayCells`, which returns `[]` for a cell without one. Dropping it therefore did not fail loudly: every compass number on a restored board would quietly read 0 and the certifier would prove from a premise the board never displayed.
- **`firstClick` is stored because the container center is not reliably the opener.** Daily/weekly certify from the board's CENTER, and on a rectangle that is the container center, which is why it was never stored. A tiling container is an arbitrary exact factorization, so whether its middle slot lands on the tiling's center cell is arithmetic luck: 4.8.8 ALWAYS diverges (cell count is `2MN-M-N+1`, unrelated to the M×N lattice, 6×7 opens at 17, container center 40), while hex diverges on even M and COINCIDES on odd M (9×7 and 11×11 both agree). The coincidences are the trap, a derivation that happens to agree on the first tiling shipped looks correct until the dimensions change. `deserializeBoard` now returns the opener (stored value, else container center) as the ONE definition; the nightly sweep's two hand-copied `floor(rows/2), floor(cols/2)` derivations both read it.
- **A container the rules would reject fails at serialize.** `rows`/`cols` are validated `[5, 30]` in both canonical rule blocks, so a PRIME cell count, which forces `1 × total`, cannot be stored. A 4.8.8 at M=8,N=8 is 113 cells, prime; without a guard the only symptom is a write that silently fails and a canonical that never appears. `containerIsStorable` (tilingGeometry.js) is the predicate, `serializeBoard` throws on it, and a test reads `firebase-rules.json` and asserts the mirrored `CANONICAL_MIN_DIM`/`MAX_DIM` still match the rules.
- **Rules and signature.** `tiling` / `cellNeighbors` / `cellPos` / `tilingWalls` / `firstClick` are whitelisted in BOTH the `dailyBoard` and `weeklyBoard` blocks, those end with `"$other": {".validate": false}`, so an un-whitelisted field makes the WHOLE write fail validation and drop silently (the 866683d class). The signature needs no registration: `canonicalStringify` recurses generically over nested arrays and objects, so the new fields are signed automatically (verified end to end, a tiling payload signs, verifies, and a one-entry `cellNeighbors` tamper is detected). Pinned in `test/tilingCanonicalRoundTrip.test.mjs`, break-tested against the un-fixed serializer; verified a no-op on production data by running the sweep over every shipped canonical on this branch and on `main` (identical 15 / 0 / 49 failure counts, all pre-existing and all on PAST dates the nightly sweep never checks).

**Mine placement CONSTRUCTS above 22% density on a tiling too (2026-07-27), and the "4.8.8 density ceiling" was that generator's absence rather than the lattice.** `generateBoard` has routed `density > 0.22 || hasGimmicks` to `generateConstructive`, place mines one at a time keeping the board solvable, with backtracking, since long before Coastline, while `generateTilingBoard` was pure REJECTION SAMPLING at every density. So the two shapes were never running the same algorithm, and the measured tiling ceiling was an artifact of that: per-layout certification on a 128-cell 4.8.8 falls to ~0.7% at density 0.22 and to **zero in 363,000 layouts at 0.30**, so sampling there was not slow, it was asymptotically dead. The proof it was the algorithm and not the geometry: a 121-cell RECTANGLE certifies only **0.185%** of random layouts at 0.30 and would fail just as surely by sampling, it ships because it stopped sampling. `generateConstructive` now takes an optional `topo` (`{neighborCache, excluded, makeBoard}`) and `generateTilingBoard` passes it above `CONSTRUCTIVE_DENSITY_THRESHOLD`. Measured before → after at default `maxAttempts`, 20 seeds: 4.8.8 128 cells at 0.28 and 0.30 went **0/20 → 20/20** (11 and 16 ms), 4.8.8 72 cells at 0.30 went 1/20 → 20/20, hex 121 cells at 0.30 went 6/20 → 20/20, and with `sonar+walls` every one of those is 20/20 (352 ms worst). It is also FASTER in the marginal band, because the old path burned 600 attempts to fail. **Both Archimedean tilings now generate across the whole 14-30% daily band**, so density is no longer a constraint on routing one of them into the rotation. The four Laves tilings (2026-07-27) invert the picture and are the reason `CONSTRUCTIVE_DENSITY_THRESHOLD` deserves a second look before any of them is routed: they need the constructive path MORE, not less, so it is BELOW the 0.22 threshold that they are weak. Measured over 12 seeds on the sampling side, floret certifies 7/12 at density 0.208, deltoidal 8/12 at 0.181 and rhombille **0/12** at 0.211, while the same boards go 30/30 constructively. The threshold was left where it is because lowering it would change every rectangular board's generator too; the practice boards are sized to sit above it instead.
- **Three things were rectangular in `generateConstructive`, not one.** The incremental adjacency helpers and `isBoardSolvable` already read a neighbor CACHE, and the `(row, col)` loops are pure container sweeps, but (1) the cache was synthesized from a bare `{_wallEdges}` stand-in, which cannot express an explicit edge list; (2) the opener's safe zone was `|r-er| <= 1 && |c-ec| <= 1`, the 3×3 block, which on a tiling is a set of cells mostly NOT adjacent to the opener (a tiling passes the opener plus its true GRAPH neighbors); and (3) board construction had to stamp `_cellNeighbors` / `_cellPos` / `_tiling`. The 3×3 test appears TWICE (initial candidate build and the rebuild after exhaustion) and missing the second would have silently let mines into the safe zone only on long searches.
- **Rectangles are byte-identical and that is load-bearing**, since `generateConstructive` is on the path of every challenge / timed / daily / weekly board. The candidate list is built ROW-MAJOR and the Fisher-Yates draws one `rng()` per candidate, so merely reordering the scan would change every board. Verified by a 420-board branch-vs-`main` differential across 5 sizes × 7 densities × both gimmick states (420/420 identical, rolling hash equal), and pinned forward by golden fingerprints in `test/tilingConstructive.test.mjs` so a future RNG-order change fails loudly.
- **The MODIFIER LOAD-BEARING filter now applies to tilings too (2026-07-27).** Every rectangular generation path requires each non-exempt modifier to contribute at least one deduction the player needs; `generateTilingBoard` never asked, so a tiling daily would have shipped modifiers that do nothing. Measured over 40 seeds each with the filter off: **sonar decorative on 45-60% of boards, compass on 40-88%**, liar 15-60%, mirror 5-20%. With the gate on, decorative boards drop to **0-5%** with **no loss in success rate** (40/40 throughout) and a median cost of 0-11 ms; the residual few are the budget expiring, which is the designed escape hatch. **`TILING_LOAD_BEARING_BUDGET` (25) mirrors the daily path's `LOAD_BEARING_BUDGET` exactly**, including its reason, past the budget the requirement drops so generation cannot spin forever, and a certified-but-decorative board is still kept as `best`, so this never trades the no-guess contract for the modifier-means-something one. `loadBearingBudget: 0` disables it (used by the tests' control arm).
  - **The topology needed no work, which is worth knowing rather than assuming.** `findDecorativeGimmicks` threads its cache into `isBoardSolvable`, and `buildNeighborCache` reads `board._cellNeighbors` when present, so a tiling board's own adjacency reaches the filter whether or not a cache is passed (verified: the verdicts agree either way). It is passed anyway, to avoid rebuilding it on each strip-test.
  - **Of the 9 `TILING_SAFE_GIMMICKS`, 5 are testable**, `sonar`, `compass`, `liar`, `mirror`, `wormhole` (the last daily-safe holdout, ported 2026-08-01: pair separation is GRAPH distance >= 3 on an explicit topology, container Manhattan meant nothing on a lattice, and distance >= 3 guarantees disjoint endpoint neighborhoods, so the certifier's union constraint always emits instead of being overlap-skipped; the rectangular Manhattan branch is preserved VERBATIM because pair placement consumes the shared RNG stream, pinned by golden fingerprints + a 1440-board branch-vs-main differential in `test/wormholeTopology.test.mjs`, and the sum ceiling that tokens must cover, 20 on rhombille, two valence-10 cells, is pinned to the geometry in `test/wormholeSumTokens.test.mjs`). Mystery and worm are exempt because they only remove or delay information; locked and walls are structural. `TESTABLE_GIMMICK_TYPES` is now EXPORTED from boardSolver.js so the generator can skip the filter when nothing on the board is testable: it opens with a baseline solve, so calling it on a walls/locked/mystery/worm board would double that attempt's solve count to be told what the exemption list already says.
- **The threshold is density-only, deliberately unlike `generateBoard`'s `|| hasGimmicks`.** A tiling applies gimmicks AFTER the base board is placed and re-certifies, and measured at or below 0.22 a modifier board already succeeds 19-20/20, so adding the clause would make low-density modifier boards slower to fix a failure that does not occur. Revisit if a modifier set turns up that fails under the threshold.
- **`forceConstructive` (2026-08-03) is the per-call opt-in below the threshold**, added for the banded daily config tables: a sub-0.22 Laves config that must generate on ARBITRARY date seeds cannot ride rejection sampling's per-seed miss rate (floret 7/12 at 0.208, deltoidal 8/12 at 0.181, fine for the Par Lab's seed-validated frozen specs, not for a rotation), so its `TILING_BAND_CONFIGS` entry sets `constructive: true` and `generateTilingBoard` routes it. Default false, every caller not passing it is byte-identical, so `?coastline=` practice boards and the lab's frozen specs are untouched. This is the narrow answer to the "revisit `CONSTRUCTIVE_DENSITY_THRESHOLD` before routing a Laves lattice" note above: the shared constant stays where it is (moving it would change rectangles too), and the routing decision lives per proven config instead.

**The DAILY SHAPE ROTATION is LIVE from 2026-08-04 (`TILING_ROTATION_START` in `src/logic/shapeRotation.js`), flipped by the v1.10 release.** It shipped DARK on 2026-08-02 behind a null constant and stayed there for two weeks; the flip carried the `CURRENT_VERSION` bump, the What's New entry, and the Daily-card/help copy in one commit, which is the coupling the constant's own comment demanded. **The constant may never move BACKWARD**: every date behind it already has a rectangular canonical in a write-once node, so a client falling back to local generation on one of those dates would build a lattice the canonical is not. Pinned as the first test in `test/shapeRotation.test.mjs` (start date set, everything before it rectangular forever, the first live month drawing both). The pieces:
- **The draw** (`resolveDailyShape`): 50% rectangle / 50% uniform over `TILING_TYPES` (his ruling, settled), seeded `${date}:shape`, deterministic from the date string alone, no target file, so every client, the precompute, and the sweep agree without fetching anything. Non-date strings (practice `?seed=` customs) never draw. A 7th tiling joins the draw the day it enters `TILING_TYPES`.
- **Tiling days are SINGLE-CANDIDATE**, no 10-way mission-seed contest, because the client fallback must replay selection deterministically and ten rhombille generations is minutes of phone CPU. The day's mission comes from `selectTilingMission` (experimentDesign.js, the single-source rule): a weight-proportional date-seeded draw (`:shapeMission` namespace) over the GIMMICK-BEARING slots only, the rectangular lottery's `count × weight` collapses to its weight term when no candidate features exist to score. Observational targets are excluded (nothing to force), and **decorrelation missions never run on tiling days** (they SELECT across a candidate pool a tiling day does not have; their cadence anchor only advances on a night that ships one, so ~half of decorrelation-due nights will land on rectangle days post-flip, accepted, and their live targets are digit shares the fit reads from rectangular rows anyway). No qualifying slot (observational primary + empty coverage) → plain dateString seed + natural lottery under the primary's banner, mirroring the rectangular plain-dateString fallback. The winning slot feeds `candidateSeed`, so tiling seeds keep the `${date}:trialN` form.
- **One builder, three consumers.** `buildTilingDailyBoard(date, type, spec)` (config = the BANDED per-date draw from `tilingBandConfigs.js` since 2026-08-03, see the Par Bands tiling bullet; the rhombille 72-cell cost ceiling survives as a table-wide pin, and `COASTLINE_BOARDS` reverts to practice-boards-only) runs the mission draw, the `getDailyGimmick` roll filtered to `TILING_SAFE_GIMMICKS`, and `generateTilingBoard` over `tilingConfigAttempts` (drawn entry, then the shape's designated in-band fallback entry). Consumed by `scripts/daily-board-pipeline.mjs` (`selectBestCandidate`'s tiling branch, which logs the type + generation ms), gameActions' daily local-gen fallback, and parResolve's canonical-miss branch, so precompute and fallback clients write the same canonical byte for byte (`DIFFERENTIAL` test). A null (every config attempt exhausted) is DETERMINISTIC, same seed, same outcome everywhere, so every path falls back to the rectangular contest in agreement.
- **The lockstep guard**: `TILING_SAFE_GIMMICKS ⊇ DAILY_SAFE_GIMMICKS` is pinned in `test/shapeRotation.test.mjs` (every mission gimmick must have a tiling story, true since the 2026-08-01 wormhole port made the sets equal), and the builder's runtime filter drops any future escapee deterministically with a console warning while the guard test fails loudly in CI.
- **Openers, everywhere.** The tiling candidate's `firstClick` rides `buildCanonicalPayload` into the payload; `candidateOpener` (pipeline) is the ONE pre-serialization opener rule, and the regenerate tool's belt-and-braces certification report now uses it, its container-center formula would have REFUSED every tiling board as "not gated-certified" (the #195 class). `liveBoardOpener` (winLossHandler, bomb pricing + win receipt) covers the no-canonical tiling case (local-gen fallback, `?dailyShape=` practice) by re-deriving `buildTiling(...).centerIndex` from the live board's own `_tiling`, which is exact integer lattice arithmetic and reproduces the generation anchor.
- **What a tiling day deliberately does NOT ship**: a crux teaser (`materializeCrux` returns null on an explicit topology, the whole materialization is a rectangular crop, and the teaser page renders a plain mini-grid; `extractCrux` itself still works, so the win receipt keeps its confession) and a board heatmap (`rollup-board-heatmap.mjs` skips tiling canonicals, the exhibit draws a rows×cols rectangle, and on a tiling those are container storage, so every cell would land in the wrong place; no map beats a wrong map). Both degrade exactly as their missing-node cases always have.
- **The Daily card names the day's shape on a lattice day**, leading its CENTER line (`Honeycomb today. <Greg's note>`) and absent on a Classic one, since naming "Classic" on half the days is noise. It reads the shape off the CANONICAL board, never by re-running the draw, a forced or fallback board can differ from the draw and the card must describe what the player will actually open (the fieldnote-drift rule). It rode the PAR CORNER for one iteration and that was measured wrong: "Paving Stones · Par 240s" overlaps the streak corner on a 174px card at every viewport, because all four corners are already spoken for. Pinned by `tests/e2e/dailyCardShape.spec.mjs`, which injects the longest real content at three viewports and was break-tested against the corner version.
- **Test-env override `?dailyShape=<tiling|rect>`**, the door to a CHOSEN shape (playtest or e2e), and the only way to force a rectangle on a date that drew a lattice. It was the only way to see a tiling daily at all while the rotation was dark, and survives the flip unchanged. Derivation-site gated the same way as `?level=` AND practice-lane-only (gameActions consults it only when `state.isDailyPractice`; the daily deep link stamps practice whenever the override is present): `/test/` shares localStorage with production, so an overridden board recording as the real date would block that day's real play. It skips resume (a stale save would ignore the requested shape) and never touches the live lane, so it cannot leak: production never sets it, and even a test build's Daily-card tap plays the normal live daily. `tests/e2e/tilingDaily.spec.mjs` is the journey (hex board mounts through the tiling renderer, one distinct clip-path, "Nothing records" toast, start-here label on the lattice, certified opener reveals on click) plus the dark-default control; smoke carries the boot entry.
- **The sweep bites on tiling payloads**, pinned in `test/verifyCanonicals.test.mjs`: a pipeline-built hex canonical + meta verify clean end to end, and the tamper classes hard-fail (lying displayed number, forged `firstClick` onto a mine, asymmetric `cellNeighbors` refused at deserialize, and a META SHAPE LIE, `tilingType: 'rhombille'` on a hex board is STRUCTURAL, never excused as solver drift, because `modelFor` prices par off that key).

- **The launch boards are deliberate, and one of them is forced** (his call, 2026-08-04). The rotation start was moved back a day, onto the release day itself, once its board was confirmed to have zero score rows: **2026-08-04's own draw is a Honeycomb**, so the launch day needed no forcing at all and its canonical is exactly what an offline fallback client would build. The following week's weekly (2026-08-10) IS forced, to **Octagons**, through the regenerate tools' new `--shape=<token>` flag. Forcing creates no divergence: every client reads `dailyBoard/{date}` / `weeklyBoard/{weekStart}` VERBATIM through `gateCanonicalTrust`, so whatever is written IS the day's board. The only residual is the standing one, a client that cannot reach Firebase at all falls back to local generation and would build the DRAWN shape instead, the same class of drift the canonical architecture absorbs for everyone online. The rest of the precomputed horizon was regenerated so the ordinary 50/50 draw takes over from there. **An unrecognized `--shape=` token is a HARD failure in both scripts**, deliberately: `buildTiling` falls through to the 4.8.8 for an unknown type, so a typo would otherwise produce a plausible board on the wrong lattice with no error anywhere.
- **A tiling WEEKLY has no rotation, it has a builder.** Which shape a week lands on is a call, not a draw, so there is no `resolveWeeklyShape`; `buildTilingWeeklyBoard(weekStart, type)` (shapeRotation.js, beside its daily sibling) is the ONE way one gets built, written before a second copy could exist rather than after two drifted. It keeps the weekly's identity (the `getWeeklyGimmicks` 2-or-3 stack, filtered to `TILING_SAFE_GIMMICKS`) and changes only the config source: `tilingWeeklyConfigAttempts` draws from the same generation-proven `TILING_BAND_CONFIGS` tables against the **WEEKLY** par band `[60, 360]s` and `drawWeeklyTargetPar`, in its own `:weeklyShapeConfig` namespace, distinct for the reason parBand.js gives about its own streams, since a weekStart IS a Monday date string and a shared stream would tie every Monday's weekly to that day's daily. Reach is the honest limit: the tables were calibrated to the DAILY band and top out ~218s, so a weekly target above that ships the table's dearest in-band entry (the same "the band is a constraint, not a coverage promise" reading). Pinned in `test/tilingWeekly.test.mjs`.
- **`regenerate-weekly-board.mjs` had its own private, UNBANDED weekly contest**, a leftover of the mirror-pair drift class the precompute closed, so the tool built to REPAIR a canonical would have produced a board the nightly pipeline never would. It now calls `selectWeeklyRngSeed` like the precompute does. It still does NOT write `dailyMeta/{weekStart}_weekly_first` (it deletes the stale one and leaves the client upsert to refill it); harmless on a tiling week, where every client is necessarily on a post-flip build, but it is a real asymmetry with the precompute.

With the rotation live, tiling dailies start producing fit rows immediately. The rest of the instrument is in place: the canonical path round-trips, features and par are computed on every `?coastline=` board (`state.coastlineFeatures` / `state.coastlinePar`, nothing submits them, a coastline run is `isLevelPractice`), the feature vector carries its own shape, and the `features` object in both the `dailyMeta` and `timed` rules blocks validates only `hasChildren(['rows','cols','totalMines'])` with **no nested `$other: false`**, so new feature keys need no rules deploy. A tiling daily records stats / streak / leaderboard / submission exactly like any daily, the win path keys on `gameMode` and the practice flags, never on the board's shape (audited 2026-08-02), so tiling scores feed the per-shape par deviations from the first rotation day. Two consequences of the flip worth watching: the lab-seeded deviations take live data with NO 20-row wait (the doubled-width lab priors do the regularizing), and a decorrelation-due night that lands on a tiling day skips its mission, so if the digit bands stall post-flip that cadence interaction is the first suspect.

**The PAR LAB (`?parlab=1`, test-env-gated, 2026-08-02) is the designed instrument for the per-shape par PRIORS.** **The battery is 82 boards after the board-66 TRIM (his call 2026-08-03: "if stuff is stabilizing, I'd rather test other regions or just have less work to do")**, the audit found cairo's slope tight (M sd 0.08, L sd 0.19: steep-and-severe confirmed), floret's inverted slope real (L calm), and his anchors pinned at 0.55-0.57, so the cuts were the 3 cairo L replicates, floret's L boards, 2 of 4 remaining anchors, and 14 of 20 remaining modifier singles, including EVERY worm single, because the challenge-250 side-only crawl ruling changes the mechanic they would measure (worm timing waits for the new crawl; `test/parLab.test.mjs` asserts worm count 0 so a reappearance fails loudly). What survived: deltoidal's wild SMALL cell attackers (S1r, S2, sd 1.26, the battery's widest open question; RESOLVED at completion as REAL, dense small deltoidal ran 2.4-5.6× par, the battery's worst boards), the 0.28-density corners on the steep lattices, the sonar valence arc top (deltoidal), one compass block per remaining direction family (hex 60°, floret 30°), wormhole's sum-20 ceiling (rhombille), and liar/locked neutrality spot-checks (floret/rhombille, liar sits on floret rather than deltoidal so the tail stays interleaved). **THE WORM ADDENDUM (his call at 82/82 completion, 2026-08-03) makes the battery 86**: the four worm singles return, hex, cairo, rhombille, floret, spanning the crawl-graph extremes, now that the mechanics they measure have shipped (the side-only crawl #217, set pace #218); the mechanism test's asserted worm absence flipped to an asserted 4. Shape-neutral pooled gimmick priors move to live tiling dailies, whose varying cell counts identify those deviations, unlike size/density, which only the lab can supply. Live daily data identifies the `PAR_MODEL_SHAPES` deviations slowly (one tiling daily per ~2 days post-flip, split six ways) and PARTIALLY: the rotation ships FIXED per-shape configs, so live rows carry a constant cellCount/totalMines within a shape and those deviation columns are exactly collinear with the shape's intercept deviation, no amount of daily data separates them. The lab is a 105-board battery (21 chunks of 5, `src/logic/parLab.js`) targeting exactly that gap, played on `/test/`, recorded locally AND auto-synced to the world-readable `parLab/` node so the analysis fetches results with no manual handoff (his ruling 2026-08-02: "grabbable without me giving you them"). The `parLab/` node's consumer is the offline prior fit alone, **`scripts/fit-parlab-priors.qmd` (2026-08-03, the battery complete at 86/86)**, which fits log(pure time / parity par) with the anchors identifying session pace and a shared practice term, and freezes its 32 deviation posteriors in `scripts/data/parlab-prior-centers.json`; the NIGHTLY refit never reads `parLab/` itself, but it DOES read that frozen JSON (the 2026-08-03 seeding rulings, see the per-shape par bullet under Greg-par Model). The fit's own conventions: pure time = timeSec − coalesce(penaltySec, 0) (penaltySec is only written when > 0, so absence means pre-fix wall clock OR post-fix zero strikes, both already pure); the board-15 probe falls to its tombstone; warm-ups are excluded but their learning slopes are checked (floret had not flattened → the practice term); worm rows fit on realized dose; strikes are treated as symptom (his ruling, the S4 strike-term sensitivity that pulls deltoidal 5.14 → 4.03 is documented in the qmd, not adopted).
- **Composition, each block earning its place**: 18 WARM-UPS (THREE per tiling at its daily config, in consecutive SAME-SHAPE runs, easiest lattice first, his call: acclimation builds on repetition, and three points per shape give the fit a learning slope to CHECK rather than assume; `warmup: true`, excluded from estimates), 54 PLAIN size×density grid boards (originally 3 sizes × 3 densities per tiling, the collinearity-breaking block only the lab can supply; REDISTRIBUTED at the designed chunk-9/10 checkpoint, his call 2026-08-03 at 55/105: hex/4.8.8/rhombille came back pinned at log-spread ×/1.4 or tighter with gentle-or-flat size slopes, so their 9 unplayed grid slots moved to the noisy three, cairo +3 LARGE-corner replicates (its slope 0.56→1.41→2.67 was the open question), floret and deltoidal +3 mid-density replicates each at every size (variance and residual learning dominate there); `GRID_PLAN` in parLab.js carries the per-shape orders, replicate ids are `p-<shape>-<cell>r` drawing fresh layouts, played ids frozen verbatim), 9 SQUARE ANCHORS interleaved ~every 9 boards (known-model boards that measure his day-to-day pace drift so a slow afternoon reads as a slow afternoon, not six slow lattices), 24 MODIFIER SINGLES allocated by MECHANISM (`MODIFIER_PLAN`): the region-geometry gimmicks, sonar (depth-2 ball scales with valence), compass (spans all three direction families), wormhole (sum ceiling 20 on rhombille), worm (crawls the graph), get 3-4 boards on the lattices where their mechanism diverges most; the mechanically shape-neutral five (mystery/liar/mirror/locked/walls) get 2 each toward a POOLED tilings-vs-square prior; per-shape × per-gimmick cells (54 of them) are unpowerable at this scale and deliberately not attempted. Post-warm-up ordering interleaves shapes (never three consecutive alike, test-pinned) so practice effects spread.
- **The chunk contract ("fill gaps as we go")**: boards have stable `id`s and progress is a per-id result log, so any UNPLAYED spec can be redesigned freely, swap, reorder, retune, without corrupting progress. Played ids are frozen history. After any chunk, export from the HUD, look at the posteriors, revise the remaining chunks in `parLab.js`.
- **Recording semantics**: rides the coastline-practice lane (`isLevelPractice` + `state.coastlinePractice` + `state.parLabSpec`, frozen certified boards, marked opener, nothing records to real progression). **Lab mines are DAILY-STYLE STRIKES** (his ruling 2026-08-02: "we want the mines to behave how they do in the daily since we're trying to parameterize a daily par model"), the `mineIsStrike()` predicate in gameActions routes both mine sites (revealCell + the chord split) through `handleDailyBombHit` for daily/weekly/parLab, so a lab mine is priced (info-value against `state.coastlineFeatures` + ramped base), marked, logged to the daily per-attempt event log, and play continues; a loss-on-mine lab would measure a more cautious solve than the model's own response frame. Rows carry timeSec in the **DAILY TIME CONVENTION** (his report 2026-08-02 "mine hits aren't counting against the time" fixed the first cut's pure-wall-clock recording): `state.preciseTime`, the penalty-INCLUSIVE final time stopTimer commits, the same number a daily submits, plus `penaltySec` (the folded total), so the offline fit recovers pure_time = timeSec − penaltySec, exactly the refit's daily recipe; rows recorded before the fix lack `penaltySec` and carry pure integer wall clock (all warm-ups, the field's presence is the discriminator). Also on the row: bombHits + bombHitEvents (same probe filter as the daily fit), features (`state.coastlineFeatures`, so `tilingType` rides along), par, wormEvents, and `seq` (the session-effect axis). Every played board completes (losses are unreachable from mines; the loss/retry machinery survives as a defensive path); a replay of a recorded layout is refused by the (id, attempt) guard, and Skip is the way out of a board, including one abandoned mid-way, whose SAME seed would otherwise re-issue half-seen. **A contaminated run is VOIDED via `?parlab=1&parlabRedo=<boardNumber|id>`** (`redoParLabBoard`): resolving rows flip to `invalid` locally, an `invalid` TOMBSTONE row syncs (the server originals are append-only, the analysis drops any (uid, id, attempt) carrying an invalid row), and the board re-issues at the next attempt = a FRESH layout. The HUD's copy button (`parlab-v1` JSON) stays as the offline fallback. E2e-pinned: the strike choreography, the full-solve win row (timeSec ≥ penaltySec ≥ 3 with one strike), and the redo round trip.
- **Sync (`src/firebase/parLabSync.js` → `parLab/{pushId}`)**: localStorage is the source of truth AND the outbox, rows gain an `fbKey` once pushed, unsynced rows flush opportunistically on every lab entry and result, and the HUD line shows `synced N/M`. The writer is **deliberately NOT `isTestEnvironment()`-gated**, the one sanctioned exception, because the lab only exists in test builds and these rows ARE the dataset (its own node, its own rules, no consumer inside the game); every other test-session write stays gated. It never runs without an auth session (a fresh e2e context never mints one, so e2e cannot write), and duplicate pushes after an interrupted mark-synced are deduped on read via (uid, id, attempt). The `parLab` rules block (cloned from `timed`: world-read, append-only, uid-owned rows, server-`timestamp`, `$other: false`) ships in `firebase-rules.json` and deploys when the branch merges to main, until then pushes are cleanly rejected and rows queue locally, flushing on a later visit. `test/parLab.test.mjs` pins the row-fields ↔ whitelist lockstep in both directions (the 866683d silent-drop class). Analysis reads filter to his uid.
- **Timing purity**: lab boards run on a deliberately EMPTY power-up inventory (the daily/timed pattern in gameActions), both `saveModePowerUps` sites are parLab-guarded so those zeros can never overwrite the real challenge inventory, and the 30% lifeline-on-win earn gained the `!isLevelPractice` guard it always should have had (the one earn site predating the practice contract).
- **Every spec is generation-PROVEN offline**: `scripts/validate-parlab-battery.mjs` builds all 105 through the same `buildParLabBoard` the client uses (deterministic, passing once means passing forever) and hard-fails on null/uncertified/over-budget; re-run it on any battery revision. Full battery generates in ~4s (worst single board ~1.1s). One design finding baked in: **rhombille's density floor is real**, sparse no-guess rhombille boards are effectively unfindable (0/12 at 0.211, reproduced at 0.18), so its density triple sits at [0.23, 0.255, 0.28], the range production rhombille actually lives in; `p-deltoidal-L0` carries a seed salt (`SEED_SALTS`) found by the validator's seed search.
- Tests: `test/parLab.test.mjs` (design pins incl. same-shape warm-up runs + the mechanism allocation + the rules-whitelist lockstep, recording contract, fast-shape builder determinism), `tests/e2e/parLab.spec.mjs` (board 1 mounts, skip records + advances, progress survives reload, no HUD outside `?parlab=`).

## Board Solver Architecture

Two-layer solver guarantees no 50/50 guesses:

1. **Board solver** (`boardSolver.js`): Simulates full game playthrough from first click. Wall-aware neighbor lists. Gimmick handling via `getPlayerVisibleCount`:
   - **Mystery / sonar / compass / wormhole:** return UNKNOWN (no direct per-cell constraint; sonar/compass/wormhole contribute separate gimmick-range constraints in Pass A/B/C, reveal-gated on `board._gatedCert` boards, see the No-Guess Contract section). Liar-stacked gimmick cells contribute NO range constraint (their displayed value already includes the lie).
   - **Liar:** return UNKNOWN. Liar's displayed value is true count ±1, a disjunctive constraint, not a single value. `buildLiarConstraints` emits `{allowedMines: [X-1, X+1]}` for Pass C tank solver.
   - **Mirror:** return `cell.adjacentMines` (the true count). The displayed value shows the partner's count for visual deception; a smart player who recognizes the pair can mentally un-swap. Using `displayedMines` would create false constraints that made post-gimmick boards spuriously unsolvable.
   - Returns `{ solvable, remainingUnknowns, totalClicks, techniqueLevel }` where `techniqueLevel` is 0 (Pass A), 1 (Pass B subset), 2 (Pass C tank/gauss), or 3 (required disjunctive liar reasoning).

2. **Constraint solver** (`constraintSolver.js`): Union-find partitions independent constraint groups. Constraints use `allowedMines: number[]`, single-element for exact, multi-element for disjunctive (liar), plus an optional `origin` (the source cell index) for provenance. Tank solver (bitmask brute-force with disjunctive check) for <=20 unknowns; Gaussian elimination for larger groups (exact constraints only, disjunctive filtered out before gauss). Gauss is sound-or-skip (reworked 2026-06-10): float row-reduction with partial pivoting, then a row yields deductions ONLY if every coefficient and the RHS snap to integers within 1e-6, extracted via the binary-variable bound rule (rhs == min/max achievable). The old extraction `Math.round`ed coefficients before pattern-matching, 0/1 systems legitimately produce 0.5 entries under elimination, and on unsatisfiable systems (wrong player flag) it fabricated "provable mine" verdicts (`test/gaussSoundness.test.mjs` pins both the fabrication case and a witness-consistency sweep). Gauss remains INCOMPLETE by design, a missed deduction is honest, a false one never is. `solveConstraints` returns, besides the flat `mines`/`safe` sets: `groups` (per-component `{unknowns, hasDisjunctive, origins}`), `cellGroup` (cell → group index for every solved cell), and `contradiction` (a tank component with zero satisfying assignments, on live player state this means a provably wrong flag).

**Deduction substrate (2026-06-10)**, the engine-as-witness layer the receipt surfaces consume:
- **Trace**: `isBoardSolvable(..., { trace: true })` returns `result.trace = [{cell, tier, sources}]`, one entry per deduced reveal, where `sources` are the origin cells of the constraints that PROVED it (Pass A: the one constraint; Pass B: the subset pair; Pass C: the whole union-find component, the honest minimal explanation for enumeration). Collection-only: counters, ordering, and behavior are unchanged; generation hot loops don't pass the flag. Invariant: `trace.length + 1 === totalClicks`.
- **Per-group disjunctive attribution**: a Pass C deduction counts as `disjunctiveMoves` (and promotes techniqueLevel to 3) only if ITS union-find component carries a liar constraint. The old version batch-flagged every Pass C deduction of a round whenever any liar constraint existed anywhere, inflating `disjunctiveMoves` in dailyMeta on liar boards and over-promoting techniqueLevel. Consequence: on liar boards, `advancedLogicMoves` (the model's searchMoves) can be higher and `disjunctiveMoves` lower than under the old counting, the honest direction; historical dailyMeta rows written by old clients carry the old counting.
- **`findDeducibleFrontier(board, {respectFlags})`**: everything provable from the live state, full safe + mine frontier, each with `{tier, sources}`, plus `contradiction`. `respectFlags: false` runs flags-blind: player flags are CLAIMS, not facts, and one wrong flag can poison the constraint system into certifying a mine "provably safe", every player-facing verdict must use the flags-blind run. `findNextSafeMove` is now a thin first-safe-cell wrapper over it.
- **`detectWrongFlags(board)`**: dual-solve diff, a player flag is provably wrong when the flags-blind run proves that cell safe; the flags-respecting run's `contradiction` additionally says "some flag is wrong" without localizing it.
- **Click timeline**: `state.clickTimeline = [{t, r, c, a}]` (a = r/f/u/c) records every player action via `recordPlayerAction` in gameState.js, reset per game, persisted with the auto-save, capped at 2000. The ground truth for player-grounded receipt claims (never narrate the solver's order as the player's).

**Player-facing proof surfaces (2026-06-10)** built on the substrate:
- **The Receipt** (`src/ui/receiptRenderer.js` + winLossHandler): post-loss, the full flags-blind frontier is painted (`.frontier-safe` outlines) with an honest verdict count, and tapping any cell in the explore view answers "was this knowable?" with a pulse of its proving region. Daily/weekly bomb hits get a one-line strike verdict (provable mine / safe cells existed / genuinely at the frontier) computed from the pre-strike state. Daily/weekly wins get a one-line board's-confession receipt: the crux (first non-trivial trace deduction) + the modifier's CERTIFIED contribution tier from `gradeGimmickContribution` (required / technique / shortcut / decorative, the copy can never overclaim). Suppressed for newcomer dailies (≤3 plays), same gate as the par breakdown.
- **The Lens: REMOVED 2026-07-18** (Christopher: "People can figure it out, or not. I don't want to be losing par data and I'm not completely confident that this feature got the attention it needs"). The 🤔 `#stuck-btn`, `handleLensRequest`, the `#board-coach` line and its `src/ui/boardCoach.js` module, `state.hintEvents` / `recordHintEvent`, and the hint payload on every submit path are all gone. **Two things deliberately SURVIVE the removal and must not be "cleaned up":** (1) the refit's `filter(n_hints == 0)` in `scripts/refit-par-model.R`, no new row can carry `hintEvents`, but historical rows do, and a hinted completion is still a contaminated observation of board difficulty, so dropping the filter would readmit every old hinted play into the fit; (2) the `hintEvents` whitelist in BOTH `firebase-rules.json` row families, a stale cached client mid-game can still submit one, and an un-whitelisted child makes the WHOLE score write fail validation and drop silently (the 866683d class). `test/archivePayload.test.mjs` pins that the payload never emits the field even when handed one.
- **The Certificate stamp (2026-06-12, the front-door PR)**: `state.boardCertificate = { clicks, tier }` built by `certificateFromCheck` in boardSolver.js (null unless the check certified a full clear, a failed solve must never stamp). Stamp sites: daily and weekly use the bestStart cell's OWN full-solve check (the chip's claim is "solvable without guessing from the marked start", so the center check that feeds features/par is the wrong witness; no full-solve anchor → no stamp); challenge stamps the C250 builder's check at newGame (the contract runs from the marked Start-here opener, like daily); timed uses the accepted check from its first-click generation loop; chaos never stamps (its modifiers apply post-loop unverified). Persisted/restored by gamePersistence. Rendered as the ✓ Certified chip riding the existing `#game-info-bar` row (NOT a bar of its own, phone vertical space is the budget, and gimmick-free boards must not pay a row for the chip) with a plain-language modal (`showCertificateModal` in headerRenderer.js: deduction count = clicks - 1, tier in plain words, daily/weekly add the generated-and-recheck line); chaos shows the inverse "No guarantees" chip on `#chaos-modifier-bar`, opening the same modal's exception copy. Companion front-door copy: title headline "No guesses. Ever." (title screen + in-game header) with the proof subline, the guarantee as the first Basics block in help, and the unnumbered tutorial promise step (`id: 'promise'`). On phones (≤480px) the in-game wordmark block (`#app #title-bar`) is display:none, every row above the board is rent, the title screen owns the brand, and the chip carries the contract; About is reachable from Settings (`#btn-about`) as well as the desktop wordmark tap.
- **Reveal Safe power-up** is deduction-first: it reveals the next PROVABLY-safe cell (a worked example) and only falls back to the old random-safe oracle at a genuine frontier. X-Ray/Scan remain oracles but power-ups are structurally empty in daily/weekly/timed, so they never touch instrumented modes.
- **Greg's voice** (`src/logic/gregVoice.js`, pure + node-tested): the meta-layer made legible, with hard rules, Greg never says a number the engine can't prove, the bad days speak too, and each surface renders once per session. Four surfaces: (1) **Field Note** on the daily title card, derived from the CANONICAL BOARD via `fieldNoteFromBoard` (the stamped `missionTarget`/`missionIsPrimary`, plus `missionType`/`missionConfounder` on a decorrelation board, when present; else the board's actual `activeGimmicks`), NEVER by re-deriving the seed's slot against the current experimentTarget.json: boards are pre-generated up to 7 days ahead and the nightly refit reorders the coverage list, so slot re-derivation names the wrong gimmick (2026-06-10 incident: a wormhole board labeled a compass study); (2) **yesterday note** in the leaderboard modal, the closed loop from modelHistory.json diffs, with all four honesty branches first-class (tightened / WIDENED / fit-rejected / nobody-played); (3) **Lab File**, when handicaps.json v2 ships `handicapDetails` ({clean, bomb} per uid, emitted by both refit paths), the win modal's "Your par" becomes an itemization: `Your par 110.5s = Greg 96.0s your pace +8.4s bombs +6.1s`. No decomposition shipped → the plain line renders; the split is never fabricated client-side; (4) **Greg's Journal** (`#journal-modal` via the More sheet; `src/logic/journalFindings.js` + `src/logic/journalProse.js` pure + node-tested, rendered by the lazy-loaded `src/ui/journalView.js` + the shared card builder `src/ui/journalCard.js` + `journalFigure.js` sparkline), the nightly experiment's public notebook, reframed DEPTH-OVER-BREADTH 2026-07-12 (PR C): ONE deep card for the active experiment (the experimentTarget.json target when named, else the latest refit row's target) with Greg's falsifiable hypothesis (`featureHypothesis` in gregVoice, structural claims, never numbers; the italic epigraph HIDES when the composed entry's arc beat already quotes the hunch, `entry.arcSpoken`, so the premise never prints twice in a row), a composed notebook entry, 1-2 FIGURES planned deterministically per card (`planStudyFigures` in journalProse: sd-trend = the uncertainty sparkline, estimate-band = live-era mean line with the ±1 SD band shaded and a zero line, band-strip = the current estimate as one labeled range bar; sd-trend point shapes rotate circle/square/diamond/tick/none, 'none' excluded whenever the series carries retro points since the dimmed dot IS the retro disclosure; captions rotate from digit-free per-type pools; ~1/3 of cards sketch a second, distinct figure, `renderStudyFigure` in journalFigure.js draws the spec), and a dated LAB LOG (`labLog`, consecutive LIVE-ERA row diffs, notable days only: runs landed with the band's ±2% move class / target switched / fit rejected; a quiet day renders only as the latest entry; capped at `MAX_LOG_ENTRIES` 6; "first assignment" phrasing is checked against the FULL history so a returning April study can't read as new); a CONCLUSIONS LEDGER of resting-verdict studies as one-line past-tense findings anchored to their close date (= `lastStudied`), each a `<details>` row expanding to the full card (expansions pre-composed with the screen's one session); one QUEUE line from `coverage_targets` (first named non-active entry, "what I want data on next" framing ONLY, with a separate honest phrasing when the queued file is closed); and the full parameter picture behind one "full ledger" link (`parameterTable`, every NAMED feature in the latest fit's candidates, ranges floored at 0, equal-rounding ends collapse to "about N%"). In-between studies (open/settling/widened, not active) have NO cards, they stay reachable via the table and their `?report=` pages. A study = the per-feature LONGITUDINAL track across all modelHistory rows, deduped to last-row-per-date, NOT contiguous target runs, which are almost all n=1 since targets never repeat within 3 days; verdicts (settling / widened / resting / open / early) come through the shared `classifySdDelta` at the ±15% bar vs the yesterday-note's ±2%, with **resting** = untargeted for `RESTING_MIN_IDLE_DAYS` (14) days AND bottom-half CV rank in the latest row's candidates ordering, the literal mechanism by which the target chooser spends boards elsewhere; never shown over widened/early, never carries a number. **The prose engine (`journalProse.js`, tested in `test/journalProse.test.mjs`)**: every study gets a NARRATIVE STATE, early / active-unresolved / grind (the data keep giving the same answer) / anomaly (widened) / closed-won / closed-lost / resting / reopened (a parked study's point estimate left the ±1 SD band it was parked with, `driftSinceClose`, live-era fits only) / revalidation (fires on experimentTarget's `reason: "revalidation"` stamp, which the R refit's 14-day revalidation targeting now emits, PR D, 2026-07-17), computed from verdict × the feature's bespoke hypothesis ARC (`lean`: cost / zero / faster / size / fork / question; hand-written sided/against/out lines per feature, player mentions only inside quoted hypotheses) × band class (`hi < 1%` = negligible). The **clue-digit studies** (`clueShare2/3/4/5plus`, PR D, see Refit Workflow) render through this same machinery with zero new prose plumbing: they carry `FEATURE_NAMES` (twos / threes / fours / high numbers), `FEATURE_UNITS` ("extra N in ten numbers" (his 2026-08-11 jargon ruling: numbers, not clues; the 5plus label is "fives and up" and zeroClusterCount reads "blank patches"), so the per-unit estimate reads naturally), `FEATURE_HYPOTHESIS` (the arithmetic-load framing, the June null as chapter one), and bespoke `ARCS` (2s / high → zero-or-cost leans, 3s → cost, 4s → size); the moment the refit targets one, it is a full study card. Each state owns 2-3 beat-skeletons assigned by feature hash; phrasing pools rotate deterministically by feature+date (FNV-1a, NO Date.now/Math.random anywhere); a shared per-screen session enforces the HARD RULE that no two visible entries share a skeleton or a closer (and keeps ledger one-liner templates distinct, the zero-band pool has 5 variants because live data parks four zero-band studies at once). Mechanized writing rails (each a test): zero em/en dashes, max ONE hedge word per sentence, digit honesty (every digit sequence in rendered prose must exist in the entry's fact object; counts ≤20 render as words via `countWord`), day counts as "study day N" (never bare "Day N"), no player-behavior claims from a band, no today's-board claims, band bounds spoken as 0% never "nothing" (`NOTHING_AS_NUMBER` guard), and MEASUREMENT VERBS on data sentences, narrowed/widened/moved/held/landed/reads/settled; never body or emotion verbs on numbers ("the model ate", "breathes easier", "wandered") or invented instruments (meters, needles), color lives in Greg's stances, pinned by the `IMPRECISE_VERBS` guard; and COMPLETE SENTENCES only, every sentence has a subject + finite verb or is a true imperative, telegraphic fragments ("Just width.", "Honest ink.") are banned, with exactly two sanctioned notations from Christopher's own spec examples: lab-log datelines ("Jul 12.") and ledger file stamps ("Closed May 30." / "Parked Jun 8."), pinned by the curated finite-verb lexicon in `assertCompleteSentences` (all three rulings Christopher's, 2026-07-12). **Estimate copy is PER TEN UNITS, universally (his ruling 2026-08-11: "I'd prefer if all are on a 10 tile basis")**: `featureScaleOf` in journalFindings is the one rule (scale 10 for every feature except the `clueShare*` digit shares, whose unit is already the composite "one extra N in ten numbers"), `estimateSummary` computes the scaled percents as exp(10 x coef), the model's own compounded prediction (never 10x the per-unit percent), and `estimateUnit`/the facts' `unit`/`unitShort` carry the phrase ("ten sonar cells", "per ten pairs") so every "per {unit}" template stays honest without knowing its scale. `bandClass`'s negligible bar scales with the estimate (exact on the log scale) or presentation alone would flip a study from zero to ambiguous. The full ledger (`parameterTable`) scales identically and each row STATES its basis via `per` ("per ten liar cells"), rendered as a muted line under the feature name, his same-day report that the ledger gave no indication of the basis. **The verb rulings from the same conversation ("scientists don't write that way"; "make sure nouns agree with verbs")**: data are PLURAL (data show, data keep, data agree), the verbs hold, sit, wait and eat are banned on bands/files/notebooks/models, and ~30 voice lines were rewritten plain; the enforcement moved INTO `~/.claude/hooks/writing_rules.py` (journal nouns joined the inanimate-subject table, waits/sides/budges/spends joined the lively verbs, plus two new classes: data-with-singular-verb and the model-ingestion family), so the hook now catches the whole class in every file. **The NIGHTLY AI REWRITE (2026-08-11, his ask: the beat-assembled entries "seem so disjunct... rewritten into a coherent paragraph")**: once per night the refit workflow composes the active study's entry exactly as a client would (`planJournalScreen` over the just-committed modelHistory + experimentTarget), sends the beats plus the fact object to a chat-completions endpoint (`scripts/rewrite-journal-entry.mjs`), validates the draft, and ships the paragraph as DATA in `src/logic/journalRewrite.json` (SW-precached beside modelHistory.json; a precached file must exist at deploy or the SW install 404s) inside the same nightly commit. Clients never run anything model-shaped: `applyJournalRewrite` (journalRewrite.js) swaps the paragraph into the active card (journalView) and the `?report=` page ONLY when the artifact's `sourceHash` equals the hash of the entry THAT CLIENT just composed AND the paragraph re-clears the validator against the client's own facts, so a stale artifact, an older cached bundle, a revalidation-night divergence, or a tampered file all fall back to the beat assembly byte for byte; the journal can never be blocked or made dishonest by a bad model night. **The honesty rails live ONCE, in `src/logic/proseRails.js`** (extracted 2026-08-11 from test/journalProse.test.mjs, which now imports them): the pool guard tests, the rewrite validator (`rewriteViolations`), and the client re-check all call the same predicates, so a test and the validator cannot drift. The validator's own rules on top: digit honesty in BOTH directions (no invented number, no dropped source number), length bounds relative to the source, single plain-text paragraph, no markup; the one rail deliberately NOT applied to model output is the finite-verb lexicon (curated to the pools' closed vocabulary, it would false-reject open-vocabulary prose and silently retire the feature). Entries under `MIN_SOURCE_CHARS` (200) skip the night, short entries read fine as beats. **The model runs ON THE RUNNER: GitHub Models, the planned free provider, was fully retired 2026-07-30** (verified live, the endpoint answers 410), and every hosted successor needs a new vendor/secret or a Copilot seat the account does not hold, so the workflow serves a sha256-pinned open-weight model (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct Q4_K_M, Apache-2.0, the 2026-08-11 bake-off winner; the pin lives once in `.github/actions/journal-llm`, and `verify-journal-llm.yml` re-proves any change to it on a real runner) through llama.cpp's OpenAI-compatible `llama-server` on localhost, weights in the Actions cache beside the R packages; swapping to a hosted provider is the three `JOURNAL_REWRITE_*` env values documented in the script (e.g. Anthropic's OpenAI-compatible endpoint + a repo-secret key), no code change. Exit-code contract: a rejected draft or too-short entry exits 0 (the fallback IS the design); machinery breakage (unhealthy server, auth, unreadable inputs) exits 1 behind `continue-on-error` with a tail step naming the remedy, the reprice pattern. Pinned in `test/journalRewrite.test.mjs`: every composed entry passes its own validator (the fallback is always publishable), each rail rejects, the client re-validates a tampered artifact away, and the `--fixture` end-to-end proves script and client compose the identical entry. **Estimate copy (Christopher's ruling 2026-07-12)**: `fmtPct` renders whole percents (one decimal only below 1%, positive values floor at "0.1"); `estimateLine` forms, zero-band the single sentence "adds somewhere between 0% and about X% to your time" (the bound is always the NUMBER 0%, never "nothing", Christopher's ruling 2026-07-12, guard-asserted in `test/journalProse.test.mjs`), positive "adds about X%, likely between Y% and Z%" (equal-rounding band ends collapse to "and the band barely strays from that"), whole-band-negative a flagged two-sentence refund ("I'm double-checking that"). The canonical line is always variant 0 of the entry's estimate beat, rotation only happens on CLEAN band classes (pos/zero, an ambiguous band keeps its one honest form so a straddling {pct} can never render a fake negative), and the band beat's variant 0 is `classifyVerdict`'s own copy, so the ruled sentences stay shipped and single-sourced. ALL notebook prose (entries, verdicts, estimates, log and ledger lines) is Greg's plain first person with ZERO em-dashes (Christopher's voice ruling 2026-07-12, guard asserts in the journal test files); framing copy ABOUT Greg (intro, captions, meta lines) stays third person, per the same ruling. CRITICAL honesty rail: the par model changed coefficient scales on 2026-07-02 (seconds → log-multipliers), the Journal reads ONE consistent log-scale series: rows on/after `SCALE_EPOCHS` contribute their live `candidates`, pre-epoch rows ONLY their `candidatesLog`, the sequential-backfit retrodiction (one-off `scripts/backfit-model-history.R`, run 2026-07-12: the CURRENT pipeline refit per historical date on cumulative day-of data with board-date prefix <= D, same filters/OLS-seeded priors/diagnostics gates as the nightly; original `candidates` kept untouched as provenance, per-date fit meta in `candidatesLogFit`, a diagnostics-failed date is skipped and leaves an honest gap). A pre-epoch row's seconds-scale `candidates` never enters a trajectory (a cross-era SD comparison fabricates a ~97% "tightening"; poison-pinned in `test/journalFindings.test.mjs`); retrodicted points carry `retro: true`, render dimmer at smaller radius on the sparkline (whose 100% baseline anchors on the LIVE era's first fit, anchoring on a retrodicted first point would let a prior-hugging early sd read every later honest fit as a 900% explosion), and disclose via the dot tooltip's " · re-measured" suffix (tooltips read "Jul 12 · uncertainty vs Jul 2: 106%"); the standalone "re-measured with today's yardstick" caption was DELETED in PR C (disclosure copy that raises questions it doesn't answer, the hedge-stacking ruling). **Retrodicted points are CHART HISTORY only, `classifyVerdict` windows to the live era's (non-retro) fits**: on a date with little usable signal the fit's posterior mostly echoes its OLS-seeded prior (measured: sonar's sd sat at prior level (~0.002) through Apr 24 with 3 sonar boards on record, then jumped to ~0.032 when the Apr 23 study's scores landed; compass stayed prior-flat into June with 5 boards), and no cheap detector separates "the data spoke" from "the prior echoed", so a verdict sentence spanning retrodictions would narrate a prior artifact as a finding. This deliberately narrows the plan's "verdict reaches back to each study's real start" to the chart only. Studied-but-unnamed features (no `FEATURE_NAMES` entry) are counted, never rendered under their code name. **Logged-out share route `?report=<feature>`** (`src/ui/journalReport.js`, lazy-imported; branch in main.js init IMMEDIATELY AFTER `?crux=`, BEFORE the startup gate, no game init, and the static modelHistory.json fetch needs no auth): renders one finding with the crux-teaser brand block + "Play today's board" CTA, through the SAME `composeEntry`/`buildStudyCard` path as the in-app notebook (the active feature, the latest refit row's target, derivable from shipped data with no experimentTarget fetch, additionally carries its lab log); an unknown or empty id falls back to the journal index (the raw id is never echoed, no jargon, no injection surface); both entries e2e-smoked in `tests/e2e/smoke.spec.mjs`. Share buttons on every journal card and on the report page emit `PROD_SITE_BASE?report=<id>` via `shareFindingLink` (Web Share, clipboard fallback); `shareFindingLink`, `makeShareButton`, `metaSummaryLine`, and the `VERDICT_CHIPS` labels are single-sourced in `src/ui/journalCard.js` (journalView consumes them through `buildStudyCard`/`chipFor`; journalReport imports them directly). `findingById` resolves any NAMED feature (falling back to `deriveStudyForFeature` for a never-targeted one) so a fresh target's share link never dead-ends. The share page deliberately claims NO link between a finding and today's board (the fieldnote-drift class, boards generate days ahead under earlier targets).
- **Greg the character (2026-06-12)**: Greg is a FICTIONAL green-crab field scientist (the name is a joke that stuck; the green-crab invasion angle stays an Easter egg, never the pitch). Canon sentence: "Greg checks every board before you play it, and keeps notes on how you solve." The three Tier 1 smiley slots (`assets/sprites/idle.png` / `win.png` / `loss.png`) ARE Greg, idle holds the clipboard, win throws claws up, loss is flipped on his back; the shipped SVG master is `assets/sprites/greg/a7b-{idle,win,loss}.svg` (the a7 blend with the open smile that matches `idle.png`; `scripts/export-greg-sprites.ps1 -Direction a7b` rasterizes it to the three PNGs at 512px transparent via headless Chrome). The superseded exploration candidates (the a/b/c first round plus the a-a7g drill-down) were archived to `assets/sprites/greg/_exploration/` on 2026-06-25, still reviewable via `scripts/greg-gallery.html`. The spriteLoader chain is untouched: Greg renders only where the resolved emoji equals the canonical 😊/😎/😵 (Classic/Dark); themes that override the smiley keep their identity (`test/spriteChain.test.mjs` pins this). Avatar surfaces always show Greg regardless of theme via `spriteImgHTML('smiley', ...)`: the Daily field note (`.sprite-greg-note`), the win-modal Greg's Time line (`.sprite-greg-par`, daily + timed), and the **leaderboard ghost row** (`_gregGhostRow` in main.js), Greg's par spliced into the daily AND weekly scores tables at its par-sorted position (ties go to the player), dashed `.greg-row` styling, crab sprite where the rank number would be, NO rank so humans keep theirs, Friends view included, skipped when par is unavailable. **Meet-Greg card** (`#meet-greg-modal`, outside `#app`): one-time intro on the first title-screen view once onboarded (`hasSeenNotice('meet_greg')`), idle sprite + two canon sentences.
