The social preview `assets/og-card.png` stays the photographic underwater-mines card; a drawn flat-style replacement was rejected on review (2026-06-12), so any og-card change keeps the photographic mood.
- **Greg’s Gym** (player-facing name; modules keep their original lexicon filenames) (`src/logic/lexicon.js` + `src/ui/lexiconUI.js`, the 🏋️ mode card on the title screen, a play destination, not footer chrome; lazy-loaded): a multi-lesson curriculum behind the **deducibility click-gate**, clicking a cell that is not currently provably safe bounces and pulses the proving region of an available deduction; the player physically cannot luck through, and the technique is NAMED only after they perform it. Flags are gated the same way (right-click / long-press): a flag only sticks on a square the clues can settle as a MINE, so a flag is a worked deduction, never a guess marker; tapping a flagged square unflags it. The gym overlay holds three views: a **lesson-select screen** (`openLexicon` lands here, not on a board), the **drill**, and **Greg's Field Notebook**; `_showView` toggles them and the `‹ Lessons` back button.
  - **The curriculum** is `LESSON_ORDER = [countingBasics, subset11, subset12, holes, triangles, oneTwoOne, oneTwoTwoOne, oneThreeOneCorner, twoTwoTwoCorner]` in `lexicon.js` (nine lessons, easiest first). Each lesson is `{id, name, blurb, rule, naming, rows, cols, mines, accepts(r), requiresPattern?, attempts?}`. The **admission predicate IS the curriculum**: `accepts(r)` is the bucket gate over the `isBoardSolvable` result; named-shape lessons add `requiresPattern` ('1-1' / '1-2' / 'hole' / 'triangle' / '1-2-1' / '1-2-2-1' / '1-3-1' / '2-2-2'), which `generateLessonBoard` verifies via `lessonRequiresShape` for the 7 STRICT shapes (`requireShape: true`) or `lessonShowsPattern` for the 2 RECOGNITION composites (1-2-1 / 1-2-2-1, `requireShape: false`, they CANNOT be forced, see below). Both read the shared `patternNames` detector the coaching and receipts use, so a lesson can never teach a shape the rest of the game cannot name. (`lessonShowsPattern`, the lighter "appears on a path" check, remains as a tested utility but is no longer the gate for any lesson.) **`lessonShowsPattern` models REAL PLAY, one reveal at a time, not a frontier wave**: it snapshots the reveal state, then repeatedly checks whether the target shape is the top read of any provable cell and, if not, makes a SINGLE flood-click and looks again (restoring the board after). One-at-a-time matters two ways: (1) triangles and 2-2-2 corners form mid-solve, not at the opening (triangles measured 0% at the opening), so an opening-only check missed them; (2) holes/triangles are TRANSIENT, the boxed pocket dissolves into a plain 1-1 the instant the wider clue's other neighbor is revealed, so a wave check (classify the whole frontier at its peak) admits boards a one-at-a-time player can never perform the shape on. Measured: under a wave check triangles surfaced ~100% but only ~80% of real one-at-a-time play, and 2-2-2 corners ~67%, a "2-2-2 corner" lesson the player could finish a third of the time without ever performing a 2-2-2, breaking the gym's premise (named only after you perform it). The one-at-a-time gate lifts engaged-play performance to 20/20 for every named-shape lesson while keeping yields healthy. **The 7 strict shapes REQUIRE the shape (`lessonRequiresShape`), rebuilt 2026-06-16 on a SOUND substrate** (`minimalProof.js` + `proofClassify.js`): the gate plays FLAGS-AWARE and, for each provable cell, asks `simplestGatingRank` for the CHEAPEST shape that PROVABLY forces it (every rank gated by `subsetForces`, the cited clues must force the square across all satisfying assignments). It flags/reveals every cell solvable by something SIMPLER than the lesson's shape (rank < the shape's curriculum rank) and admits the board only if that play STALLS with a shape-rank cell still required → the technique is unavoidable. This replaced the old flags-BLIND gate, which mistook a flag-then-count cell for a forced subset read and so admitted boards solvable by pure counting (Christopher's report: holes / triangles / 1-2-1 / 1-2-2-1 / 2-2-2 all leaked counting-solvable boards). **1-2-1 and 1-2-2-1 are RECOGNITION lessons, not forced ones, proven IMPOSSIBLE to force (2026-06-16):** a 1-2-1 ALWAYS decomposes, its two outer mines are each a plain 1-2 (the 1 ⊂ the 2, rank 2 < the 1-2-1's rank 5), so a flagging player flags those and the middle COUNTS out; `lessonRequiresShape` correctly returns false for every 1-2-1 board. It IS a pair of 1-2s and cannot be required the way an ambiguous-pocket hole or a forced-corner 1-3-1 can. So those two use `lessonShowsPattern` + an accepts gate demanding real subset reasoning (`techniqueLevel >= 1`, not pure counting) on normal boards (fast, ~6-90ms); the player recognizes the shape and the coaching names it. The other 7 shapes have a genuinely irreducible deduction (ambiguous pocket / forced corner mine / no cheaper proof) and are force-required. **The 1-2 lesson was the hard case and exposed a real classifier bug**: evaluated soundly, 1-2 was 0%-required (1-1 was 97%). Two reasons. (a) `classifyPattern` named pairs from the frontier's ATTRIBUTED sources, which shadow a 1-2's freed square to "1-1," so '1-2' almost never surfaced. (b) the textbook 1-2's headline payload is a forced MINE (the square only the 2 sees), and the gym never requires flagging (you finish by revealing safes; the gate is flags-blind, so a flag never unlocks anything), so a mine-find can't be "required." The fix recognizes the 1-2 by its OVERLAP geometry (`matchesOverlapPair`), which finds the forced-SAFE square the lesson's own rule already describes, "the square only the smaller number sees is safe." That safe square IS requirable, exactly like 1-1, so 1-2 went 0% → 100%. `matchesOverlapPair` (two plain clues sharing hidden squares; when `valBig - valSmall === |bigClue's exclusive|`, the small clue's exclusive squares are all safe and the big clue's exclusive all mine; 1-1 is the equal-value subset case) replaced the shadow-prone source-based pair naming in `classifyPattern`, which also fixes how 1-2s are named in coaching and receipts. `countingBasics` = `techniqueLevel === 0 && passAMoves >= 3`; `subset11` / `subset12` share the canonical-subset bucket gate (tier 1, nothing harder) and are split by `requiresPattern` '1-1' vs '1-2' (the bucket counts can't tell a 1-1 from a 1-2; the classifier reads the two clue digits); `holes` / `triangles` = solvable, `techniqueLevel <= 1`, no advanced logic, with the boxed-pocket shape on the path (a clue boxed to exactly 2 hidden cells, a hole, or exactly 3, a triangle, sitting inside a wider ≥4-hidden clue that shares the whole pocket, so the wider clue's extra cells are forced; it is a generic subset given a shape name); `oneTwoOne` / `oneTwoTwoOne` / `oneThreeOneCorner` / `twoTwoTwoCorner` = solvable, no liar, `techniqueLevel <= 2`, shape on the path (`oneThreeOneCorner` = a 3 at an L bend with a 1 on each arm sees five squares; the two 1s cap four of them at one mine each, forcing the third mine into the square only the 3 sees, leaving each 1's far square safe, a modified 1-2; `twoTwoTwoCorner` = the hardest, a tier-2 multi-clue read: a central 2 adjacent to the safe target whose OTHER hidden cells split into two disjoint groups, each lower-bounded to ≥1 mine by a flanking 2, the two groups use up the central 2's whole count, so the square only the central 2 sees is safe). `lessonRequiresShape` reveals by FLOODING zeros (like a real click), so a revealed 0-clue never sits with hidden neighbors, that impossible state otherwise confuses the recognizers. Measured generation time (2026-06-16, the rebuilt gate; median over varied seeds): holes ~270ms, triangles ~380ms, oneThreeOneCorner ~120ms, twoTwoTwoCorner ~630ms (the dearest strict), oneTwoOne ~6ms, oneTwoTwoOne ~88ms (recognition composites are near-instant on normal boards); worst-case seed ~1-2.3s, and NOTHING fails to generate, live, no pre-baked content. A one-time per-board cost behind the lesson-load; add a spinner or tune board size if a strict corner ever feels slow. The flags-aware gate flags only AVOIDABLE mines (rank cheaper than the shape), so a shape's OWN forced mine, the 1-3-1 corner, the 1-2-1's outer 1-2s, stays unflagged and its recognizer still sees it (the old gate's pocket-family-only flagging hack is gone). Per-lesson `attempts` budgets cover the lower strict yields. Verified end-to-end in `test/lexicon.test.mjs` (the 6 strict lessons REQUIRE their shape, the 2 recognition composites SHOW theirs + need subset reasoning, all 9 generate and stay completable through provably-safe clicks alone), with the underlying classifier soundness swept against a brute-force oracle in `test/proofClassify.test.mjs` (0 violations over ~14K named cells). **Hard gate (added after triangles once shipped wired-but-non-generating at 0/4000, count-based shape detection was shadowed by the frontier attributing each cell to its first/smallest subset):** every lesson must measure >0 yield BEFORE it ships. Tested invariant: every lesson is completable through provably-safe clicks alone (the gate can never soft-lock). **Gym entry:** the `#mode-card-gym` card has the `.mode-card` class for the title grid but NO `data-mode`; the generic mode-card handler in main.js guards `if (!mode) return` so it can't fall through to `switchMode(undefined)` (which started a hidden challenge game under the gym overlay, fixed 2026-06-15).
  - **Shared pattern detector** (`src/logic/patternNames.js`, pure, node-tested in `test/patternNames.test.mjs`): `classifyPattern(board, ded, {rows, cols, neighborCache?}) -> {name, family}` over a `{row, col, tier, sources, kind}` frontier/trace entry. Names: `1-1 | 1-2 | 1-2-1 | 1-2-2-1 | 1-3-1 | hole | triangle | 2-2-2 | flag-reduction | pair` (tier-1 non-canonical, e.g. 2-3) `| region` (tier-2/3 enumeration, deliberately unnamed) `| count` (tier-0). **Geometry, not tier:** the SAME visual 1-2-1 resolves at tier 1 or tier 2 depending on flanking clues (a flanking 1-1 subset gives a tier-1 path), so the named shapes are recognized by board scans, never by the move-type buckets (which only say "the larger constraint had ≤3 unknowns"). `isOneTwoOne` / `isOneTwoTwoOne`, a collinear run of plain clues with the value sequence whose HIDDEN neighbors all sit on ONE side of the clue line (the forcing "front"), consecutive clues overlapping, target in the front. The 1-3-1 corner is NOT collinear: `isOneThreeOneCorner` matches a 3 with two perpendicular orthogonal 1-neighbors (the L bend) whose five hidden squares partition into the two 1s' disjoint shared pairs plus the single square only the 3 sees; it is `kind`-aware (that lone square is the forced MINE, the two 1s' outer squares the forced SAFE). `isHole` / `isTriangle` (`matchesPocket`): a plain clue boxed to exactly k hidden cells (k=2 hole, k=3 triangle) whose EFFECTIVE value (face value minus adjacent KNOWN mines, flagged, revealed, or strike) is LESS THAN k, an AMBIGUOUS pocket: a mine is in there but you don't know which cell, and that ambiguity IS the hole/triangle, that is a SUBSET of a wider plain clue (≥4 hidden) of the SAME EFFECTIVE value sharing the whole pocket, so the wider clue's extra cells are SAFE. The `effective < k` test is LOAD-BEARING: effective == k means the pocket is ALL mines (unambiguous), a plain reduction, NOT a hole; that false match (using FACE value) shipped non-hole boards under the Holes lesson until 2026-06-15 (Christopher: "not all holes require a hole, are you sure you know what a hole is?"). The EFFECTIVE value is why TRIANGLES carry LARGER NUMBERS: a 3 next to a marked mine is effectively a 2 over a 3-cell pocket, still ambiguous (minesweeper.online's holes H1-H3 are all 1s; the triangle examples go to 2-3 with a marked mine). Holes are the '1-1' family by construction (equal effective value). The marked mine must be PRESENT (flagged/revealed) for the recognizer to count it, which is why `lessonRequiresShape` flags proven mines for the pocket family (see the gym section). `matchesOverlapPair` names the bare **1-1 / 1-2** by OVERLAP geometry (two plain clues sharing hidden squares; when `valBig - valSmall === |bigClue exclusive|`, the small clue's exclusive squares are all safe and the big clue's exclusive all mine, 1-1 is the equal-value subset case). 0-clues are excluded (a 0-clue is counting, not a pair) and it is **soundness-swept against ground truth** in `test/patternNames.test.mjs` (a named-safe square is never an actual mine, a named-mine square always is, across 150 full solves; the recognizer can even out-prove the frontier's gauss on big components, so the sweep checks ground truth, not frontier membership). This REPLACED the old source-based pair naming, which read the frontier's attributed sources and shadowed a 1-2's freed square to "1-1", so '1-2' never surfaced and the 1-2 lesson couldn't be required (it measured 0% required until this; see the gym section). The 1-2's requirable payload is its forced-SAFE square (the square only the 1 sees), NOT its mine, the gym never requires flagging, so a mine-find can't gate a lesson. `isTwoTwoTwoCorner` (gym-only, the hardest): a central plain 2 adjacent to the target whose other hidden cells split into two DISJOINT groups, each lower-bounded ≥1 by a flanking 2 (each flank's effective count minus the cells it sees outside the shared group), two disjoint ≥1 groups consume the central 2's whole count, freeing the cell only the central 2 sees; flags-blind (uses revealed/strike mines, the only knowns on a gym board). An incidental 1,2,1 digit run in open field (hidden on both sides) fails the one-side test and is never named, the **honesty contract** (a tier-2/3 region with no recognizable shape returns `region`, never a fabricated name). `isTwoTwoTwoCorner` is **soundness-swept** in `test/patternNames.test.mjs`: across generated boards it must NEVER name a cell the flags-blind solver can't independently prove safe. `hole` / `triangle` / `2-2-2` are **gym-only**, deliberately NOT in proofExplainer's `NAMED_SHAPES`, so a generic subset that isn't a visually-clean pocket can never make a receipt overclaim. Because the recognizers read HIDDEN neighbors, the caller must pass a state where the pattern's cells are still hidden. **Critical:** the gym classifies a move BEFORE it mutates the board, `_onCellClick` computes `moveName` before `_floodOpen` (flooding dissolves the front, so a post-reveal classify falls back from `1-2-1` to the bare pair `1-1`), and `_tryFlag` classifies before setting `isFlagged` (flagging removes the square from its clues' hidden sets, this is what lets the 1-3-1's forced-corner FLAG be named `1-3-1` rather than a bare tier-2 region). `_celebrate` cheers a named shape for a safe reveal OR a proven-mine flag (`PATTERN_SHAPE_NAMES`).
  - **Sound-proof substrate** (`src/logic/minimalProof.js` + `src/logic/proofClassify.js`, node-tested in `test/proofClassify.test.mjs`), the engine the rebuilt require-gate (and any future per-technique stat) rests on. `minimalProof.js` reads the constraint system `boardSolver.buildBoardConstraints` builds (refactored OUT of `findDeducibleFrontier` so both share one source of truth) and, for any deduced cell, finds its MINIMAL forcing clue-subset: SOUND (a subset that forces a cell forces it on the full board, by monotonicity, adding constraints only removes satisfying assignments) and COMPLETE (every deduction has one, else honest `region`); `subsetForces` is the soundness gate (the cited clues must force the square across every satisfying assignment). `proofClassify.js` names a cell FROM its proof: `classifyByProof` returns the RICHEST shape whose clues provably force it ("most specific wins", for player-facing technique stats), `simplestGatingRank` the CHEAPEST ("is anything simpler enough?", for the require-gate). Geometry (the `patternNames` recognizers, which now report their matched clues via an optional capture param) DESCRIBES a shape; `subsetForces` PROVES it, so a name can never overclaim on incidental geometry, the soundness flaw the old board-scan recognizers had no defense against. Swept against a brute-force oracle: 0 unsound names over ~14K classifications.
  - **Receipt naming**: `proofExplainer.explainDeduction` appends "That is the 1-2-1 pattern." to the tier-1/tier-2 sentence in the `full` style only, gated on `classifyPattern` returning a player-known shape, so the win-receipt confession, the daily crux teaser sentence, and post-loss copy all gain the honest name with zero extra wiring.
  - **Lesson-aware coaching** (`_celebrate`, keyed on the classifier name, not the tier): named shapes earn a cheer every time (the first per board includes a recognition tip from `PATTERN_COACH`); proven-mine flags get their reasoning once per board then a nod; the **Counting lesson frames every reveal as counting** (a tier-0 board is solved by flagging the forced mines and clearing what those numbers satisfy, the player's process IS counting, even where the flags-blind gate routes a square through a subset), with the flag-reduction beat ("flag it, and every number beside it now needs one fewer") taught on the first flag. `flag-reduction` is NOT a standalone lesson (it is literally Pass A, `remaining = expected - flagged`); it lives inside Counting. A bigger subset pair (a `pair` classification, e.g. 2-2 or 3-2) gets a `_nodPair` nod, "a 2-2 is a 1-1 wearing bigger numbers... what matters is that they match, not their size" (family-keyed on `classifyPattern`'s `family`), so the player learns to read past the larger digits and see the basic 1-1/1-2 underneath. Every performed technique is recorded to `gymTechniqueCounts` in `statsStorage.js` (local-only, `recordGymTechnique` / `getGymTechniqueCounts`; a disguised pair records under its family). All messages render on the `.lexicon-coach` line UNDER the board, updated in place.
  - **Field Notebook** (`_renderNotebook`, a view inside the gym): one entry per technique with a tiny board sketch (`SKETCHES`, rendered as `.lexicon-sketch` mini-cells), the plain `rule`, and the player's gym performance count (`TECHNIQUE_KEYS` sums the classifier names per lesson). V1 counts gym performances only; receipts may NAME a shape but never claim "you performed this live."
  - The gym is fully voiced through the theme sound engine (reveal/cascade/flag/unflag/win + `playGateBounce`) and supports CHORDING, gym flags are gate-proven mines, so a satisfied number has proven its remaining neighbors safe and the chord can never hit a mine (a half-flagged chord attempt gets the teaching bounce; an idle tap on an unflagged number stays silent).

**Generation (challenge mode):** `buildChallenge250Board` in `src/logic/challenge250Builder.js`, the ONE builder the play path (gameActions' challenge branch at newGame) and the offline validator share. Rect path: pre-generated wall edges → wall-aware `generateBoard` → up to 25 gimmick re-rolls per base (60 bases) with **constructive mystery** (`placeMysteryConstructive`, shared from boardGenerator.js, placed one at a time, verified after each, kept only if the board stays solvable) → certify from the fixed opener → strict load-bearing. Tiling path: `generateTilingBoard` with an infinite load-bearing budget + the result's `decorative` stamp checked on the outcome. No technique floor (par-per-cell tiers carry difficulty); the opener blocks gate each draw on the 3-deduction floor instead. Timed still generates plain boards on the first click; chaos generates on first click and stays exempt from solvability.

## Important Patterns

- **DOM structure:** `#title-screen` is a sibling of `#app`, not inside it. Modals are inside `#app`, when `#app` has `.hidden` class (display: none), child modals can't render. Modals that need to show on title screen must be outside `#app`.
- **Title screen layout (2026-06-12 grouping; heroes 2026-08-10):** six mode cards, **Daily and Weekly lead as full-width HEROES** (`.mode-card-hero`), then The Climb, Quick Play, Chaos and Greg's Gym in the 2-column grid below (the Gym card has no `data-mode`, it opens the lesson overlay). An odd visible count among the GRID cards spans the last card full-width since locked Chaos hides its card, counted by `updateTitleProgress`, which toggles `.odd-cards` on the grid, because the hidden Chaos card keeps its `:nth-child` slot and a pure-CSS odd test can't see it. **The heroes are excluded from that count** (they always span, so they can never be the orphan); with exactly two heroes the parity is unchanged either way, which is why the exclusion is written down rather than relied on. A hero turns its content on its side, icon beside the text instead of above it, and takes the **Past chip into the grid as a real item** (`grid-template-areas: "icon name past" / "icon note note"`) rather than leaving it an absolute corner: on a hero the note's first line reaches the top-right, measured on neon. That also frees the hero's own padding, which is where the extra grid row is paid for; measured at 360x630 (the reference phone with browser chrome showing) the six-card front door is SHORTER than the pre-hero one (fits with 18px spare where the flat grid had 11, and 7px of scroll vs 14 with Chaos unlocked). The hero band (`padding-bottom`) must clear the TALLEST theme's stat line, chalkboard's Comic Sans runs ~3px taller than classic's at the same font-size, and 20px put the note over the streak there. Footer: a three-button footer: How to Play, Progress (`#progress-sheet`: Stats / Achievements / Leaderboard), More (`#more-sheet`: Collection / Settings / Greg's Journal / What's New). Sheet rows call `hideModal(sheet)` then the destination's prep + `showModalFromTitle`, plain hideModal so `_returnToTitle` survives the hop and the destination's close returns to the title. The What's New NEW badge rides the sheet row; `#more-btn-dot` on the More button shows the signal while the sheet is closed. The Modifiers help tab is rendered from `getGimmickDefs()` at boot (`#help-modifier-list`) so the reference can't drift.
- **`_returnToTitle` flag** in `src/ui/titleScreen.js` tracks when modals were opened from the title screen (set via `showModalFromTitle`, consumed by `closeModalAndReturn`; `setReturnToTitle(false)` for entering-a-game paths).
- **`$()` / `$$()`** are querySelector/querySelectorAll helpers from `src/ui/domHelpers.js`.
- **"Gimmicks" in code = "Modifiers" in player-facing UI.** Always use "Modifier" in user-facing text.
- **Daily mode** uses seeded RNG (`createDailyRNG(dateString)`) for deterministic boards per date.
- **`CURRENT_VERSION`** in main.js drives the "What's New" badge: first load after an update shows a NEW badge on the title-screen What's New button; the modal opens on click (it does NOT auto-open).
- **`generateBoard` calls `cleanSolverArtifacts`** on every return path (solver leaks `isRevealed` onto cells).

## Service Worker (sw.js)

Network-first with cache fallback. `ignoreSearch: true` on cache.match. Install uses `cache: 'reload'` to bypass HTTP cache. Activation deletes all old cache versions. Core assets pre-cached; non-default themes lazy-load.

**Cache busting:** Only bump `CACHE_NAME` in `sw.js`. No per-file `?v=` query strings on imports. SW registers on localhost too, unregister via DevTools or add `?v=N` when testing locally.

## Game Modes

**The ladder is "The Climb" to a PLAYER and "challenge" to the code (2026-08-10).** The word "Challenge" is reserved for the head-to-head mode; every player-visible string that meant the ladder now says The Climb (or "Climb Level N"). Nothing STORED moved and nothing may: the runtime mode value is `'normal'`, the storage name is `'challenge'`, and the module filenames (`challenge250.js` et al.) are coupled to sw.js precache paths. Two tests hold the line from opposite sides, `test/challengeStorageContract.test.mjs` requires the nine stored things to keep the name, and `test/climbCopy.test.mjs` forbids the word in player-visible copy (scanning index.html's visible text plus every `src/**/*.js`, with a `SANCTIONED_PHRASES` hook for the head-to-head mode's own copy when it lands). A find-and-replace across the repo therefore turns one of them red rather than turning up in production.
- **The Climb (`gameMode: 'normal'`, stored as `challenge`):** the Challenge 250 ladder, 250 authored levels in 50 blocks of 5; a level is a PAR RATING (spec = tier × shape × modifier set) and every attempt draws a fresh certified frozen board with the marked Start-here opener. Checkpoints every 5 (block = survival unit; death returns to block start); classic loss + lifelines, never strikes; modifiers from L6 (walls intro block); all seven shapes from block 6 on.
- **Timed:** Race the clock, 4 difficulty tabs (Beginner/Intermediate/Expert/Extreme). Every timed board gets a par at generation (same PAR_MODEL as daily; gimmick terms zero on gimmick-free boards) shown as a par-relative delta on the win modal; wins submit {name, time, level, uid, par, features} to timed/{pushId} (features embedded per row - every timed board is unique, no meta bucket to join). Quick play has its OWN equation, PAR_MODEL_TIMED (between TIMED_PAR_MODEL markers in difficulty.js, refit-owned): timed rows exist only on WINS (losses never report), so the sample is win-censored and is never pooled with daily completions. The refit fits it as par-for-a-winning-run on the LOG scale (like the daily model, `scale:'log'`), on ratio-adjusted times (`log(time / k)`, dividing out the player's multiplicative handicap) with priors centered on the daily log posterior and a two-tailed outlier screen ([0.3x, 3x] predicted - the slow tail catches AFK rows); below TIMED_FIT_THRESHOLD=20 usable rows it ships as a verbatim copy of PAR_MODEL. predictPar selects the model on features.modeTimed (stamped by the timed path).
- **Daily:** One seeded puzzle per day, no levels. Modifiers are force-injected on improvement days when the target maps to a gimmick (sonar, wormhole, walls, mystery, liar, locked, mirror, compass, worm), see Adaptive Experimental Design. Falls back to the natural ~35% lottery only when the target is a non-gimmick feature (move-type counts, structural features). The SHAPE ROTATION (50% square / 50% one of the six tilings, dark behind `TILING_ROTATION_START = null`) lives in `src/logic/shapeRotation.js`, see the Board Topology section. Past dailies are replayable via the **Daily Archive** (a "Past dailies" link on the Daily card opens a month calendar), see that section.
- **Weekly:** ONE single canonical puzzle for the whole ET week (Monday → Sunday), one attempt per day for 7 days. Best time across the 7 attempts wins. Same bomb-hit mechanic as daily (info-value penalty: `+infoValue + BOMB_PENALTY_BASE × (1 + BOMB_PENALTY_RAMP × (strikeNumber − 1))`, the base RAMPS gently per hit (`strikeNumber = priorHits + 1`, `BOMB_PENALTY_RAMP = 0.5` in `handleDailyBombHit`, so 1st +3s, 2nd +4.5s, 3rd +6s, 4th +7.5s …; first hit unchanged, each later one adds half a base, discourages casual mine-popping without clobbering a legit multi-hit day, since the anti-cheat gate below handles brute-force; softened 2026-06-16 from a steeper × n ramp), where info-value is the par-seconds of deduction the struck mine was anchoring, computed by `computeBombInfoValue` via a double solver run, see `src/logic/bombInfoValue.js`. NO re-fog: every other revealed cell stays revealed; the mine is preserved so adjacent numbers don't drop. The hit cell stays revealed with `cell.isStrike = true` so the strike sprite marks where the bomb was; strike cells count as flags for chording, persist across subsequent hits, AND subtract from the LCD mine counter (`mineCounterRemaining` in headerRenderer, board-derived from `cell.isStrike`, mode-blind: the old per-mode bombHits read was gated on daily/weekly, so Par Lab strikes never reached the display and the counter sat stuck, useless as an endgame counting clue, his report 2026-08-02; pinned in `test/remainingSafe.test.mjs`). A chord that exposes SEVERAL mines (two wrong flags around a satisfied number) charges EVERY one as its own strike in one batch, per-mine marginal info-value + ramped base, one shared popup (`handleDailyBombHit(row, col, extraMines)`; pinned in `test/chordPerMine.test.mjs`); challenge/timed keep the re-fog (`unrevealChordMines`) since one lifeline/loss handles the first mine there. Penalties live in the `bombHitEvents` log, not in wall-clock `elapsedTime`. Popup labels Minor/Key/Critical mines at info-value ≥ 2 / 6.5 / 13, quantile-anchored by `scripts/reanchor-bomb-tiers.mjs`. No game-over. **Anti-cheat:** a run that finds most of the board's mines by stepping on them is probing the layout, not playing, `isBombHitCheat` (difficulty.js) flags it and the submit gates in `submitOnlineScore` / `submitWeeklyScore` / `submitArchiveScore` reject it (returns `'cheat'` / `false`, never queued for retry), so it never reaches the leaderboard or the par fit. The player still finishes, and **the DAY still counts**, streak, completion and the `dailyHistory` row are all written, the same ruling the `'divergent'` branch carries ("I don't want people losing their streak, but I also don't want bad data"). **TWO arms, because a bare fraction is scale-FREE and the board scale moved under it** (2026-08-09): flagged when `hits > max(BOMB_HIT_CHEAT_FLOOR (10), BOMB_HIT_CHEAT_FRACTION (0.50) × mines)` OR `hits >= BOMB_HIT_EXCAVATED_FRACTION (0.80) × mines`. The first arm is "far more mistakes than a bad day", floored because half of a 6-mine board proves nothing; the second is "you excavated the board", and it is the ONLY arm that can fire where the floor exceeds the mine count, so without it every board of ≤10 mines would be ungated even against a total excavation. **The old flat 30% was written against the 25-30-mine rectangles of the time, where it means nine mistakes**, and the shape rotation ships `TILING_BAND_CONFIGS` entries with as few as **6 mines**, where the same 30% means TWO. It refused Kate at 3 hits on the 9-mine Kites daily of 2026-08-09, an ordinary bad day on the dearest lattice we ship, and cost her the leaderboard row and (before this) the dot on her own history chart. **Calibrated on every row ever submitted**: 602 per-attempt readings across daily / archive / weekly, worst GENUINE run 25% of the mines (Kate, 7/28 on 2026-05-01, two hits from refusal under the old rule), the three real probing episodes at 81 / 100 / 100%. The two populations never come near each other, and loosening to these numbers changes **ZERO historical verdicts**, the same four readings are refused before and after, so the par fit is byte-identical. Note the observed 25% is **CENSORED by this very gate** (a refused run was never written, so the true right tail of honest play is unobservable, which is exactly what happened to Kate), which is why the floor sits at 10 rather than the 8 the visible data alone would put it. Mirrored in `refit-par-model.R`, `backfit-model-history.R`, `par-experiment.qmd`, `intercept-sensitivity.qmd` and `fit-parlab-priors.qmd`, **all six copies must move together**; pinned in `test/bomb-penalty.test.mjs`.) Board size 8-14 × 8-14, 2-4 modifiers stacked, daily-density. Architecture mirrors daily's canonical-board: `weeklyBoard/{weekStart}` is write-once on Firebase, pre-generated by `precompute-weekly-board.yml` Monday 00:00 UTC (`scripts/precompute-weekly-board.mjs`). Cloud-synced one-attempt-per-day cap at `users/{uid}/weeklyAttempts/{weekStart}/dayAttempts/{N}` so clearing localStorage can't grant a second try. **The cap refuses a SECOND attempt; RESUMING the first is not one** (issue #246). The attempt is committed on the FIRST CLICK, so a mine hit followed by a restart cannot buy another, but the mode card refused entry on that same marker, and since it is the only production caller of `switchMode('weekly')`, the resume branch behind it could never run: one Home tap mid-attempt and the board was unreachable for good, the day spent on a puzzle nobody finished. All three doors (mode card, `?mode=weekly`, Play Again) now route through the pure `weeklyEntryPlan` in resumeEligibility.js, `attempted` from the marker, `resumable` from `canResumeMode('weekly')`, which reads the same slot against the same clock context `tryResumeGame` uses. A FINISHED attempt reports `resumable: false` because winning clears the slot, so the block still fires exactly where it should. Gathered once in `weeklyEntryContext` (main.js) so the three doors cannot drift; pinned in `test/resumeEligibility.test.mjs`, including a source scan that no door regrows a private marker check. Note the e2e harness CANNOT reach this class: `markWeeklyDayAttempted` is a no-op under `isTestEnvironment()`, so the gate never arms there. Leaderboard is one row per player per week at `weekly/{weekStart}/{uid}` (set, not push) carrying `bestTime` and a `dayTimes` map. Submission helpers: `submitWeeklyScore`/`fetchWeeklyLeaderboard` (firebaseLeaderboard.js), `markWeeklyDayAttempted`/`loadWeeklyAttempts` (firebaseProgress.js). Seed selection in `selectWeeklyRngSeed.js` scores 10 candidates by `activeGimmicks.length + advancedLogicMoves * 0.01`, weighted toward the week's target par by the banded argmax in `parBand.js` (weekly band 60-360s, see Par Bands; the precompute calls this same function, ONE copy). Gimmick pool: `getWeeklyGimmicks` picks 2-4 distinct from the daily-safe subset, no `singleOnly`. **First-attempt-of-the-week qualifies for par-model fit data, LIVE since 2026-08-04, and it never was before.** The submission has existed since v1.6 behind `WEEKLY_FIT_DATA_ENABLED`, set false as a deliberate hold while the weekly's rules were still moving; the flag outlived its reason and was never flipped, so `daily/{weekStart}_weekly_first` was EMPTY for all fourteen weeks with leaderboard rows. The symptom was silent because the PRECOMPUTE writes the matching `dailyMeta/{weekStart}_weekly_first` at generation and only the SCORE half was gated, meta present, scores absent, nothing failing. `scripts/backfill-weekly-fit-rows.mjs` (+ its dispatch workflow) recovered all 34 player-weeks: each player's first played day comes out of the day-indexed `dayTimes`/`dayBombHits` (holes for unplayed days, and Firebase returns a sparse array as an OBJECT, so both shapes are read), and features are recomputed from the stored canonical. It deliberately includes NO invented `bombHitEvents`/`wormEvents`, those were never recorded per attempt, so the rows join the refit's existing legacy-bomb path rather than faking precision, and is marked `backfilled: true` (whitelisted in the rules) so the fit can always tell them apart. Mechanism: when `Object.keys(state.weeklyDayTimes).length === 0` at win time, winLossHandler additionally calls `submitOnlineScore('{weekStart}_weekly_first', ...)` so the score lands in `daily/{weekStart}_weekly_first` and features in `dailyMeta/{weekStart}_weekly_first`. Days 2-7 attempts are speedruns of a known board and stay out of the fit. Weekly does NOT touch the DAILY streak / handicap / `dailyHistory`, it has its own WEEK streak (below). The Stats modal has a Weekly tab with a 7-day line chart.
- **The Weekly card gets the Daily card's furniture (2026-08-05, v1.11): a week streak, a par, and Past weeklies.** His ask, and the shape of it is the daily's: numbers in the CORNERS so the center line stays one descriptor and the card stays its siblings' height. Week streak bottom-left, par bottom-right, the Past chip top-right; the top-LEFT corner stays deliberately EMPTY, because that is the daily's molt slot and **the weekly has no molt days** (his call: a week is already seven chances at one board, so the insurance would be insuring the window that IS the insurance).
  - **The week streak: ONE completion banks the week** (his rule, "only need to play one of the weekly to get a week streak"), so the seven attempts are the week's generosity rather than seven obligations, and finishing on Tuesday and again on Friday is the same week. COMPLETION banks it, not merely opening a board, the same bar the daily uses, and the weekly has no loss state, so "played" would otherwise be one click on a board nobody solved. Consecutive weeks build it; any wider gap restarts at 1; `best` is a high-water mark that never lapses. A streak is ALIVE through the week after its last completion (this week is still running, so nothing is broken yet) and reads 0 after that, a read-side view, never a rewrite. Math in the pure `src/logic/weeklyProgress.js` (`applyWeekContinuation` / `isWeekStreakAlive` / `projectWeekContinuation` / `liveWeekStreak`), storage in `statsStorage` as a TOP-LEVEL `stats.weekStreak = {streak, best, lastWeek}`, deliberately not `modeStats.weekly`, which does not exist, because creating it would silently start routing every weekly completion through `saveGameResult`'s per-mode counters. Cloud-synced as ONE object at `users/{uid}/weekStreak` (the moltDay atomic-snapshot lesson) and whitelisted in `firebase-rules.json`; the users block ends with `$other: false`, so the whitelist is what keeps the WHOLE progress write from dropping (the 866683d class), pinned in both directions by `test/weekStreak.test.mjs`. Merge rules mirror the daily's on dates: newer week adopted verbatim (even a SHORTER streak, the other device saw the break), same week adopts the longer, older keeps local, and a node with no `lastWeek` says nothing rather than zeroing. **The self-heal PUSHES its correction** (issue #248): `reconcileWeekStreakFromHistory` wrote only localStorage, and nothing but a completion writes this node, so for the very players the backfill exists for, long history, no post-ship completion yet, the cloud kept the value the heal had just disagreed with, and the progress listener re-applies the cloud VERBATIM on every write under `users/{uid}` (a `lastSeen` beacon, a daily completion, another device). The card was raised at boot and knocked back down for the rest of the session, and a completion landing on the low side banked it. Both self-heals now write back: `getWeekStreakRecord()` / `getDailyCloudSnapshot()` are the ONE definition of each node's payload shape (read back from storage, never re-assembled at the call site, the completion path reads the same accessor), and both push AFTER `applyCloudProgress` has merged the cloud in, so what goes up is cloud-merged then corrected rather than a stale local value overwriting a fresher remote one. `getWeekStreakRecord` deliberately returns the STORED streak, not `getWeekStreak`'s lapse-adjusted view: a lapse is a read-side 0, and writing it would make it permanent for every device. **The heal derives from BOTH per-week records now, split at a stated epoch, which closes the two halves #254 left open.** `users/{uid}/weeklyCompletions/{weekStart}` is the per-week COMPLETION record the weekly always lacked: the win path writes it via `markWeeklyCompleted` (firebaseProgress, internally test-gated like the attempt marker) on the week's FIRST completion only, gated on `recordWeeklyCompletion`'s `extended` flag with a write-once rule behind it, payload `{timestamp}` (server sentinel; `backfilled: true` is reserved for the recovery script), whitelist lockstep pinned in both directions by `test/weeklyCompletions.test.mjs` (the 866683d class). The heal's derivation is `bankableWeeks` (weeklyProgress.js): weeks before `WEEKLY_COMPLETIONS_EPOCH` ('2026-08-10', FROZEN in both directions, moving it earlier claims records that were never written and moving it later re-admits the #254 class) keep the documented attempts generosity, since attempts are the only per-week record of that era, while from the epoch on an attempt alone banks NOTHING, so an opened-and-abandoned week stops counting once its era is covered by the record. Both sources route through `streakBearingWeeks`, unchanged and still fail-CLOSED without a `currentWeek` (this module has no clock by design), so the live-week exclusion covers completions exactly as it covers attempts (#254 itself: an abandoned attempt on the LIVE week banked it, spliced it onto a genuine run, measured: a real 2-week run read 3, and raised the monotonic `best`, which nothing can lower); `reconcileWeekStreakFromHistory(attempted, currentWeek, completed)` stays upward-only, which is also what makes a dropped completion write harmless to the streak itself, since the trio is written at the same win. `weekArchiveState`'s set comes from `fetchCompletedWeeks` now, so `'done'` means FINISHED and an attempted-but-abandoned past week is tappable again (the board was never cleared; there is something left to find). History for NAMED players is seeded by `scripts/backfill-weekly-completions.mjs` (+ dispatch workflow, dry-run default): every `weekly/{weekStart}/{uid}` row proves a completion (the rules require `bestTime`, which only a win writes), entries are skip-if-existing so a re-run never replaces a live first-completion stamp, and re-running is the intended way to catch wins from stale clients that predate the record. A NAMELESS player's pre-epoch completions have no evidence anywhere and stay unrecorded, failing OPEN on every surface: those archive weeks read playable (a replay records nothing) and the streak keeps its generous pre-epoch derivation.
  - **Par on the card** comes from `computeWeeklyPar` (parResolve), which solves the week's canonical once per session. There is no cheap local path like the daily's cached features, the weekly's board exists only on Firebase, so `refreshTitleWeeklyPar` REFUSES to start when `state.firebaseReady` is false rather than opening a poll that cannot land, and the corner just goes missing until a later title visit. The weekly still shows no par at the END of a run: days 2-7 are speedruns of a known board, and rating a memorised solve against a first-encounter par means nothing. On the card it is the opposite question, how big the waiting board is, before you spend one of seven attempts on it.
  - **Past weeklies** (`#weekly-archive-modal`, a LIST not a calendar, one board per week, so a month grid would be six empty cells around the row that matters). `weeklyProgress.js` owns the window: `FIRST_ARCHIVE_WEEK = '2026-05-04'` (the mode's launch; there is no backfill, because regenerating a past week from its seed would be a board nobody played) through last week, current week excluded as the live Weekly's job. `launchWeeklyArchive` (modeManager) mirrors `launchDailyArchive` exactly, and `state.isWeeklyArchive` walls the replay off: the caller-set PAST weekStart survives newGame's clock derivation, `weeklyDay` is NULL (it belongs to no day and consumes no attempt), the board comes from `_weeklyArchiveRaw` verbatim and never touches the live stash, there is NO local-gen fallback, it never persists (it shares the weekly save slot), and the whole win block, attempt marking, leaderboard row, first-attempt fit row, week streak, is skipped. Which weeks are marked `'done'` comes from `fetchCompletedWeeks` reading the player's OWN `users/{uid}/weeklyCompletions` keys, never from `weeklyAttempts` (an attempt is a first click, and marking done from attempts locked abandoned weeks out of their own replay) and not the shared `weekly/` leaderboard (no uid index, it grows with every player, and a nameless player's completions never reach it); unknown history fails OPEN, like the daily calendar's. The in-game header names the week instead of a weekday so a replay can never read as this week's attempt.
  - **An archive replay never EXPIRES on a clock wake** (`isLiveGameExpired` now returns false for `isArchivePlay` / `isWeeklyArchive`). Expiry exists so yesterday's unfinished attempt cannot resurrect as today's; a replay is not an attempt at anything, and before this, backgrounding the tab during one and returning expired the board mid-play with a "New day!" toast. The DAILY archive had the same hole and is fixed in the same line.
- **Chaos:** Rapid rounds with random modifiers, exempt from solvability guarantee. **The board's SHAPE rolls per round since 2026-08-04** (`src/logic/chaosShape.js`, pure): the round rolls its own shape, ~50% lattice, and the round's rectangular size is TRANSLATED rather than re-authored, `getChaosDifficulty` ramps a side length and a density, and a lattice has no side length, so the round's CELL COUNT is the currency and each shape picks the (M, N) landing nearest it at the same density. Three things are specific to this mode and none of them applies anywhere else in the game:
  - **The board is a lattice BEFORE the first click.** Chaos generates its mines FROM the click, so the placeholder `createEmptyBoard` is stamped with the real topology at newGame and the mines go into it afterwards. That is also why `generateTilingBoard` gained `openerIndex`: every other tiling caller opens on the patch center, and chaos must open where the player actually clicked or the first click could be a mine.
  - **Two lattices are excluded and it is measured, not aesthetic.** Rhombille and deltoidal both stall: rhombille's certifier has no Pass B and leans on Pass C for every board, and deltoidal measured 3.7s on a round-12 board, against ~1.1s worst for the four that ship. Chaos is the ONE place a multi-second generation is unacceptable, because it happens under a click with the clock about to start; both stay on the authored ladder and in the endless pool, where generation hides behind a level card. Sub-0.22-density plans route `forceConstructive` for the same reason a miss there is a failed click rather than a slow one.
  - **`state.totalMines` is reconciled with the board, not the plan.** The generator places `min(mines, placeable)` since the opener and its neighbors are excluded, so the requested count is a ceiling; the LCD counter is board-derived and the win check counts safe cells, so state has to agree with the board. Pinned in `test/chaosShape.test.mjs` (shape roll, size translation, ribbon guard, a generation-budget check) and `tests/e2e/chaosShape.spec.mjs` (the lattice is on screen pre-click and the click opens ground; break-tested by forcing the roll to zero). Quick Play stays rectangular.

## Privacy / sensor-permission posture

By design, GregSweeper requests ZERO sensor permissions and reads NO clipboard / camera / microphone / location data. Audited 2026-05-10:

- No `navigator.geolocation` calls anywhere.
- No `getUserMedia` (camera / microphone) calls.
- `navigator.clipboard.writeText` is used in `main.js` (Settings copy-link) and `diagnosticsModal.js` (copy diagnostic state). Write-only, modern browsers grant this without a permission prompt for user-initiated actions. No `clipboard.read*`.
- No `navigator.permissions.query()` calls.
- Notifications and persistent-storage are the only Web APIs that prompt the user, and both are gated by explicit opt-in actions (Settings toggle, install heuristic).

When adding a new feature, treat this list as a constraint: if it requires a sensor permission, surface that decision explicitly rather than silently expanding the permission surface.

## Molt Day (daily-streak insurance)
Every 5 consecutive daily completions banks one **molt day** (player-facing name; glyph 🦀), capped at 2. A banked molt day is spent automatically and lazily at the next completion to cover a missed ET day, so the streak survives a day off. When the gap exceeds the bank the streak resets to 1 and the bank is RETAINED (all-or-nothing: a molt day is spent only when it actually saves the streak). Covered days add nothing to the count and never help earn. Weekly never touches streak; archive plays (`state.isArchivePlay`) and practice dailies (`?seed=`, `state.isDailyPractice`, the `isPractice` guard in `saveGameResult`) never earn, spend, or touch it. Archive `dailyHistory` rows carry `archive: true` and are filtered out of the streak derivation by `streakBearingDates` (archiveEligibility.js) before `reconcileStreakFromHistory` runs, so a replayed gap day can't retroactively splice the streak. The metaphor is Greg the crab sheltering while it molts.
- **One pure module** `src/logic/moltDay.js` is the single source of truth (no imports): `applyStreakContinuation` (the commit math), `projectContinuation` (read-only "what would completing today do"), `isStreakAlive` (read-side lapse check, a covered gap is still alive), `coversTonight` (the push gate). Constants `MOLT_EARN_EVERY = 5`, `MOLT_CAP = 2`. Consumed by THREE surfaces so they can never disagree: the completion path (`statsStorage.saveGameResult`), the provisional notice on the title card (`getMoltProvisionalNotice` → `main.js`), and the push script (`send-push.mjs` imports `coversTonight`).
- **Storage** (`statsStorage.js`): `modeStats.daily.moltBanked` + `moltLastUse = { date, covered, streakKept }` (defaults 0/null, backfilled like other added fields). `getDailyStreak()` now returns `{ streak, best, banked }` and is bank-aware (a covered gap reports the running streak, not 0). `saveGameResult` records a transient `_lastMoltEvent` drained once by `consumeMoltEvent()` (NOT persisted, so a stale "earned" can't resurface). `applyCloudProgress` rides the molt fields in the SAME date-anchored snapshot as `(dailyStreak, lastDailyDate)` so the bank never pairs with the other side's streak (pinned by `test/moltDayCloudMerge.test.mjs`). **Legacy-preserve rule (fixed 2026-06-16):** a missing cloud `moltDay` node, OR the bare default `{banked:0, no lastUse}`, is the pre-molt shape every existing account carries, absence of information, NOT an authoritative 0. `adoptMolt` only overwrites the local bank when the cloud carries REAL molt state (`banked > 0` or a recorded `lastUse`); otherwise it preserves local. Without this, the overwrite listener (`applyCloudProgress(cloud, {overwrite:true})` on every snapshot) zeroed the freshly-granted backfill on every boot, so an existing-streak player never kept their molt days (Christopher, 92-day streak, saw 0 crabs). A genuine cross-device spend (`{banked:0}` WITH a `lastUse`) is still adopted, so a spend can't resurrect.
- **Cloud**: `saveProgress` writes `users/{uid}/moltDay = { banked, lastUse }`. The rule block IS whitelisted in `firebase-rules.json`, the `users/{uid}` node ends with `$other: false`, so an un-whitelisted child would make the WHOLE progress `update()` fail validation and drop silently (the 866683d class of bug); pinned by `test/moltDayRules.test.mjs`.
- **One-time launch backfill** (`backfillMoltDays` in statsStorage, `backfillGrant` pure in moltDay.js): an existing streak earns the molt days it would have banked (>4 → 1, >9 → 2). Self-guarding (skips once any bank or `moltLastUse` exists), so it runs safely every boot, never re-grants, and never undoes a spend cross-device; local-only (the next completion syncs it, and a 2nd device converges from the shared streak). Called at boot after `_reconcileDailyStreak`.
- **Daily-card indicators** (`updateTitleProgress`): the card frames Greg's note with its corners, 🦀 tokens AND the streak ("N days") together at bottom-left in one `.daily-corner-group`, par ("Par Ns") bottom-right, the Past chip riding the hero's grid at top-right. The molt tokens sat in their own top-left corner until the card became a full-width hero, whose left column is the mode icon; they ship as ONE positioned group with the streak so neither has to know the other's width, and they may NOT nest (a streak reads 0 while a bank survives, since a lapse past the bank keeps the crabs). The streak + par are PLAIN corner text in the note's muted color (NOT pills, NOT italic), so the Past chip stays the one pill that pops; they hug the corners absolutely like Past (a reserved `padding-bottom` on the daily card keeps them clear of the note across breakpoints). Greg's note keeps the THEME-matched Greg sprite (`themeSpriteImgHTML` resolves the active world's Greg via `getThemeEmoji('smiley')`, not the forced-canonical `spriteImgHTML`). A provisional "Molt day holds your N day streak" replaces the note before play when a cover is holding the streak; completed shows "Played today" + the dimmed `.daily-completed` card. No in-game LCD change.
- **Earned celebration**: earning a molt day flags `flagMoltCelebrate` (statsStorage); `showTitleScreen` drains it once (`consumeMoltCelebrate`) to show the `#molt-earned-modal` popup, and on dismiss the crab drops into the card's top-left corner (`moltPlace` keyframe, `_animateMoltPlacement`). The covered/used event still shows its quiet inline `.molt-note` in the win modal.
- **Tests**: `test/moltDay.test.mjs` (bank-math table + `backfillGrant`), `test/moltDayCloudMerge.test.mjs` (snapshot atomicity + the legacy-preserve rule + an end-to-end legacy-streak backfill-then-listener boot), `test/moltDayRules.test.mjs` (rule block), `test/sendPushMolt.test.mjs` (the three push branches).

## Push Notifications
PWA web push via FCM, opt-in via Settings (`Notifications` block alongside the modifier-popup toggle). The `notify-daily-ready.yml` workflow runs an hourly cron at `0 * * * *` UTC; `send-push.mjs` reads each enabled subscriber's `notificationPrefs.hourLocal` from Firebase and only sends to those whose chosen ET hour matches the current ET hour. So each subscriber gets at most one push per day at the time they picked in Settings.

Message text rotates day-to-day from a small per-category pool (`DAILY_BODIES`, `WEEKLY_BODIES`, `STREAK_BODIES`, `STREAK_COVERED_BODIES` in `send-push.mjs`). Pick is deterministic by date so all subscribers on the same day see the same line, but the wording varies day-to-day instead of repeating "Today's daily is waiting" forever. Categories: `weekly` (Monday, overrides daily), `daily` (default), `streak` (8pm ET evening rescue when `dailyStreak ≥ 3 && lastDailyDate < today`).

The script runs two passes per hour, both routed through the pure decision helpers `reminderDecision` / `streakDecision` (exported from `send-push.mjs`, imported by `test/sendPushMolt.test.mjs`): (1) reminder, sends daily/weekly to users whose `hourLocal` matches the current hour and `dailyReminder !== false`; if a molt day covered yesterday (`moltDay.lastUse.date` === yesterday) the daily body is swapped for an acknowledgment ("Your molt day covered Tuesday. Streak intact at N."). (2) streak-warning, fires only at hour 20 (8pm ET); sends to users whose `streakWarning !== false`, `dailyStreak ≥ 3`, and `lastDailyDate < today`. **Molt-day aware**: if `coversTonight(...)` (the bank covers them skipping tonight) the at-risk warning would be a lie, so a soft `STREAK_COVERED_BODIES` nudge goes out instead. The streak push uses a separate FCM tag so it stacks with the morning reminder instead of replacing it. Debugging: `node scripts/send-push.mjs --dry-run --fixture <users.json> [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--hour N]` prints each pass's decisions with no credentials or network (example fixture at `test/fixtures/push-users.example.json`); the main IIFE is guarded so the module imports cleanly for tests.

- `src/firebase/firebasePush.js`: `enableNotifications({hourLocal})`, `disableNotifications()`, `loadNotificationPrefs()`, `updateNotificationHour()`. Hardcoded `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` constant, set this from the Firebase Console value (Project Settings → Cloud Messaging → Web Push certificates) before push will work. Empty string = `'no-key'` toast.
- Subscription stored at `users/{uid}/pushSubscription = { token, subscribedAt }` with auth.uid match. Token is the FCM-managed string from `messaging.getToken({vapidKey})`, server-side script POSTs against `fcm.googleapis.com/v1/projects/{id}/messages:send` using this token.
- Prefs stored at `users/{uid}/notificationPrefs = { enabled, hourLocal, dailyReminder, streakWarning }`.
- SW handlers in `sw.js`: `push` event renders the notification with icon + tag + deepLink in `data`; `notificationclick` focuses an existing tab matching the deepLink or opens `?mode=daily` / `?mode=weekly`.
- **Manual prereqs (one-time setup before push works)**: (a) Generate VAPID key pair in Firebase Console → Cloud Messaging → store the **public** key as the `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` constant in `src/firebase/firebasePush.js` (safe to ship publicly), (b) Generate a service-account JSON in Firebase Console → Project Settings → Service Accounts → store the entire JSON (one line) as the `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` repo secret. Without (a) the toggle shows "Push not configured yet"; without (b) the workflow fails on access-token mint.
- iOS: requires add-to-home-screen on iOS 16.4+. `firebasePush.js` detects iOS-non-standalone and surfaces "Install GregSweeper to your home screen first" instead of silently failing. Android/desktop work without that hoop.
- Categories: `weekly` (Mon morning), `daily` (default), `streak` (8pm ET rescue). The cron picks based on the calendar; per-category opt-out via `notificationPrefs.dailyReminder` (default true) and `notificationPrefs.streakWarning` (default false, explicit opt-in via Settings).

## Greg-par Model (Daily)
Daily par is a **log-linear (multiplicative) regression** over board features (`PAR_MODEL` carries `scale: 'log'`): `par = exp(intercept + Σ coef·feature)`, the lognormal MEDIAN, so par is always positive (no more negative-intercept-on-tiny-boards bug) and each coefficient is a **log-multiplier**, NOT seconds. Fit in R offline against real completion data. Coefficients are in `PAR_MODEL` in `src/logic/difficulty.js`; `predictPar` branches on `model.scale` (absent ⇒ the legacy additive-seconds form). The whole model is in `src/logic/dailyFeatures.js` (`computeDailyFeatures`, `predictPar`, `breakdownPar`, `applyParModel`). (Migrated from additive seconds → log 2026-07-02, v1.6.127; the player handicap became a multiplicative ratio in the same change, see Handicaps.)
- **Move type, not move count, is the primary signal.** The solver in `boardSolver.js` classifies every deduction into one of five buckets at the three `totalClicks++` sites: `passAMoves` (trivial propagation), `canonicalSubsetMoves` (Pass B subset with larger-constraint size ≤3, 1-1 / 1-2 / 1-1-1 shapes), `genericSubsetMoves` (Pass B, size ≥4), `advancedLogicMoves` (Pass C tank/gauss), `disjunctiveMoves` (Pass C with liar). Invariant: `passA + canonical + generic + advanced + disjunctive + 1 === totalClicks` for solvable boards. The solver still classifies all five, but the **model pools them for identifiability** (2026-06-08 rework): a VIF/sparsity audit showed the four raw move-types are too sparse / small-count to fit separately, so `predictPar` uses two earned tiers, **pattern** (`canonicalSubsetMoves + genericSubsetMoves`, subset deductions) and **search** (`advancedLogicMoves`, tank/gauss enumeration). `passAMoves` is dropped as a model predictor (it's a board-size proxy, VIF 7.4, absorbed into `cellCount`); `disjunctiveMoves` stays unmodeled (dropped 2026-05-04, confounded with `liarCellCount`). The solver keeps counting all five for diagnostics.
- **Stored feature vector (`dailyMeta`):** raw move-type counts + board dimensions (rows, cols, cellCount, totalMines, wallEdgeCount, etc.) + gimmick cell counts, UNCHANGED by the rework, so every historical board stays usable with no migration. The **model predictors are DERIVED from these** at predict/fit time: `cellCount` (lone size axis), `totalMines` (raw, VIF 3.6, fine once the redundant size features are dropped; density was tried 2026-06-08 but made the coefficient unreadable), `patternMoves`, `searchMoves`, `wallEdgeCount`, `zeroClusterCount`, and the modifier cell counts (kept split, sparse, prior-anchored until coverage missions build data). `nonZeroSafeCellCount` is dropped (near-redundant, VIF 23.7). One source of truth: `COEF_TERMS` in `dailyFeatures.js`, where each term is a function of the raw features (so the R refit derives the same from stored `dailyMeta`).
- **End-of-game modal** renders a per-term breakdown below par: `+18s search moves · +12s pattern moves · …`. Under the log model `breakdownPar` allocates the above-baseline seconds by each term's share of the log-difficulty (`coef·x / Σ coef·x`), so the chips still read in seconds and sum to par. Baseline terms (`exp(intercept + size)`) merge into a single chip to keep the line readable.
- **R refit workflow:** export Firebase JSON from the console → run `scripts/fit-par-model.qmd` → paste the emitted `PAR_MODEL = { ... }` block into `difficulty.js` → commit + deploy. The .qmd also runs diagnostics (residual plots, QQ for log-linear check, VIF for collinearity, bootstrap CIs).
- **Pre-ship scale anchor:** `scripts/calibrate-today.mjs` regenerates today's daily board locally and verifies `predictPar` lands inside the observed completion-time range before shipping new coefficients. One-board check, not a fit.
- **The board's shape gets its OWN par equation, composed from the square fit (per-shape equations, 2026-08-01, superseding the 2026-07-23 intercept offsets).** Christopher's ruling: "we might just want different par equations for each shape... priors can be informed by the square tilings." `PAR_MODEL_SHAPES` (refit-owned, between `PAR_MODEL_SHAPES:START/END` markers in difficulty.js) carries one FULL coefficient set per tiling, keyed by the exact `TILING_TYPES` strings; `modelFor(features)` in dailyFeatures.js dispatches, `modeTimed` wins first (quick play is rectangles-only), then `tilingType` selects its shape block, then `PAR_MODEL` for rectangles AND as the silent fallback for an unknown type (the same hazard `buildTiling`'s fallback carries: a typo prices as a rectangle with no error anywhere, so outside-code types must validate against `TILING_TYPES`; the `'6.6.6'` deep-link alias is normalized by `modelFor` and is never a block key). `predictPar` and `breakdownPar`'s default both resolve through it, so a tiling daily's win-modal numbers are automatically its shape's own equation with no caller edits. **The fit stays ONE joint brms model, not six:** the base formula is unchanged, and each shape contributes its 0/1 indicator (the old intercept offset relocated as that shape's intercept deviation) plus one `shape<S>_x_<F>` interaction per base feature, a SIGNED deviation of that feature's log-multiplier from the square's, every column under the same per-column zero-variance gate as `archivePlay`/`wormLoad` (an interaction is nonzero only on its shape's rows, so with no tiling rows the whole layer collapses and the fit is exactly the pre-shape fit). One fit because the player random intercept `(1|uid)` must be estimated jointly, or six per-shape fits would ship six disagreeing handicaps. **"Priors informed by the square" was UPGRADED to "priors informed by the lab" (2026-08-03, his four seeding rulings after the Par Lab prior fit):** the 18 CORE deviations (intercept, per-cell, per-mine × 6 shapes) now center on the Par Lab posteriors at DOUBLED lab width, `normal(lab mean, 2 × lab sd)`, loaded from the frozen `scripts/data/parlab-prior-centers.json` (terms with ≥ `LAB_SEED_MIN_ROWS` (5) lab rows; the n=1 gimmick-by-shape cells stay `normal(0, INTERACTION_PRIOR_SD = 0.5)` per his observations-not-conclusions ruling, and so does any unseeded term). Signed either way, because a lattice can make a feature cheaper, hex composes to a NEGATIVE per-cell coefficient, which is expected and handled (breakdownPar folds baseline-flagged terms into one exp() chip, so no negative chip can render). **Sampling geometry is TWO-PATH (2026-08-01, fixing the same-day incident):** #203's first cut removed the class-wide `lb = 0` blanket from `build_priors` so the signed deviations could share class b, betting positivity on the lognormals' own support plus a custom init, and the first dispatched run was REJECTED (divergent 14/4000 against a gate of 10, 2950/4000 max-treedepth exceedances, min ESS 464) on the SAME data the bounded setup had fit cleanly twelve hours earlier (Rhat 1.007, ESS 1060, 0 divergent). The bound is a TRANSFORM statement and support only a density one: with `lb = 0` Stan samples class b through a log transform, without it the positive-support lognormal is a hard wall at zero in the sampled space (several OLS-seeded medians sit at ~0.001, pressed against it), exactly divergences + treedepth blowup, and the init (`make_positive_init`, now GONE) only patched initialization, never geometry. So the fit selects on ONE boolean where the formula is built: with NO active deviation column (every night until tiling scores exist) it is the flat formula with the blanket restored, bit-identical in specification to pre-#203; with active columns it becomes a brms non-linear split, `bf(log(pure_time) ~ base + dev, nl = TRUE)`, where each nlpar gets its OWN class b (the sanctioned way to bound one group of fixed effects and not another): `base` (intercept, base features, `(1|uid)`) keeps the lognormals under a `nlpar = "base"` blanket, `dev` (`0 +` the active columns) carries the signed normals unbounded. Both paths flow into the same extraction through `flat_fixef`/`flat_ranef_uid`, which strip the nl path's `base_`/`dev_` name prefixes back to the flat names, so the split is a formula-construction detail, not two pipelines. The timed fit keeps its own blanket; the digit fit (no signed terms anywhere) is always flat and gets its blanket back through `build_priors`' flat path. **The ship guard, and the LAB-SEED property (replacing the launch PARITY property on 2026-08-03):** each emitted shape block = final shipped base + deviations, where a LAB-SEEDED core term ships its fitted posterior whenever its column entered the fit (no 20-row wait, the guard exists to stop bare prior medians, and a lab center is 86 boards of designed data) and its lab CENTER when no live rows exist at all (the zero-variance gate keeps the column out of the formula, so there is no posterior to read); UNSEEDED terms keep the full `NEW_FEATURE_DATA_THRESHOLD` (20) earn guard. So the shipped blocks price the lattices from day one, seeded 2026-08-03 by `scripts/seed-par-model-shapes.R` through the nightly's own EMITTER region, daily-config multipliers hex 1.02 / 4.8.8 1.20 / rhombille 1.77 / cairo 2.03 / floret 2.77 / deltoidal 5.14, and rectangles still price byte-identically on `PAR_MODEL`. The contract test now pins block-minus-base against the frozen lab JSON (seeded terms within emission rounding, unseeded coefficients exactly), so difficulty.js and the lab artifact cannot drift apart silently; the lab-priors loader in the refit fails SOFT (missing file → zero-centered deviations) precisely because that test then fails LOUD on the refit's own commit. This only affects tiling boards, which production cannot build while the rotation is dark, `?coastline=`/parLab practice pars on test builds got honest overnight, and nothing else moved. The measured WHY behind per-shape-never-pooled is unchanged: a honeycomb certifies at techniqueLevel 0 on 95-99% of boards and produced ZERO tier-2 boards at any density, while 4.8.8 spans the full range much as a rectangle does (33/40/27 across tiers 0/1/2 at density 0.20), the reasoning tiers carry most of a shape's difficulty on their own, so a whole per-shape model fit independently would ship its priors back looking like a fit, and the deviation parameterization is what lets each shape's equation EXIST without ever being fit from nothing. Rhombille has NO Pass B at all while 62% of deltoidal boards reach tier 2; Pass B's substrate (adjacent-pair neighbor overlap caps at 2 on both Archimedean tilings against 4 on a rectangle, mean shared 2.86 rect / 1.91 4.8.8 / 1.88 hex; mean adjacent valence gap 2.34 on 4.8.8 vs 0.80 rect / 0.54 hex) is the standing evidence the shapes differ from each other as much as from the square, which is why deviations are per-shape, never pooled "tiling" terms. Fitted deviations ride modelHistory rows as the additive `shapeDeviations` field ({mean, sd} per fit term), NEVER `target_candidates` (a deviation is not force-injectable, and the Journal reads `candidates` only, so no Journal change). **The digit frame went rectangles-only in the same change:** at fixed density the digit shares differ BY LATTICE (rhombille's 5plus share 4.4× the 4.8.8's, 15× the hex's), so tiling rows would turn the digit coefficients into part-shape indicators the pairwise decorrelation machinery cannot see; the cost, digit data at half speed under a 50/50 rotation, is accepted until the shape confound gets a design of its own. R-side pricing stays ONE equation mirroring the FIT: `apply_par_model` takes a `shape_devs` list (indicator + interaction terms added to the linear predictor, algebraically identical to applying the composed block to that shape's rows), and the outlier screen / residual fallback recover the SHIPPED deviations by differencing `PAR_MODEL_SHAPES` against `PAR_MODEL` (`parse_par_model_shapes_devs`), so a tiling row is always screened against the equation it actually shipped under.
- **`tilingType` is DERIVED from the board and ABSENT on rectangles.** `computeDailyFeatures` reads `board._tiling.type`; it is not stamped by the caller the way `features.modeTimed` is, because a daily board's features are recomputed independently by every client, by `daily-board-pipeline.mjs`, and by the nightly `verify-canonical-boards.mjs`, a caller-stamped shape could differ between them and a derived one cannot. It must stay ABSENT (never `'rect'`, never `0`) on a rectangular board: the verify sweep's vintage escape hatch is `stored === undefined && recomputed === 0`, so a new key recomputing to a truthy value would hard-fail every historical date. Since 2026-08-01 nothing derives indicator TERMS from it at all, `modelFor` uses it to SELECT the model, so it still never reaches `dailyMeta` as anything but itself. A new tiling type is a new VALUE of an existing key, never a new key, which is why `FEATURES_EPOCH` does not move when one lands and why `SOLVER_DERIVED_FEATURE_KEYS` stays as it is. Pinned in `test/tilingFeatures.test.mjs` + `test/tilingParModelContract.test.mjs`. **The two hand-maintained `sprintf` templates that used to emit `PAR_MODEL` and `PAR_MODEL_TIMED` are GONE**, their slot/arg counts silently drifted twice, each drift shifting every later coefficient one slot. All three blocks (daily, timed, `PAR_MODEL_SHAPES`) are emitted by one generator (`emit_model_block` / `emit_shape_models_block` / `patch_js_markers`, between `# EMITTER:START/END` markers in refit-par-model.R) driven by two registry tables: `COEF_TO_PREDICTOR` (JS key → R predictor, in emission order, the coefficient EXTRACTION iterates it, and emission, per-shape composition and `apply_par_model` all read it, so adding a coefficient is one table entry plus its prior, and a key absent from the table never has a posterior to lose) and `SHAPE_TABLE` (tilingType string → predictor stem, pinned against `TILING_TYPES` by the contract test, which covers all six shapes generically rather than the two its predecessor hand-listed). The contract test asserts the templates stay dead (zero `%.4f`/`%.5f` template slots in the R source, diagnostics messages legitimately keep other widths) and the generator exists; the EMITTER markers are load-bearing because the parity harness extracts and evals exactly that region, verified to reproduce the committed difficulty.js byte-for-byte from parsed current values (idempotence), so a nightly landing identical coefficients rewrites nothing. The R side's `tiling rows:` progress message stays name/value pairs over `SHAPE_TABLE` for the same reason the templates died.

## Par Bands (daily/weekly difficulty distribution)

**Christopher's ruling (2026-08-02): every daily lands at par between 20s and 240s, every weekly between 60s and 360s, each distribution "skewed lightly toward the easy end, occasional hard boards."** Before this, difficulty was incidental, whatever the mission lottery picked (past dailies: p50 79s, 5% above 180s; the weekly hardest-stack-wins argmax shipped p50 121s with a 251s tail). The shape is the **"hard-day coin,"** his pick from a rendered three-way comparison (vs a single scaled Beta and a log-uniform): 85% of days draw an easy target from Beta(2,5), 15% flip hard and draw from Beta(2,2) on the upper span. The ceilings were raised 180→240 / 300→360 in the same conversation with the easy hill **kept anchored where he approved it**, so the bands keep the original geometry as `easyHi`/`hardLo`: daily easy on [20,180] / hard on [108,240], weekly easy on [60,300] / hard on [192,360].

- **One source: `src/logic/parBand.js`** (leaf, imports only seededRandom; in the typecheck set and the sw.js precache, the swPrecache gate catches a statically-imported module missing from ASSETS). Bands, `HARD_DAY_PROB` (0.15), `PAR_KERNEL_SIGMA` (0.35), the target draw, the kernel/clamp, and the weekly `bandedArgmax` all live here, consumed by BOTH sides of both mirror pairs.
- **The draw** (`drawDailyTargetPar`/`drawWeeklyTargetPar`): deterministic from the date string alone (the `resolveDailyShape` pattern, no fetched file to desync on). Namespaces `${date}:parTarget` / `${weekStart}:weeklyParTarget` are distinct BECAUSE a weekStart is itself a Monday date string, sharing one would correlate every Monday daily with its week's weekly. Exactly two rng() calls per draw (regime coin, position). The Beta inverses bisect closed-form polynomial CDFs (integer Beta parameters, no `Math.pow`), bit-identical on every engine; `Math.exp/log` appear only in the kernel, the same functions `predictPar` already uses upstream, so no new cross-engine divergence class.
- **Daily composition** (`selectMissionWinner(entries, dateString, bandCtx)`): candidates carry `par = predictPar(features)` (both `selectDailyRngSeed.js` and `daily-board-pipeline.mjs`, features were already computed; par is one call). Inside the existing lottery, each non-decorrelation score is multiplied by its band weight: 0 outside the band, the log-scale closeness kernel inside (1.42× off keeps ~61%, 2× ~14%, 3× <1%). Composition rules, all pinned in `test/missionWinner.test.mjs`: **decorrelation supremacy is checked on RAW scores** and a decorrelation winner is exempt from the band (its R-derived weight is calibrated against exactly that contest; those rare days chase a residual corner); an **all-zero-score pool draws ∝ band weight** (replaces the old uniform draw, no mission signal, so the band alone settles it); a **par-less pool disengages** to the legacy draw verbatim; an **all-out-of-band pool** falls back to the unclamped kernel (nearest-in-log wins rather than undefined). Every branch consumes exactly ONE rng() from the same `:missionDraw` stream, so banded and pre-band clients stay stream-compatible.
- **Weekly composition**: `selectWeeklyRngSeed.js` computes features + par per candidate and picks via `bandedArgmax`, (gimmick count + advancedLogicMoves×0.01) × band weight, ties to the earlier slot; the 2-4 modifier stack stays forced (measured: average stack 3.0 → 2.6-2.9). **The weekly selection rule is now ONE copy**: `precompute-weekly-board.mjs` calls `selectWeeklyRngSeed` and rebuilds the winner through its existing retry loop, replacing the private contest copy it had (the weekly half of the mirror-pair drift class the daily slot arithmetic already demonstrated; the no-solvable-candidate fallback, bare weekStart seed, is what the selector returns in that case, so the fallback shape is unchanged).
- **Honest limits, measured before shipping** (40 future daily contests + 16 weekly replayed): selection can only pick what generation offers, pools top out ~100-140s most days (≥180s on ~8/40 dates), so a hard target above ~170s usually ships the pool's hardest in-band board; **extra hard-reach candidate slots were offered and deliberately deferred** (his call, 2026-08-02: ship the band, measure the feel first). The daily floor is ~32s (no candidate prices lower), so the [20,32) sliver is structurally empty, the band is a constraint ("land between"), not a coverage target. Mission-win shares shift ≤ ~4 points under the kernel (worm 34%→30%), so the experiment keeps steering which modifier a board studies while the band steers how long it runs.
- **Tilings are banded via CONFIG TABLES, not a candidate contest (Phase 2, 2026-08-03, `src/logic/tilingBandConfigs.js`).** A tiling day is single-candidate (no pool to select across), so the band steers WHICH CONFIG the one candidate is built on: `TILING_BAND_CONFIGS` holds 5-8 generation-proven configs per lattice, and `tilingConfigAttempts(type, date)` draws one by the same closeness kernel against the same `drawDailyTargetPar` target, a date-seeded lottery (`${date}:shapeConfig` namespace, one rng() call, P ∝ `parBandWeight`), lottery-not-argmax for the mission-winner reason (an argmax ships the identical config for every nearby target) PLUS a fit reason: varied cellCount/totalMines across a shape's live rows is what keeps the lab-seeded size/density deviations identifiable (fixed configs made those columns intercept-collinear, the collinearity the Par Lab existed to break). **Entries freeze probe-median FEATURE VECTORS, never par numbers**: pricing runs through `predictPar` at draw time, so a nightly refit that moves a shape's equation re-prices config selection the same night with no re-calibration commit; the cost is the standard code-version-drift residual the canonical architecture absorbs. This retired the fixed `COASTLINE_BOARDS` daily configs (deltoidal's priced ~285s against the 240s ceiling; its banded table tops at ~194s), `COASTLINE_BOARDS` itself is unchanged and still owns the `?coastline=` practice boards. Proof + calibration: `scripts/calibrate-tiling-band-configs.mjs` (not CI; measures candidate grids, and for committed entries requires 12 plain + 9 single-gimmick + 1 stacked probe ALL certified, clean at freeze, worst single generation 827ms, rhombille). Sub-threshold Laves entries set `constructive: true` (→ `generateTilingBoard`'s `forceConstructive` opt-in; sampling is weak exactly below 0.22 there while the constructive placer goes 30/30; one 4.8.8 entry at density 0.219 opts in too). One entry for each shape is `fallback: true`, the in-band generation-failure retry `buildTilingDailyBoard` walks before the rectangle fallback, deterministic everywhere. Reach limits, re-measured after the 2026-08-07 refit: hex 29-216s / 4.8.8 30-186s / floret 43-176s / rhombille 49-111s / deltoidal 65-195s / cairo 54-140s in THREE SIZE RUNGS ONLY (its fitted per-mine deviation cancels the base rate, so density does nothing, mine-count variants at fixed size exist purely to feed the fit); sub-floor and above-ceiling targets ship the nearest entry via the kernel fallback, the same "constraint, not coverage" reading as the rectangular ~32s floor.
  - **Cairo lost its top rung on 2026-08-07 and cannot get one back, for an arithmetic reason worth knowing before anyone tries.** The refit moved its equation and took the two 110-cell entries from ~237s/~220s to 279s/269s, past the ceiling; they were also never provable, being absent from the calibrator's candidate grid. Cairo cells = `2MN - M - N`, and of those totals only **40, 45, 49, 60, 84, 110, 112** are BOTH canonical-storable and legal under `boardFitsPhone`, every value between 84 and 110 is prime (97, 103) or has no factorization with both container dims in [5, 30] (93, 94). Both 110 and 112 price past the ceiling at ANY density, since the per-mine deviation cancels the base rate.
