The 66-cell 10x4 patch is not a way out either: it is the tall ribbon `MIN_WIDTH_USE` was added to reject on that same date, so `boardFitsPhone` refuses it and it can never be committed, the calibrator grid listing it was stale and has been trimmed to match. **Both halves of that trap are the same lesson the grid's own comment states: a committed entry absent from the grid is measured but never proven, and a grid entry the fit rules now refuse is a trap for the next person reading it.** `test/tilingBandConfigs.test.mjs` pins table validity (storability, proven density ranges, floors 0.14 open / 0.18 Laves / 0.23 rhombille, cap 0.29, the rhombille 72-cell cost ceiling), IN-BAND pricing at the shipped equations (the designed refit-drift alarm: an entry drifting out of band reddens CI on the refit's own commit; fix by re-running the calibrator, never by widening the band), draw determinism + goldens, and target tracking vs a uniform-draw baseline (break-tested).
- **Canonical note**: a selection-rule change is the standard code-version-drift class the canonical-board architecture absorbs, boards already precomputed under the old rule keep their canonicals (clients read them verbatim); only a fully-offline fallback client could diverge on those dates, same accepted residual as the 2026-07-30 lottery change.
- **Tests**: `test/parBand.test.mjs` (frozen target goldens, independent polynomial CDF check, regime/namespace/window pins, kernel/clamp/argmax edges, and REGRESSION selection goldens break-tested, unbanded, 2026-09-01 picks trial0/104s where banded picks trial9/86s against a 27s target) + the `test/missionWinner.test.mjs` band block.

## Leaderboard Modal (scope x view)

The leaderboard modal (600px, `leaderboard-modal-content`) is a Daily/Weekly SCOPE pill-toggle crossed with three VIEW tabs (`data-lb-view`): **Scores** (the classic table, top-3 rank coloring, an "N played" count in the date line), **Adjusted** (the DEFAULT view, both scopes, `time / k` where `k` is the player's multiplicative handicap ratio from the SHIPPED handicaps.json, via `rankAdjusted` in `src/logic/leaderboardViews.js`; ranks by fractional performance vs one's own par. Daily ranks today's times; Weekly ranks the week's `bestTime` by the same dimensionless ratio (the fit is daily-anchored, so weekly leans on the ratio generalizing across boards). The HC chip is a RATING vs Greg, `(1−k)×refPar` seconds AND `(1−k)×100`%, positive = FASTER/better (`ratioDisplaySeconds`/`ratioDisplayPercent`). Players not in the fit rank by raw time (`k=1`) with an `unrated` chip; client-side provisional handicaps are self-only and never applied to others), and **Friends** (friend-code block + add-by-code + list with two-tap remove, above a scores table filtered to friends ∪ me via `filterToFriends`). Friends I/O lives in `src/firebase/firebaseFriends.js` (lazy-imported); pure logic in `src/logic/friendCodes.js` + `src/logic/leaderboardViews.js` (node-tested). `fetchOnlineLeaderboard` rows carry `uid` for the Adjusted/Friends views.

## Daily History Chart
- `src/ui/dailyHistoryChart.js`, pure-SVG timeline of the signed-in user's past 30 days of deltas from par (lives on the Stats modal's Daily tab; a play-day column can show several dots, a live daily plus archive replays). The stats tab's percentile-trend chart is toggleable Adjusted/Raw (Adjusted default: each player's best time / their shipped `k` via `rankAdjusted`). No external library. Dots colored green (under par) / gray (even) / red (over).
- Data source: `users/{uid}/dailyHistory/{date} = { time, submittedAt, archive? }` in Firebase, ONLY the raw time is stored. Timeline placement is by the day the run was PLAYED (`attributePlayedDate` in archiveEligibility.js, surfaced as `playedDate` by `fetchUserDailyHistory`): live rows belong to their board date (even when the write flushed late), archive replays to their submittedAt ET day, so a replay never back-dates a dot or pollutes the career/rolling averages, while par/features still join on the BOARD date (`boardDate` in statsRenderer). When a live play and a replay share a day, the live play owns the chart dot. Par and delta are computed at render time against the current `PAR_MODEL` (via `predictPar` on `dailyMeta/{date}` features) combined with the user's current ratio handicap. This keeps older entries automatically in sync with the latest model after each refit, no server-side rewrites needed.
- The delta plotted is against the user's PERSONAL par (`global par × k + bombSeconds`), so a dot below zero means "better than your typical" regardless of how fast you are in absolute terms.

## Handicaps (user-specific par offsets)
- Each user has a handicap as a **MULTIPLICATIVE RATIO `k`**, their typical `time / par` vs Greg's CLEAN par across recent dailies. `k > 1` = typically slower than Greg, `k < 1` = faster/better. `personalPar = par × k + bombSeconds`: clean skill scales with the board (the ratio), the player's typical bomb cost is a SEPARATE additive-seconds term. The RATING shown to players is `(1−k)×refPar` seconds and `(1−k)×100`%, **positive = faster/better than Greg** (opposite sign from the Lab File "your pace" line, which is a par composition `gregPar×(k−1)` that must sum to personal par).
- Stored in `src/logic/handicaps.json`, a static JSON asset committed to the repo. Keyed by Firebase anonymous uid. Not in Firebase, shipping via a static file sidesteps write-permission issues, and handicaps then update the same way everything else does (commit → GitHub Pages redeploy).
- Computed server-side (GitHub-Actions-side) by `scripts/refit-par-model.R` running daily: fits `brm(log(pure_time) ~ features + (1|uid))` where `pure_time` has ALL bomb cost removed BEFORE logging (so there is no bomb regressor). The ratio is `k = exp(recentered random intercept)` (geometric-mean recentering → play-weighted geometric mean of `k` is 1), clamped to a WIDE `[0.1, 10]` sanity bound, partial pooling is the real regularizer; the clamp only rejects garbage, never a well-sampled player. Each player's mean per-play bomb cost is a SEPARATE `bombSeconds`. Falls back to a residuals path (geometric-mean ratio + bombSeconds) when N or n_players is below the fit threshold.
- **`handicaps.json` is `format: "logratio-v1"`**: `handicaps {uid: k}`, `handicapDetails {uid: {k, bombSeconds}}`, top-level `refPar` (median day-of par, for the seconds display), `secPerBombHit`. Static JSON committed to the repo (ships via GitHub Pages redeploy).
- Client reads via `src/logic/handicaps.js`: `loadHandicaps()` fetches once; `getHandicapRatio(uid)` returns `k` (default 1 = neutral/unrated); `ratioDisplaySeconds`/`ratioDisplayPercent` + `getHandicapSeconds`/`getHandicapPercent` give the RATING display; `personalPar(par, uid) = par × k + bombSeconds`; `getHandicapsMeta()` returns `{updatedAt, modelFitN, nPlayers, method, format, refPar, secPerBombHit}`. A dual-reader treats a legacy additive file (no `format` tag) as unrated (`k=1`) so additive seconds are never misread as ratios. Used in the end-of-game modal ("Your par" vs "Greg's Time"), the Adjusted leaderboard, and diagnostics.

## Refit Workflow (.github/workflows/refit-par-model.yml)
- **The push RETRIES on a rebase.** A plain `git push` loses the whole night's fit when a human pushes to main while Stan is sampling, and that is not hypothetical: the 2026-08-03 run fit cleanly (4 files, 315 insertions) and died on `! [rejected] main -> main (fetch first)` because a PR merged mid-run, so the model just did not update that day and the failure read as a modeling problem. The four files the refit commits are refit-OWNED, so rebasing onto whatever landed is always the correct merge.
- **The refit's anchor is the PRECOMPUTE, not a wall-clock hour (moved 2026-08-07, again 2026-08-09).** The cron is `'17 23 * * *'`, 23:17 UTC, 3h43m ahead of `precompute-daily-board`'s 03:00 UTC run. **It moved an hour earlier because the margin had gone to the queue**: measured over the two nights the 00:17 cron ran, it STARTED at 02:28 and 02:24 UTC, a consistent ~2h10m late, finishing ~25 minutes before the precompute rather than the 2h43m intended, and another half hour of delay would have put it AFTER the board it informs, silently. At 23:17 the same delay lands it ~01:30, about 90 minutes clear. `test/poolReprice.test.mjs` pins that the two crons stay 3-12 hours apart so this cannot silently invert again. The original reasoning holds: so a night's board is generated under the model and experiment target fit that same night. The old 13:13 UTC slot put the refit AFTER the precompute it feeds, leaving every board written against a model 13.8h old. **This is NOT a fix for experiment-target drift** and must not be described as one: a board written on day D is still played on D+7 under a target rewritten seven times since, which is inherent to the seven-day horizon (the drift fix is that clients no longer re-derive the board, daily and weekly, same arc). What the move buys is coherent ordering plus his original complaint, that the refit reliably had not run by the time he checked at 10am. Still 17 minutes past and never on the hour, because GitHub queues top-of-hour crons behind everyone else's and the old 14:00 slot fired 60-120 minutes LATE all week (measured 2026-07-30..08-01); 00:17 UTC also sits clear of the Pages congestion window (~15:00-16:30 UTC). **Two consequences to know.** ET days roll at 04:00 UTC, so the fit now runs ~3h43m before the ET day in progress ends and plays from roughly 8pm ET onward land in the NEXT night's fit, a one-cycle delay on the tail of each day, falling uniformly on late-evening play, never a hole. And the `tilingBandConfigs` in-band CI alarm (the designed refit-drift alarm) now reddens ~2.7h before the precompute instead of ~14h, so a bad night has a much shorter window to be caught before boards are written on it. Cron ignores DST, so the anchor is ~7:17pm EDT / ~6:17pm EST the PREVIOUS evening, plus drift; the one-cycle lag therefore starts around 7pm ET rather than 8pm.
- **The refit RE-PRICES THE CHALLENGE POOL (2026-08-09).** Every pool entry's `ppc` is measured at the model of the day, and the ladder's band, the endless admission floor and the validator's acceptance band all match against it, so a refit left the whole pool mislabeled with nothing saying so. `scripts/reprice-challenge-pool.mjs` re-answers each price from `scripts/data/pool-features.json`, which stores the feature vector of the draw that set each entry's median par. **Measuring a spec is expensive and MODEL-INDEPENDENT, generation, certification, strict load-bearing, timing, and only the price is not**, so this costs seconds rather than the ~20 minutes a re-measurement would, and it relabels IN PLACE and never re-selects, so level assignments move only as far as prices genuinely moved. Exact rather than approximate: par is exp() of the linear predictor and so monotone in it, which makes the median-par draw the median-linear-predictor draw. The step is `continue-on-error` and checked afterward, a boundary violation means a re-SEARCH, which is a human's call, and the model fit (which also drives par, the handicaps, the experiment target and the next precompute) must not be lost to it. `src/logic/parModelFingerprint.js` records WHICH equations a capture was taken under, because a PR's checks run against the branch merged into main and the refit lands nightly, so "stale" and "broken" are different states wanting different remedies. **The SEARCH CACHE stores features and a fingerprint too** (`--reprice-cache` re-answers every cached price instantly, seeding itself from the pool store); entries measured before that keep their old price and the emit warns how many it is choosing from. **ADMISSION NEEDS HEADROOM, NEVER A PASSING MEASUREMENT**, the same lesson landed three times, on the generation cap (`ENDLESS_GEN_HEADROOM` 0.35, tightened from 0.75 after entries admitted at ~1000-1500ms measured 2207ms and 2327ms on other seeds; it costs 1.5% of the material), the endless floor (`PPC_FLOOR_MARGIN` 1.03) and the par ceiling (`PAR_CEILING_MARGIN` 0.95).
- Pulls `daily/*` and `dailyMeta/*` from Firebase (both world-readable, no secrets required), fits the Bayesian mixed-effects model via `brms` + Stan, patches `src/logic/difficulty.js` between `PAR_MODEL:START` / `PAR_MODEL:END` markers, and rewrites `src/logic/handicaps.json`. Commits both files under "Christopher Wells" identity and pushes to main, then explicitly dispatches the Pages deploy, GITHUB_TOKEN pushes never fire push-triggered workflows (GitHub's recursion guard), so without the dispatch each refit sat on main undeployed until the next human push (found and fixed 2026-06-10). Also pulls `dailyBoard/*` (world-readable) for the clue-digit derivation below. If that dispatched deploy fails in the Pages congestion window, the retry watchdog (`retry-pages-deploy.yml`, see Deploy under Development) re-dispatches it unattended.
- Guards: `MIN_SCORES_TO_FIT = 30` (total scores required, priors do the regularization so the old `MAX_COEF_DRIFT` clamp was retired), `MIN_PLAYS_FOR_FIT_INCLUSION = 5` (per-user TOTAL-play threshold, only users at/above this contribute to the global PAR_MODEL and receive a handicap entry in handicaps.json; below-threshold users get a provisional handicap computed client-side via `estimateHandicapFromHistory`. Set low because partial pooling shrinks a small-N user's random intercept toward zero and the heavy players anchor the fixed effects, so a light player can't drag the coefficients, the threshold only excludes true one-offs. Bomb-hit plays count toward the threshold now that they're included in the fit via the `bombHits` regressor), `ADAPT_DELTA = 0.99` with `MAX_DIVERGENT_FRAC = 0.25%`, a fit that comes back with Rhat > 1.05, ESS < 400, or more than 0.25% divergent transitions is rejected and the previous `PAR_MODEL` stays.
- Priors: the coefficients are now LOG-multipliers, so the prior CENTERS are OLS-seeded per refit from `lm(log(pure_time) ~ features)` (`compute_log_ols_seeds`), each a `lognormal(log(seed), sigma)` with the wide `PRIOR_SIGMAS` (sigma = 1.0). Variance priors are on the LOG scale (`normal(0,1)` residual, `student_t(3,0,1)` between-user), NOT the old seconds-scale values (which would be absurd on a log response). The seconds-scale `PRIOR_MEANS` at the top of the script are retained as documentation only. (OLS-seeding is dynamic per run; freeze a hardcoded log-scale `PRIOR_MEANS` if a stable non-ratcheting anchor is wanted.)
- After the fit: random intercepts are play-weighted-recentered in LOG space (geometric-mean centering, brms can park the baseline in either the global intercept or the random intercepts; without recentering the handicaps come out shifted by a common constant), then `k = exp(recentered ranef)`, clamped to `[0.1, 10]`. Each player's mean per-play bomb cost rides SEPARATELY as `bombSeconds` (never folded into `k`). The log-intercept is MEDIAN bias-corrected over day-of rows so `mean(log pure-play time) = mean(predicted log-par)`, par = `exp(Xβ)` is the time Greg beats half his runs (bombs live in the handicap, never in par).
- First CI run after a `brms` package cache miss takes ~5 min (Stan + dependencies compile). Subsequent runs hit the cache and finish in ~2-3 min including Stan model compilation.
- **Bomb-hit plays are kept in the fit; ALL bomb cost is subtracted out of `pure_time` BEFORE logging** (new-mechanic base surcharge `bombBaseSum` + legacy at a fixed `LEGACY_BOMB_RATE`/hit ≈ 15s, shipped as `secPerBombHit`), so there is NO bomb regressor under the log model. Each player's per-play bomb cost then rides as the separate additive `bombSeconds` in their handicap. The per-player bomb cost that par excludes is then folded back into THAT player's handicap (see Handicaps), so bombs are a personal handicap component, never board difficulty. Kept-not-filtered replaced an earlier filter-out that dropped ~60% of plays with asymmetric bias (Chris's bomb-hit rate is higher than Kate's). Clients submit per-hit `bombHitEvents: [{ t, row, col, penalty, infoValue }, ...]`. **Brute-force exception:** a run that found most of the board's mines by stepping on them IS dropped from the fit (the `df` filter mirrors the client `isBombHitCheat` gate, see the two-arm rule under Weekly), a probe's time (tiny wall-clock + every mine's info-value) is a garbage data point no matter how it's priced. The historical probes (e.g. the 100%-mine brute-force on 2026-06-15) are excluded the same way without being deleted from the leaderboard. **The bomb accounting needs only the strike COUNT, not the event log**, which is worth knowing before any backfill: `bombBaseSum = Σ(penalty − infoValue)` and `penalty = infoValue + BASE × (1 + RAMP × (i−1))`, so the difference is the ramped base alone and `bombBaseSum(n) = 3n + 0.75n(n−1)` exactly (13.5s at 3 strikes, 21s at 4). R reads it from `bombHitEvents` purely so the pre-2026-06-15 flat-base cohort needs no date split. The consequence is a trap: a row carrying `bombHits > 0` and NO events routes to the legacy path and is charged `LEGACY_BOMB_RATE` (15s/hit), while a row carrying `totalBombPenalty > 0` and no events computes `bombBaseSum = 0` and is charged NOTHING, so **there is no field combination that prices an event-less new-mechanic row correctly**, and one must either carry its events or accept the legacy rate.
- New features added to the fit follow a threshold guard: until ≥20 plays have nonzero values for a given new feature, its coefficient is pinned to 0 in the SHIPPED `PAR_MODEL` even if brms has fit a posterior. This avoids the lognormal-prior median (~prior_mean × 1.65) being shipped as a real coefficient, which would inflate `predictPar` with no data justification. The fit still treats it as a regressor so other coefficients aren't polluted by missing-data variance.
- **Clue-digit studies (2026-07-17, PR D, absorbed from Christopher's `cwells/par-digit-feature` experiment, commit 51d451f).** The arithmetic-load question: beyond board size and the reasoning tiers, does clue MAGNITUDE (more 2s vs 3s) cost time? `clue_digit_counts`/`digit_shares_from_boards` derive the per-board TRUE-adjacency digit counts FROM THE CANONICAL BOARDS at fit time (full historical coverage from day one, every `dailyBoard` back to `DIGIT_ERA_START = 2026-04-27` carries the data, no client instrument needed; before that date the stored board was regenerated by the backfill and does NOT match its `dailyMeta`/played time, so it is the provenance floor). Parameterization per his qmd: NONZERO-DIGIT SHARES with 5+ lumped and 1s the omitted reference (raw counts are density in disguise, mean clue ≈ 8×density, r = 0.94, reproduced locally 0.944, so only the SHAPE at fixed density can carry signal), scaled ×10 so one unit is "one extra clue-in-ten of that digit". The four features are `clueShare2/3/4/5plus`. **They are measured in a SEPARATE canonical-era SECONDARY brms fit** (`digit_df`, reusing the same lognormal + lb=0 + OLS-seed `build_priors`/`compute_log_ols_seeds` machinery, so a digit study reads on the same positive log-multiplier scale as every other study), whose posteriors merge into `target_candidates`, so a digit share can become the experiment target (a journal study) and appear in the parameter table, WITHOUT ever entering `PAR_MODEL`. This is the STRONGEST form of the new-feature ship-guard: the shipped `predictPar` is byte-identical with or without the digit block (verified: `grep clueShare src/logic/difficulty.js` = 0), so a digit coefficient still confounded with density can never move real par. The secondary fit is fully failure-tolerant (any error / bad diagnostics drops the digit candidates for the night and leaves the primary pipeline untouched) and gated on ≥`MIN_SCORES_TO_FIT` canonical-era rows with variance. **Note (flagged for review): on the current log-scale data (211 canonical-era rows) the digit effect is NOT the null Christopher's June seconds-scale read found (LRT p = 0.52, 155 rows), clueShare3 lands ~+30% per unit, tight (CV 0.18); digit VIFs 1.5-3.9.** Promote to a shipped par term only once the density confound is resolved on more players (the same instrument-first discipline as the per-technique predictors above).
- **Gimmick-contribution study (2026-07-30, instrument-first, secondary fit only).** Christopher's hypothesis: an INTEGRAL gimmick and an incidental one price differently, and raw cell counts cannot see it. The instrument is the strip-solve counterfactual the load-bearing filter always computed and discarded, per testable type, `<g>Required` + `<g>ClicksSaved` (no technique-delta, his ruling), plus the locked split `lockedMineCount`/`lockedNumberCount` (what is UNDER a lock is different work: a mine to prove vs a number delayed; `applyLocked` places both). **Plumbing:** emitted by `computeDailyFeatures` ONLY when the caller passes `opts.contributionOpener` (the certified opener), the final daily/weekly boards, both precompute meta writers, and the verify sweep pass it; the candidate-scoring hot loops (selectDailyRngSeed, the pipeline's per-candidate pass) never do, so a candidate vector just lacks the keys and no selection cost is paid. All ten contribution keys are SOLVER_DERIVED (sweep warns, never hard-fails, on drift) but are a distinct sub-class from the RESULT_DRIVEN eight: they derive from their own strip-solves, not the solverResult argument, `RESULT_DRIVEN_FEATURE_KEYS` + `CONTRIBUTION_FEATURE_KEYS` compose `SOLVER_DERIVED_FEATURE_KEYS`, pinned in `test/dailyFeaturesClassification.test.mjs`. The locked split is STRUCTURAL (hard-fail). **History:** dailyMeta is write-once and R cannot run the JS solver, so `scripts/backfill-gimmick-contribution.mjs` (read-only, re-runnable) measures every stored canonical ≥ CANONICAL_ERA_START, dailies AND weeklies, keyed `{weekStart}_weekly_first` to match the score join, into the committed `scripts/data/gimmick-contribution.json`; re-run it once after the precompute horizon rolls over so the boards written pre-feature get rows. **R side:** a fully tryCatch'd secondary fit (the digit-block pattern, including its select-aside join guard) on meta-carried keys coalesced over the backfill file, archive replays excluded (no archivePlay offset in this frame, the digit frame shares that latent gap for when archive pooling opens), columns gated on real variation (nonzero on ≥3 distinct dates, what that drops today is the thin/empty ClicksSaved columns; the wormhole/mirror Required flags PASS the gate at 11/13 and 17/18 boards but are near-collinear with their own pair-count controls, so their posteriors read as a shared effect until a non-required board splits them), with UNBOUNDED normal priors because a contribution effect has no a-priori sign (a big shortcut plausibly refunds time; build_priors' lb=0 would forbid the hypothesis half the study tests). The fit's controls deliberately DROP `lockedCellCount`: the study's split sums to it exactly, and keeping all three makes the design rank-deficient, the locked posteriors would be prior-blend artifacts whose bands never narrow. Posteriors land in the modelHistory row's additive `contribution` field, deliberately NEVER `target_candidates` (not force-injectable; a mission targeting one could never win a day since candidate vectors lack the keys) and NEVER `PAR_MODEL` until promoted through the normal path. Name lockstep across JS/R/backfill is pinned by `test/contributionContract.test.mjs`; the JS agreement with `gradeGimmickContribution` (one strip machinery, receipt and data can never disagree) by `test/contributionFeatures.test.mjs`.
- **Revalidation targeting (2026-07-17, PR D, approved 2026-07-12).** Every `REVALIDATION_INTERVAL = 14` days the primary target becomes the STALEST SETTLED feature (bottom half of the CV ordering, longest since last targeted) instead of the top-CV feature, so a closed finding gets deliberately re-checked. The cadence is anchored to `lastRevalidationDate` in `experimentTarget.json` (robust to missed refits, unlike a modulo on refit count); a MISSING anchor makes the first post-deploy run due, so the mechanism is visible immediately. "Settled" mirrors the journal's RESTING contract (bottom-half CV); the `recentTargets` (last 3) exclusion still holds; a revalidation only fires when a qualifying settled feature exists (else it falls through to the normal CV pick and leaves the clock for the next run). On a revalidation day the `reason` field is stamped EXACTLY `"revalidation"` (the journal fires its revisit story on `meta.reason === 'revalidation'`); normal days keep the descriptive CV sentence.
- `scripts/refit-par-model.R` is the full pipeline; `scripts/fit-par-model.qmd` is the exploratory Quarto notebook (diagnostic plots, VIF, bootstrap CIs) for interactive review.
- **Per-technique predictors: tested 2026-06-16, pooled RETAINED, EXPAND LATER.** `scripts/par-experiment.qmd` (data from `scripts/extract-par-experiment.mjs`, sound `classifyByProof` per-board technique counts, both flag models) fits the named techniques as separate / nested par predictors against the pooled tiers. Result (N=207, after the >30% cheat filter): VIFs all < 2.3 (collinearity is not the issue), the **pooled pattern/search tiers win on AIC**, and the line composites add nothing beyond their atoms (nested LRT p=0.67, confirms "a 1-2-1 is just a pair of 1-2s" at the time level). But it is a POWER null: two players (Chris + Kate) carry 189/207 completions, so per-technique structure is unfittable yet. **Christopher's intent: keep the pooled model shipped, re-run this experiment as the user base grows**, the named techniques live in the Gym (teaching), not the par model, until the data can support them.

## Canonical Daily Board (cross-client board agreement)
Every player on the same ET date plays the same board, period. Three divergence sources are eliminated:

1. **Code-version drift** between clients on different cached SW bundles. Same seed + different code = different board. Closed by storing the resolved board on Firebase, not the seed.
2. **Experiment-target drift** between clients with different cached `experimentTarget.json`. Same code + different target = different `selectDailyRngSeed` winner = different board. Same fix.
3. **Firebase cold-load race** where `loadDailyBoard` returns null because the Firebase SDK hasn't initialized yet, and the client silently falls through to local generation against a stale `experimentTarget.json`, different `:trialN` winner from whatever wrote the canonical, so same date but divergent board. Closed by `runStartupGate` in `main.js`, which renders nothing user-interactive until the SW is current, Firebase is ready, and today's canonical is in memory. Without this gate, a player who lost the cold-load race would silently play a divergent board (this happened to Kate on 2026-05-06 v1.5.30, the trigger for the gate).

Mechanism:
- **`dailyBoard/{date}`** in Firebase Realtime DB stores the fully-resolved board (mine layout, gimmick assignments, displayed numbers, wall edges), write-once at the rules layer.
- **`getLocalDateString()`** is anchored to America/New_York via `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA', { timeZone: 'America/New_York' })`. A player in Tokyo loading at 9am JST sees the puzzle for the previous ET date if it's still before midnight ET.
- **`src/firebase/dailyBoardSync.js`**, `loadDailyBoard(date)` / `saveDailyBoard(date, payload)` / `serializeBoard({ board, rows, cols, ... })` / `deserializeBoard(raw)`. Both load and save now go through `waitForFirebaseReady` (50ms-poll up to 8s) so a cold-load race can't make them silently no-op. The serialiser drops `false`-valued booleans to keep the JSON tight (~30% smaller), and the deserialiser fills them back in from defaults so the consuming code sees a fully-shaped cell object.
- **Startup gate (`runStartupGate` in `main.js`)**, runs before `init`'s routing branch, behind the `#boot-overlay` div in `index.html`. Three steps: (1) `ensureLatestServiceWorker`, `registration.update()` and wait up to 3s for any new SW to activate (controllerchange handler in `<head>` reloads us if it does), (2) wait up to 8s for `firebase.apps.length > 0` and stash the result in `state.firebaseReady`, (3) pre-fetch `loadDailyBoard(today)` into `state.canonicalDailyBoard = { date, raw }`. Practice loads (`?seed=`) skip step 3. Boot overlay only hides at the END of `init` (after routing + first newGame await), so the player never sees a flash of a wrong board.
  - **Everything the boot path awaits must be BOUNDED, and `init().catch` does not help** (2026-08-07, his "it won't load on loading screen" report). The overlay comes down only at the end of `init`, and the `.catch` safety net at the bottom of main.js catches a THROW, a hang is not one, so a promise that never settles strands the app on the loading screen with nothing logged. Two shapes have shipped, both pinned by SOURCE SCAN in `test/bootHang.test.mjs` because neither is reproducible at a cheaper layer (one needs a socket that accepts a read and never answers, the other a browser that refuses storage writes): (1) **Realtime DB's `once('value')` has no timeout of its own**, on a half-open socket (a phone waking from sleep, a captive portal, a dropped wifi handover) it never settles. Every board/progress read was already `Promise.race`d; the gate's completion-reconcile read was the one that was not. (2) **A throw inside a `setTimeout` callback does not reject the promise the callback was meant to resolve**, it escapes as an uncaught error and `resolve` is never called. The live hazard is `sessionStorage`, which throws on WRITE in a storage-restricted context (Safari private browsing, a locked-down PWA container), inside the iOS `reg.waiting` forced-reload block that exists precisely for that platform.
- **Daily entry path** (`gameActions.js` daily branch, `async`): read `state.canonicalDailyBoard` first; if it exists and matches `state.dailySeed`, deserialize verbatim (skip generation, gimmicks, solvability retry). Fall back to a fresh `loadDailyBoard` call only when the cached canonical is for a different date (tab open across midnight) or when the gate's fetch failed. If both miss, the live daily ABORTS rather than local-generating (the divergence block below), which leaves the old fire-and-forget `saveDailyBoard` fallback write unreachable on the live lane; only `?seed=` practice still runs the local-generation flow, and practice never writes the shared bucket. The solver still runs locally on the resolved board to compute features (move-type counts) and the best-start cell, that's deterministic, so different clients agree on features even if they came in via different paths.
- **Completion ↔ cloud reconciliation** in `runStartupGate` (one TIMEOUT-RACED `daily/{today}` read, both directions; row matching via the pure `src/logic/scoreRowMatch.js`, node-tested). **The lock's question is "has this ACCOUNT finished TODAY'S BOARD", which is NOT the streak's "did it play a daily today"**, those came apart on 2026-08-07 when a divergent board could count for the streak while its score was refused, and every symptom below is that conflation. (a) Local "completed" flag set + this account has NOT finished the canonical → unlock the replay. Three signals, cheapest first: the **board seed this device recorded** with the completion; then the account's cloud row being POSITIVELY divergent (truthy `rngSeed` ≠ canonical); then the one-time **vintage unlock**. A missing row alone still never clears (a player with no name set, or one whose submit is queued offline, legitimately has none, clearing on missing-score used to let raced uid lookups unlock replays). (b) Local flag unset + a cloud row matching the canonical's effective seed → ADOPT the completion (`markDailyCompleted(today, canonicalSeed)` + drop any in-progress daily save), so finishing the daily on one device marks it completed on every device sharing that account. The `?mode=daily` deep link gates on `isDailyCompleted` like the Daily card (a notification tap can't relaunch a completed daily). Skipped on the test env and for `?seed=` practice.
  - **The completion RECORDS ITS BOARD** (`minesweeper_daily_completed_seed`, written by `markDailyCompleted(date, seed)` from the same `state.dailyRngSeed || dateStr` expression the score row uses). Before this the only record of which board was finished lived in the cloud score row, and the submit guard (#252) stopped writing that row for **exactly the divergent case the unlock reads**, so the guard silently removed its own remedy: a player who played a divergent board was marked completed, refused at submit, and locked out of the real board for the rest of the day with no signal anywhere that could free them. An ABSENT seed means a completion predating this (UNKNOWN), never "played the canonical".
  - **The unlock has to be STICKY** (`unlockDailyReplay` → `minesweeper_daily_replay_unlocked`), because `applyCloudProgress` re-derives the lock from the cloud's `lastDailyDate` and the progress listener re-applies the cloud on EVERY write under `users/{uid}`, a `lastSeen` beacon is enough. A plain clear survives milliseconds, which means **the documented divergent-replay unlock had been dead well before the submit guard landed**. `applyCloudProgress` now defers to the marker; `markDailyCompleted` and the account-switch reset both retire it. `unlockDailyReplay` also drops the date's cached par/moves/features (they describe the wrong board), so the boot gate and the at-submit unlock cannot disagree about what a replay resets. Pinned in `test/dailyReplayUnlock.test.mjs`.
  - **The VINTAGE unlock is one boot, ever** (`minesweeper_daily_vintage_unlock_done`), for completions recorded before the seed was stored: local record for today with no seed AND no row for this account on today's board → unlock. "No row" is deliberately a weak signal, which is why it is spent once and never becomes a standing rule; anyone who really did play the canonical is re-locked immediately by `adoptCompletion`, whose row match is the precise test. Verified against live production data before shipping.
- **The at-submit unlock** (winLossHandler's `'divergent'` branch): the moment the client LEARNS the board was wrong is the moment to give the real one back, rather than waiting for the next boot. Writes `dailyHistory` (the day counts, his constraint, "I don't want people losing their streak, but I also don't want bad data") and calls `unlockDailyReplay`.
- **Submission dedupe** in `_doSubmitOnlineScore`: before pushing, read the date's rows and skip the push (returning `'duplicate'`, a third outcome alongside `true`/`false`) when this uid already has a row for the SAME effective board seed. Matching is per BOARD, not per uid: rows omit `rngSeed` when it equals the dateString, so the effective seed is reconstructed (`effectiveRowSeed`), practice rows can never block the real daily, and a divergent historical row doesn't block a canonical replay. First completion wins: callers toast "Already on the board from another device" and skip the `dailyHistory` write on `'duplicate'`; the offline retry queue treats `'duplicate'` as resolved (covers the offline-race where another device submits before the queue flushes). Both submit paths (auto in winLossHandler, manual in main.js) anchor the score's date to `state.dailySeed` (the board's date), so a finish at 12:00:01 AM lands on the board's own leaderboard.
- **Resume eligibility** (`src/logic/resumeEligibility.js`, pure + node-tested; consumed by `tryResumeGame` in `src/game/gamePersistence.js`): a persisted save resumes only if (a) its `gameMode` matches the slot it was loaded from, (b) a daily save carries BOTH `dailySeed` and `dailyRngSeed` and the seed equals today's ET date (practice `?seed=` equals its custom seed instead), (c) the save's `dailyRngSeed` matches the canonical board's when one is cached for that date (divergent-canonical drop), (d) a weekly save carries full identity (`weeklySeed`/`weeklyDay`/`weeklyRngSeed`) matching the live ET clock, and its `weeklyRngSeed` matches the cached weekly canonical's when one is stashed (the daily's divergent-canonical drop, extended to the weekly 2026-08-07), (e) cells have `row`/`col` (v1.5.19 corruption check), and (f) any explicit `cellNeighbors` topology on the save still validates (right length, in range, symmetric, a corrupt one would restore a board whose adjacency disagrees with the one it was certified under). Date anchors compare against the CLOCK (`getLocalDateString`/`getWeekStart`/`getWeekDayIndex`), never against live state fields, a session that survives midnight ET carries yesterday's seeds in state. Saves missing their seed identity are REJECTED, not waved through: the pre-fix Daily card handler nulled the live seeds before `switchMode` persisted the outgoing game, and those fingerprint-less snapshots resumed yesterday's board as "today's" daily (found 2026-06-12).
- **Date-rollover discipline (2026-06-12):** daily/weekly identity is derived inside `newGame` from the ET clock (practice keeps its caller-set seed via `isDailyPractice`); the mode-card handlers no longer pre-set identity fields before `switchMode` (mutating identity before the outgoing game is persisted forges or strips the snapshot's fingerprint, the root cause above). A live session that sleeps through midnight (background tab, suspended PWA) is caught by `expireRolledOverGame` in `main.js` on `visibilitychange → visible`: `isLiveGameExpired` (same pure module) checks the live game's date anchor against the clock; on expiry the persisted save is cleared, `state.status = 'expired'` (persist only writes playing/idle, so the dead game can't re-save), and the player lands on the title screen with a toast. Actively-visible play across midnight is deliberately NOT interrupted, finishing keys under the board's own date.
- **codeVersion provenance**: SW posts `{ type: 'codeVersion', value: CACHE_NAME }` to clients on activate, plus replies to `getCodeVersion` requests. Page caches the result in `state.codeVersion` and stamps it onto canonical-board writes (replaces the stale `'v1.5.19'` literal that used to lie about which build wrote a board).
- **On-demand par calc** in `main.js` (leaderboard modal opening before play) calls `loadDailyBoard` directly, also benefits from `waitForFirebaseReady` now.
- **Practice daily** (`?seed=...`) bypasses canonical fetch/write, practice is per-user and shouldn't pollute the shared bucket.
- **Pre-generation** runs nightly at 00:00 UTC via `.github/workflows/precompute-daily-board.yml` (`scripts/precompute-daily-board.mjs`). Anchoring to UTC gives a 4-5h buffer before the daily flips at 00:00 ET. The seed-to-board pipeline is in `scripts/daily-board-pipeline.mjs` (shared with the regeneration tool, ONE copy) and runs the SAME pipeline `selectDailyRngSeed` + `gameActions.js` would: load the experiment target, force-inject the gimmick if applicable, run candidate selection across 10 trial seeds, pick the winner, write board + dailyMeta to Firebase. Idempotent, write-once rules silently no-op repeated writes for the same date. **Script auth is in `scripts/anon-auth-rest.mjs`** (shared by both precomputes + the backfill/bootstrap one-offs): its REST sign-in sends a `Referer: https://gregsweeper.com/` header, the web API key is HTTP-referrer-restricted (2026-07-06) and rejects referer-less requests outright, and `deleteSelf(idToken)` removes the run's anonymous Auth account in a `finally` once the writes land (best-effort, never fails the workflow), so script runs stopped accumulating orphan Auth users (~35/month before). The pre-gate/pre-cleanup backlog is cleared by the one-off `scripts/purge-anon-orphans.mjs` via `purge-anon-orphans.yml` (dispatch-only, dry-run default, five-way filter, see the script header). **`scripts/report-player-progress.mjs`** (+ `report-player-progress.yml`, dispatch-only, READ-ONLY) is the only way to see challenge progression across players: `users/{uid}` is owner-read (`auth.uid === $uid`), so nothing without the service account, not a browser, not the app looking at anyone but you, can read a ladder position, a streak or a molt bank. It prints a per-player table plus the ladder distribution, and its first job is to separate the UID COUNT from the PLAYER COUNT: every visitor gets an anonymous session on boot and a `users/{uid}/lastSeen` beacon after it, so the node holds an entry per BROWSER that ever opened the site (measured 2026-08-05: 145 uid nodes, 4 players), and one human can hold several uids besides. The two ladders are reported SIDE BY SIDE and never merged, the epoch-gated `challenge250.maxCheckpoint` is the live position, the top-level `maxCheckpoint` is the retired 120-level ladder's history that no client reads, because merging them would report a pre-reset climb as a current one, issue #239's resurrection in a reporting tool. Its token carries DATABASE scope only (`scripts/service-account-auth.mjs`, the shared mint extracted the same day rather than becoming an eighteenth hand-rolled copy; the existing eighteen are deliberately unmigrated), so it cannot reach the Auth records, and no `pushSubscription` token can enter the output (test-pinned). `--fixture <json>` renders the whole report offline for debugging, the `send-push.mjs` idiom. Classifiers pinned in `test/playerProgressReport.test.mjs`. **`scripts/merge-player-uids.mjs`** consolidates one human's multiple anonymous uids (pre-account-linking device fragmentation): rewrites leaderboard-row uids in place, moves/merges uid-keyed weekly rows (per-day best on collision), merges the users/{uid} nodes (history/attempts union, checkpoint/best max, the (streak, lastDailyDate, moltDay) trio as one date-anchored snapshot, powerUps per-key max, pinned in `test/mergeUids.test.mjs`), and removes the emptied source node; the source AUTH record is deliberately left for the aged-orphan purge. Dry-run default; service-account auth; first used 2026-07-07 (anemone-guy ×3, MJP+Stickleback, Hieronymus Bosch ×2).
- **Board cache is network-first with cache fallback** (`loadDailyBoard`/`loadWeeklyBoard` + `src/firebase/boardCache.js`): while online, every load fetches the canonical and refreshes the local copy (~2KB); offline, the cached copy keeps daily/weekly playable. The cache is deliberately NOT authoritative, an admin regeneration can replace an unplayed future board, and a blindly cache-first client that had prefetched the old layout would play a divergent board on the day. Server-reachable-and-empty returns null (no resurrection of deleted canonicals); admin deletions must always be followed immediately by a rewrite.
- **`canonicalStringify` normalizes the Firebase round-trip, because signing a value the database discards breaks every signature whose payload includes one (issue #143, closed 2026-07-27).** Realtime DB has no empty node: writing `[]`, `{}` or `null` to a child path DELETES that child. `serializeBoard` sent `activeGimmicks: []` for a gimmick-free daily, so the payload that came back was not the payload that was signed, verification returned false, and `gateCanonicalTrust` rejected a legitimate canonical as tampered, which makes every client fall back to LOCAL generation on that date, reintroducing exactly the cross-client divergence the canonical architecture exists to eliminate, and simultaneously wedges the nightly sweep on a false positive with no working remediation. It went live on `dailyBoard/2026-08-03`, a DECORRELATION-mission board: that mission rolls the natural gimmick lottery rather than force-injecting, and an unforced roll is gimmick-free about half the time, so decorrelation nights raise the rate of gimmick-free dailies sharply. The fix is at the substrate, `canonicalStringify` drops the same values Firebase drops, bottom-up so a container emptied by its own children goes with them, so the signed string is invariant under storage for every field, present and future, instead of each new field having to remember to be omitted-when-empty. `serializeBoard` also omits an empty `activeGimmicks` now (like `wallEdges`/`gatedCert`), keeping the written payload honest about what lands. Two rails: non-finite numbers count as dropped (`JSON.stringify` renders NaN/±Infinity as `null`), and `signCanonicalPayload` THROWS via `unstorableShapeReason` on a shape storage cannot return unchanged, an empty at an ARRAY-ELEMENT position, which RTDB turns into a sparse object rather than a shorter array. The verifier stays TOTAL and never throws, because `gateCanonicalTrust` fails OPEN on an exception and a throw there would TRUST a malformed board. Verified a no-op on production: the signed string is byte-identical on all 166 stored canonicals, so no existing verdict moves.
- **Canonical-board signatures (the #114 trust-model close, 2026-07-05)**: every pipeline-written `dailyBoard`/`weeklyBoard` carries a `sig`, ECDSA P-256 over `canonicalStringify(payload)` (recursively key-sorted JSON, `sig`/`writtenAt` excluded, empty/non-finite values normalized away, see the #143 bullet above), private key ONLY in the `CANONICAL_SIGNING_KEY` Actions secret (precompute daily/weekly + regenerate daily/weekly + bootstrap all sign; each THROWS without the key). Clients verify BEFORE playing via `gateCanonicalTrust` in dailyBoardSync (applied to network AND cached copies in `loadDailyBoard`/`loadWeeklyBoard`; an untrusted canonical is treated as MISSING → local-gen fallback + reportCaughtError, and is never cached). Trust policy in `src/logic/canonicalSignature.js` (`assessCanonicalTrust`, node-tested): key date < `SIGNATURE_EPOCH` (2026-07-06) → grandfathered unsigned; signed → must verify (an invalid sig is affirmative tamper evidence, rejected outright); unsigned post-epoch → trusted ONLY when `writtenAt` (rules-validated `=== now` on a write-once path, so unforgeable) falls inside the board's own play window (the daily's ET date; the weekly's Mon..Sun), exactly the first-client-fallback shape, so convergence on precompute-failure days survives while a pre-poisoned future board (writtenAt before its window) is rejected. Verifier-machinery EXCEPTIONS fail open with a report (a browser quirk must not brick a legit daily; attackers can't trigger exceptions, only clean `false`s). Key rotation is additive: new pair, replace the secret, prepend the SPKI to `PUBLIC_KEYS`, the epoch never moves. The `sig` child is whitelisted in both rules blocks.
- **Nightly integrity sweep** (`.github/workflows/verify-canonical-boards.yml`, 00:30 UTC + dispatch; `scripts/verify-canonical-boards.mjs`, read-only over world-readable paths, no secrets): the DETECTION layer for the #114 poisoned-canonical vector (canonical paths are write-once but anon-writable, and clients play them verbatim). Re-verifies every FUTURE-dated dailyBoard (+ dailyMeta + crux) and every current/future weeklyBoard: clean deserialization, mine count === totalMines, byte-identical recompute of the adjacency + displayed-number layers (liar offsets are stored, so recompute is deterministic), and center certification under the board's own gatedCert contract, the generator's exact acceptance. dailyMeta feature keys must match a recompute EXACTLY unless `computeDailyFeatures` declares them solver-derived, in which case they warn (legit drift when a solver change lands mid-horizon); crux payloads must be SOUND (a provably safe cell follows from their own numbers, and the claimed answer is among them, version-independent). Post-epoch FUTURE canonicals must additionally carry a VALID `sig` (`verifyFutureSignature`, the unsigned fallback shape is written same-day, never ahead, so an unsigned future board is poison or a pipeline that lost its signing key). Any failure exits non-zero, so the failed workflow run is the alarm, days before the board reaches players; remediation (the regenerate workflows) is printed in the log. Tamper classes pinned in `test/verifyCanonicals.test.mjs` + `test/canonicalSignature.test.mjs`.
  - **Structural is the DEFAULT, and an ALLOWLIST is what made two guards silently inert (issue #180, closed 2026-07-27).** The sweep used to hard-fail only the feature names on a hand-written `STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_KEYS` list, which failed twice over in one stroke: two entries named `wormholeCellCount` / `mirrorCellCount` while `computeDailyFeatures` emits the PAIR counts (a player reasons about a pair), and because the comparison loop iterates the RECOMPUTED keys those dead names matched nothing while both real modifier counts fell through to WARN-only on a green workflow. Every structural feature the list just never mentioned was unguarded the same way, `density`, `gimmickTypeCount`, `nonZeroSafeCellCount`, `zeroClusterCount` (a SHIPPED par predictor) and the four `clueShare*`. The list is GONE. `dailyFeatures.js` exports `SOLVER_DERIVED_FEATURE_KEYS`, the producer declaring which of its own outputs depend on the `solverResult` argument, and the sweep imports it and hard-fails everything else, so a structural feature added tomorrow is guarded the moment it exists and the cost of mis-tagging is a loud false alarm rather than a silent hole. The membership is pinned by DIFFERENTIAL, not by eye (`test/dailyFeaturesClassification.test.mjs`): the vector is computed twice over one board with two different solverResults and exactly the tagged keys must move, so a dead name cannot survive and a newly solver-fed key cannot hide.
  - **Inverting that default collides with the precompute HORIZON, which is why `FEATURES_EPOCH` exists.** `dailyMeta/{date}` is write-once and the pipeline writes seven days ahead, so for a week after any new feature key ships, every future board in flight carries a meta that legitimately lacks it. The old value-based hatch (`stored === undefined && recomputed === 0`) covers a PLACEMENT feature like `wormLoad`, which recomputes 0 on a board that does not carry it, but NOT a DERIVED one: the clue shares recompute nonzero on essentially every board, and measured against the live database an absence-hard-fails rule reddens `dailyMeta/2026-07-19..22` on `clueShare2: stored undefined`, printing a remediation that would regenerate four perfectly good boards. So an absent key is vintage when the recompute is 0 **or** when the meta's own rules-validated `writtenAt` predates `FEATURES_EPOCH` in `verify-canonical-boards.mjs`; anything else is lying BY OMISSION and still hard-fails, which is what stops a poisoner zeroing a feature by leaving it out. **Bump `FEATURES_EPOCH` in the same commit as any new feature key**, forgetting reddens the nightly for up to a week on legitimate boards, which is loud and self-announcing, the safe direction to fail. A change to an existing feature's DEFINITION (not its existence) is deliberately NOT excused: the pre-written horizon then carries numbers the current code disagrees with, clients compute par from the STORED features, and the alarm saying so is correct, regenerate, or wait out the horizon.
  - **The sweep is future-dates-only, so running it over HISTORY reported 64 alarms that were all benign**, and that is exactly how a real failure gets lost. Both classes are now named rather than tolerated: (1) `verifyMetaAgainstBoard` takes the date and reports anything before `CANONICAL_ERA_START` (2026-04-27, exported from `archiveEligibility.js` and cross-checked against the refit's `DIGIT_ERA_START`) as PROVENANCE, before canonical storage existed the board was regenerated by `backfill-old-dailies.mjs` while the meta still describes the per-device board actually played, so the two describe different boards by construction. The META is the record that matters there: score rows join to it, so the par fit reads a consistent meta+score pair and only the canonical is the imposter. Omitting the date keeps the old strict comparison, so this is opt-in, not a global relaxation. (2) The 15 `dailyBoard` failures were all ONE defect, see the repair below.
- **Stale mine adjacency (`scripts/repair-mine-adjacency.mjs` + `repair-mine-adjacency.yml`, dispatch-only, dry-run default).** The invariant is "a mine carries no number, so its `adjacentMines` is ALWAYS 0". The old `calculateAdjacency` SKIPPED mine cells, so a cell `swapMines` promoted from safe to mine KEPT the count it held while safe, and that value serialized into the canonical. **15 dailyBoard dates carry 43 such cells** (0 weekly); every single inconsistency is on a mine and every one is `stored ≠ 0 → recomputed 0`. The producer was fixed 2026-07-10 (one adjacency primitive), so no board written since can carry it, this is backlog cleanup that becomes a no-op once run. **Provably unobservable**, which is why it is safe on played boards: `recomputeDisplayedMines` writes no number onto a mine, and `clueShares` / `nonZeroSafeCellCount` / `zeroClusterCount` all skip mine cells, so measured across all 15 the repair changes zero displayed numbers, zero features, zero par, and no certification verdict, and poisoning every mine with 99 then recomputing still moves 0 displayed numbers. The script re-proves that per board (`proveRepairInert`) and REFUSES any board that fails the proof or carries a `sig` (all 15 predate `SIGNATURE_EPOCH` and none is signed, so re-signing is not needed). Pinned in `test/mineAdjacencyRepair.test.mjs`. **RAN 2026-07-27** (`-f apply=true`): 15 repaired, 0 refused, and the detector now finds nothing, so re-running it is the intended no-op. Every canonical BOARD sweeps clean afterward (152 daily + 14 weekly, 0 failures); the 8 `dailyMeta` rows that still disagree are the unrelated `zeroClusterCount` definition change documented under Board Topology, not a residue of this repair.
- **Force-regeneration of an UNPLAYED future date**: `scripts/regenerate-daily-board.mjs` via `.github/workflows/regenerate-daily-board.yml` (`-f date=YYYY-MM-DD -f dryRun=true|false`). Service-account auth bypasses write-once; replaces dailyBoard + dailyMeta with a fresh run of the shared pipeline; prints gated AND ungated certification verdicts. Rails: refuses non-future ET dates (`--force-past` to override) and any date with score rows. Built for the 2026-06-14 reveal-gating re-certification. Weekly equivalent: `regenerate-weekly-board.yml` (also wipes leaderboard + attempt markers).
- **One-off bootstraps** (e.g., a date played by a real user before the canonical-board ship): `scripts/bootstrap-daily-board.mjs`, hardcode date + seed + force-injection state, regenerate, write. Used to lock 2026-04-27 to Chris's actual played v1.5.18 board.
- **Audit script**: `scripts/audit-divergent-scores.mjs` reads `daily/*` and `dailyBoard/*` and reports any score whose `rngSeed` mismatches the canonical's for that date. Report mode needs no credentials; DELETION needs the service account and is per-row (`--delete --only <date>/<pushId>`, repeatable) via `audit-divergent-scores.yml`.
- **DIVERGENT SCORES ARE NOW PREVENTED, DETECTED AND REMEDIABLE (2026-08-07).** Two real divergences had sat unnoticed, one for two months, because divergence was only ever checked at BOOT. `startupReconcilePlan` spots a divergent row and unlocks a replay, which is right, but by then the row is written and joined against the wrong feature vector in the par fit. Three layers now:
  - **Prevention at submit.** `_doSubmitOnlineScore` reads `dailyBoard/{date}/rngSeed` beside its existing dedupe read and returns a new `'divergent'` outcome on a mismatch, so the row never reaches the leaderboard. It fails OPEN on a read error, exactly like the dedupe, a flaky read must never eat a real score. **The win path still writes `dailyHistory` on that branch**, which is the whole design: streaks read `users/{uid}/dailyHistory` and leaderboards read `daily/`, so the DAY counts and only the comparison is refused (his constraint: "I don't want people losing their streak, but I also don't want bad data").
  - **A harder startup gate.** One empty read is not proof the canonical is unavailable, and falling through to local generation is not graceful degradation for a daily, it is near-certain divergence, because the canonical is precomputed up to SEVEN DAYS ahead against the experiment target of that moment while the nightly refit rewrites that file underneath it, so a client re-deriving the day's board draws from a differently-sized candidate pool. (That is how a 2026-08-06 player landed on `:trial6` against a `:trial13` canonical: the pool is 16 slots on a decorrelation night and 6 otherwise.) The gate now retries twice with linear backoff INSIDE the `firebaseReady` branch, and only when `navigator.onLine` is not false (trusted negatively only), so an offline boot never pays a retry budget that cannot land; a recently-synced cache still serves the board offline, and with no cache the daily aborts (next bullet). **WHICH failures it retries is the part that had to be got right (issue #255, fixed 2026-08-10).** A canonical read returns null for four reasons and only ONE can improve on a second look, so the loop was spending its budget on the two that provably cannot: a date the server says is EMPTY answers the same way next time, and an UNTRUSTED payload returns the same bytes. `loadDailyBoardResult` / `loadWeeklyBoardResult` are reason-carrying siblings of the plain loaders (which keep their signature, nine callers want a board or nothing), classifying through the shared `canonicalReadReason`, and the pure `src/logic/canonicalRetry.js` owns the decision. **The budget is now WALL-CLOCK and checked BEFORE each attempt**, never raced against one in flight, since truncating a read that was about to land would defeat the retry's whole purpose: that bounds the loop at the budget plus at most one fetch timeout (~5.5s), where the attempt-count version allowed `retries × FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` = 11.5s while its own comment promised "about a second and a half". A cheap failure still gets both attempts, the bound is on time, not tries. `weeklyCanonical.js` names this same ambiguity from the other side and works around it via the WRITE; that workaround is still correct and untouched.
  - **The live daily no longer falls back to local generation at all** (his ruling 2026-08-07, after his own client generated locally and lost the score). `newGame`'s daily branch aborts when no canonical resolves, same rule the archive path has always had, and the right precedent: a board that cannot be verified is not offered. This does NOT require a network (`loadDailyBoard` is network-first with a cache fallback and the boot prefetch pulls a week of boards, so a recently-opened offline client still gets the REAL board); what is gone is INVENTING one. The abort sets `state.status = 'aborted'` and clears the daily slot, because `newGame` has already installed an empty placeholder board stamped with today's identity and status `'idle'`, which `persistGameState` DOES write, and `pagehide`/`beforeunload` call it unconditionally, so without it the failed attempt could be saved and later resumed as a mine-free "today's daily". `revealCell` refuses `'aborted'` for the same reason (the placeholder is on screen for a frame or two before the title screen swaps in, and cascading a mine-free grid would satisfy the win check against a stale `totalMines`). **The residual, stated:** the first-client fallback for `dailyBoard` is now gone for the daily, so a precompute outage lasting longer than the 7-day horizon would leave the daily unplayable rather than divergent. That is the loud failure over the silent one, and the horizon makes a single failed night a non-event.
  - **The WEEKLY was the unguarded twin, closed the same arc (2026-08-07, v1.10.14/15).** Its submit path now runs the daily's check: `_doSubmitWeeklyScore` reads `weeklyBoard/{weekStart}/rngSeed` directly and refuses a mismatch (a week's whole leaderboard is ONE board, so a divergent row is not a slower time on the same puzzle, it is an incomparable time on a different one), and the `{weekStart}_weekly_first` FIT row is checked against the WEEKLY canonical via `canonicalSeedPath`, whose predecessor read `dailyBoard/{weekStart}_weekly_first/rngSeed`, a node that does not exist, so the fit-row guard had silently no-oped on every weekly fit row ever submitted. Both submit paths decide through the pure `src/logic/submitGate.js` (`planScoreSubmission`, behaviorally tested in `test/submitGate.test.mjs`; the decision was extracted because a verdict buried in an async Firebase call is a verdict nobody can test): both reads FAIL OPEN (null is "unavailable", never "mismatch", so an outage cannot silently drop real scores), and `'divergent'` is RESOLVED, never queued, since the board will still be wrong on the next flush. The played seed rides the weekly row on BOTH write shapes, the `update()` map and the first-write `set()` payload; on only one it would be absent for half of all players, and an absent seed reads as "nothing to check". `test/rules-contract.test.mjs` DERIVES the weekly whitelist from the writer, so a new row field cannot silently drop the whole write (the 866683d class). A weekly save whose `weeklyRngSeed` mismatches the cached canonical's is dropped at resume (resumeEligibility), and the startup gate retries the weekly canonical CONCURRENTLY with the daily on the same reason-carrying terms, because the weekly's blast radius is wider: the fallback below can establish the week for every player, and a weekly attempt is one of only seven. **The local-gen fallback SURVIVES BY RULING and is the deliberate asymmetry with the daily** ("a week with no canonical at all is worse than a week whose canonical came from the first player through the door"): the writer stays as the precompute-failure recovery, but the write's ANSWER is now read. A write-once REFUSAL is the server saying a canonical already exists, the one thing a read alone cannot establish (null means both "empty" and "unreachable"), so the client re-reads and PLAYS the settled canonical instead of its own board (`weeklyLocalGenPlan` in `src/logic/weeklyCanonical.js`, node-tested in `test/weeklyCanonical.test.mjs`; a settled payload that will not deserialize is not a reason to drop the playable board already in hand). The residual, stated: a genuinely offline client still plays its deterministic local board, and the submit guard settles the comparison whenever the score finally travels.
  - **Nightly detection.** `verify-canonical-boards.mjs` swept FUTURE dates only; it now also compares every score row since `CANONICAL_ERA_START` against its canonical and exits non-zero, across all THREE row families: `daily/` day-of rows, the `{weekStart}_weekly_first` fit rows (matched to the WEEKLY board via the same `canonicalSeedPath` the submit guard uses), and `weekly/` leaderboard rows, with weekly rows that predate the seed's recording counted and REPORTED as unverifiable rather than assumed clean. The era floor is what makes it an alarm rather than noise: every board before that date was regenerated by `backfill-old-dailies.mjs`, so 91 of the ~94 divergent rows disagree BY CONSTRUCTION. It REPORTS and never deletes, deleting player data on a schedule is fine until a canonical is itself wrong, and a scan that throws is reported as UNKNOWN rather than clean, which was a real trap: the first cut printed the same "no divergent rows" line whether it had scanned or crashed. Pinned in `test/divergenceGuards.test.mjs`; the detector was break-tested (274 rows found when every canonical seed was forced to mismatch, exit 1).

## Daily Archive (replay past dailies, feed the model)
Past dailies are replayable. A "Past dailies" link on the Daily card opens a month calendar (`#archive-modal`, outside `#app`); tapping a date plays that date's canonical board. Archive plays are a day-of daily in every visible way (same board, par, features, Certified chip) but are walled off from anything that would corrupt the day-of ritual or the par fit.

- **A finished board is NOT replayable** (Christopher, 2026-07-18: "I don't want them able to play archive boards they've already played"). A daily is a one-off: once a board is cleared there is nothing left to find on it, and a replay recorded nothing anyway (`archiveSubmitPlan` is first-completion-only), so it was playable but pointless. `archiveDayState(date, today, completed)` returns `'playable' | 'done' | 'today' | 'unavailable'` and is the single source for the decision; the calendar paints `'done'` as a NON-INTERACTIVE span carrying the ✓ and an "already played" label, so a completed month still reads as a completion map (his call) without inviting a tap. The click handler RE-DERIVES the state rather than trusting the rendered class, because the grid is painted once per month view while the completed-set refreshes on open. **`completed == null` means UNKNOWN, not empty, and fails OPEN**: a signed-out player or a failed `loadDailyHistory` read cannot be told what they have finished, and blocking the whole calendar on a flaky read is worse than letting a replay through, the data are protected regardless, since `archiveSubmitPlan` fails CLOSED on unknown history and records nothing. The calendar's completed-set and the Board Heatmap's spoiler gate both derive from the same `users/{uid}/dailyHistory` node (via `loadDailyHistory` and `fetchUserDailyHistory` respectively), so the two surfaces cannot disagree about what you have played.
- **Two pure gates** (`src/logic/archiveEligibility.js`, node-tested in `test/archiveEligibility.test.mjs`): `isArchivableDate(date, today)` offers `FIRST_ARCHIVE_DATE` (2026-03-06, the app launch) through yesterday (today is the live Daily's job; canonical storage only began 2026-04-27, so launch..04-26 were regenerated from each date's seed with the current pipeline and backfilled by `scripts/backfill-old-dailies.mjs`, fresh boards stamped with old dates, NOT the unrecoverable per-device originals, and all below `ARCHIVE_FIT_EPOCH` so they never feed the fit); `archiveSubmitPlan(date, hasHistory)` returns `{submitFit, writeHistory}` enforcing first-completion-only (a `dailyHistory` row present → record nothing) and the `ARCHIVE_FIT_EPOCH` (2026-05-07, the dailyHistory ship) below which a date stays playable but never submits a fit row (it could double-count a pre-history live play). `CRUX_VIEWED_KEY_PREFIX` is the shared localStorage key PR 4's `?crux=` route writes and the archive submit reads.
- **Launch** (`launchDailyArchive(date, raw)` in modeManager): the calendar PROBES the canonical first (`loadDailyBoard`; archive has NO local-gen fallback, a missing board isn't offered), then this bypasses `switchMode`'s resume-the-real-daily path and forces a fresh `newGame` with `state.isArchivePlay = true`, the caller-set past `dailySeed`, and the fetched board in `state._archiveRaw`. `newGame` keeps the past seed for archive, reads `_archiveRaw` verbatim, never re-stamps `canonicalDailyBoard` (today's stash), and ABORTS rather than local-gen if the board is somehow missing. `switchMode` clears `isArchivePlay` on every normal mode switch so a resumed real daily can't inherit it.
- **Never persists, and never CLEARS either** (`ownsSaveSlot` in gameState.js, read by both ends): archive shares the daily save slot, so persisting would clobber an in-progress real daily, and a past-date save would fail resume anyway. The outgoing real game is persisted before archive starts, so returning to today's daily resumes intact. Archive is always re-launched from the calendar. The CLEAR end had no such guard until issue #247: `handleWin` ended with a bare `clearGameState(state.gameMode)`, so winning a past daily DELETED the real daily in progress, the player's reveals gone and the board back with a zeroed clock, in the one mode where a manual restart is deliberately impossible (`blocksManualRestart`), which also made it a way to re-clock a board already scouted. The same line hit the weekly slot from a past-weekly replay. `ownsSaveSlot` is the one question both ends ask, false for `isArchivePlay`, `isWeeklyArchive` and `isLevelPractice` (the ?level= / coastline / Par Lab lane, which borrows the challenge slot), and winLossHandler's three end-of-game paths clear through `_clearOwnSave`. Those lanes never persist, so skipping the clear leaves nothing behind. Pinned in `test/saveSlotOwnership.test.mjs`, which guards both ends by source scan because the rule was already right and the defect was a caller that never asked it.
- **Win path** (`winLossHandler`): `isArchivePlay` is excluded from `isRealDaily`, so streak, `saveProgress`, `markDailyCompleted`, the residual cache (`appendDailyResidual`), and the "day streak" suffix are all skipped. Submission routes through `submitArchiveCompletion`: read `dailyHistory[date]`; on a fresh date submit `submitArchiveScore` to `dailyArchive/` (only when `date >= ARCHIVE_FIT_EPOCH`) and write `dailyHistory` (the dedup key + the chart feed); a replay records nothing. Archive uses ONLY the auto-submit path (the manual name form stays hidden, with a defensive guard in main.js so a stale form can't post archive into `daily/`).
- **R refit** (`scripts/refit-par-model.R`): archive rows fetch from `dailyArchive.json`, parse through the shared `parse_score_rows`, bind with an `archive` marker, drop `cruxViewed` rows, and are held OUT of the fit until `ARCHIVE_FIT_THRESHOLD` (20) rows exist (instrument-first, like timed mode and new features). Once pooled, an `archivePlay` offset term (positive-only lognormal prior, since `build_priors` bounds every fixed effect at `lb=0`; a non-negative replay-pace offset is the documented assumption) absorbs the cohort shift and is netted out of the intercept bias-correction. `archivePlay` is FIT-ONLY, never written into `PAR_MODEL` (predictPar stays day-of par), exactly like `legacy_bombs`. Archive rows that collide with a same-`(uid,date)` day-of row are dropped (defends the falls-open dedup).

## Crux Teaser (`?crux=` share route)
A standalone share page showing a PAST daily's crux, the first step plain counting could not reach, as a "find the safe square" mini-puzzle, then a CTA to today's board. The teaser itself writes nothing, but the route is NOT fully write-free: `initAnonymousAuth` and the module-level lastSeen-beacon wiring in main.js run before the `?crux=` early-return, so a prod crux visitor still gets an anonymous session and (after auth settles) a `users/{uid}/lastSeen` stamp, deliberate, since a share-page visitor is a prospective player. Built for public growth (the par model needs sample size). Never spoils the live board: the route refuses today and later, and the default (`?crux=` / `?crux=1`) is yesterday ET.

- **One source of truth** (`src/logic/cruxExtract.js`, node-tested in `test/cruxExtract.test.mjs`): `extractCrux(board, rows, cols, fr, fc)` finds the crux = the first `isBoardSolvable(..., {trace:true})` entry with `tier >= 1` (the SAME definition the win-receipt confession uses, `winLossHandler._renderWinReceipt` now calls `extractCrux`, so receipt and teaser can never disagree about the square). Default first click is board center, matching the receipt and the feature/par solve. All trace entries are SAFE reveals (mines are flagged, never traced), so the crux is always a safe deduction; the sentences come from `explainDeduction`.
- **Pre-crux snapshot** (`boardSolver.js`, opt-in `{captureCruxState:true}`): captures `sim.slice()` (0 hidden / 1 revealed / 2 flagged) the instant before the first `tier>=1` reveal, the exact state a player faces at the crux. Additive and off in generation hot loops (like `trace`); lets `materializeCrux` skip re-implementing flood/unlock.
- **Materialization** (`materializeCrux`): crops the snapshot to the sources + answer + a widening ring (1→3, capped at 9×9 so the teaser stays phone-sized), rebuilds a plain mini (other gimmicks stripped) with every revealed number RECOMPUTED to count the mines shown so the real layout satisfies it (`findDeducibleFrontier` on a satisfiable system can never prove a false square), then re-verifies the answer is provably safe at `tier >= 1` from the shown numbers alone, widening until it is. **Walls are carried, not stripped**: in-crop wall edges (both endpoints inside the crop) are remapped into the mini so the recompute + re-verify are wall-aware, and they ship in the payload so the teaser DRAWS them, numbers, adjacency, and what the player sees all agree; out-of-crop walls can't affect an in-crop count, so they're dropped. Returns the `cruxes/{date}` payload `{rows, cols, cells:[{r,c,n}], answer:{r,c}, sources:[{r,c}], walls?:[edgeKey], tier, sentence, sentenceSocratic}` (~600 B, max ~1.4 KB) or null. **Coverage ≈ 77% of past canonicals** (measured 37/48); the rest are genuine breather boards (no tier>=1 step) or cruxes whose proving region is too wide to crop. `scripts/measure-crux-yield.mjs` reports yield/tiers/sizes.
- **Teaser page** (`src/ui/cruxTeaser.js` + `#crux-teaser` sibling of `#app`): `main.js` init branches on `?crux=` BEFORE the startup gate (resolve date, refuse `>= today` and `< FIRST_ARCHIVE_DATE` → fall back to yesterday, then render and return, no game init, no canonical prefetch). `loadCrux(date)` (dailyBoardSync) fetches the world-readable node (works logged-out). The mini renders revealed clues + fog, plus wall lines via `_renderMiniWalls` (a bar in each walled edge's gap, positioned from the laid-out cells' offsets, `pointer-events:none`). **The frame is "look how much Greg can prove", not "find THE safe square"** (a real position has many provably-safe cells, not one, a single-answer model wrongly bounced the others). The teaser recomputes the FULL deducible frontier ON THE PAGE (`findDeducibleFrontier` over the payload's numbers + walls via `_boardFromPayload`) and leads with the counts ("Greg can prove N squares safe and M mines"); left-tap / Enter OPENS a provably-safe cell (✓, advances a counter), right-click / long-press FLAGS a proven mine (the drawn flag sprite via `spriteImgHTML`, not an emoji), and any gated wrong gesture bounces with a teaching nudge, open only safe, flag only mines, the same control scheme and gating as the Gym. Greg and the flag render as the canonical drawn sprites (`spriteImgHTML('smiley'|'flag')`), so the share page matches the real game's look. A "Show me all" button (or finding every safe) lights up the whole frontier + the crux sentence + the play CTA. Because the frontier is recomputed client-side, this needed NO re-backfill; the payload's `answer`/`sources` fields are now vestigial (the `sentence` is still the featured crux explanation). Viewing sets `localStorage[CRUX_VIEWED_KEY_PREFIX + date] = '1'` (the flag the Daily Archive submit reads to drop a previewed date from the par fit). When there's no crux node, the route solves the canonical client-side (`loadDailyBoard` + `extractCrux`) to distinguish a genuine BREATHER (no tier>=1 step, shows "{date} was a breather…") from a crux that just couldn't be cropped (the rare liar case, keeps the plain "no teaser" line); no Firebase marker needed, and a liar board is never mislabeled a breather. Fog cells use class `crux-fog`, NOT `hidden` (the global `.hidden` is display:none).
- **Writes**: the nightly `precompute-daily-board.mjs` and the admin `regenerate-daily-board.mjs` write `cruxes/{date}` alongside the board + meta (regenerate refreshes/clears it so a stale layout is never described). `scripts/backfill-cruxes.mjs [--dry-run] [date]` populates past canonicals (strictly-past only), now via the `backfill-cruxes.yml` dispatch workflow. **Rules: `cruxes/{date}` is world-readable and `".write": false`, writable by NOBODY (issue #206, closed 2026-08-10).** It was cloned from `dailyMeta` and inherited that node's `auth != null && !data.exists()`, but the family rule is that the anonymous write-once form is granted ONLY where a real first-client fallback writer exists, and this node has none, the same reasoning that put `".write": false` on `boardHeatmap`. Measured live before the fix: **56 past dates carried a canonical board and no crux**, each permanently claimable by anyone who minted an anonymous session (the app does this on boot), on a public path the share route renders verbatim under the GregSweeper masthead; because both legitimate writers ALSO authenticated anonymously, neither could ever replace a node someone else had taken. Coverage is ~77% by design (breather boards, uncroppable cruxes), so that gap is structural and permanent rather than a backfill to run. **The service-account migration is deliberately NARROW**: `scripts/crux-write.mjs` is the one path to the node and mints its own token, while the precompute's `dailyBoard` and `dailyMeta` writes keep their anonymous auth, those have fallback writers and must keep working, so a missing or broken service account costs a teaser and can never cost a daily board. The nightly therefore fails SOFT (`mintCruxToken({ required: false })`, logs and skips) and the backfill fails HARD (`required: true`, cruxes are its only job, so writing zero silently would look like success). A service-account token BYPASSES the rules, so write-once is re-established by hand with an explicit `cruxExists` check; only `regenerate-daily-board.mjs` overwrites, which it does by deleting first, and it already used `access_token` so it is unaffected. Pinned by `test/cruxRules.test.mjs`, which also asserts the three nodes that DO keep an anonymous grant each have a named client writer, the question that should have been asked when this block was cloned.
- **Share card upgrade** (`generateShareCard` daily branch, HARD CEILING 5 lines + URL): title+date · `⏱ {t}s · beat Greg by {Δ}s 🦀` (Greg = par; over-par → "Greg won by") · pace bar · modifier icons + the board's hardest step in plain words (from the certified `state.boardCertificate.tier`, never more than proved) · strikes. The win modal's `#gameover-crux-challenge` ("🦀 Challenge a friend", daily/weekly wins only) copies `?crux=<yesterday>` (hardcoded prod base, like the card; cruxes read from prod even on the test build).

## Board Heatmap ("where the mines went off", Journal PR E)

The Journal's one POPULATION exhibit, and the only Firebase phase of the Journal arc: a past daily board drawn as a grid, each square shaded by how many players detonated a mine on it. `bombHitEvents[].{row,col}` on the score rows is the ONLY per-cell signal a client ever submits (`clickTimeline` never leaves the device; `hintEvents` carry no coordinates), so it is the one thing this can be built from.

- **Pure layer** `src/logic/boardHeatmap.js` (node-tested in `test/heatmapAggregate.test.mjs`) owns every decision that could fabricate a claim: `aggregateBombHits(scoreRows, board)` (the rollup's whole decision layer), `selectHeatmapDate(entries, completedDates)` (the gate), `heatmapCopy(plan, dateLabel)`, `heatLevel`/`maxCellCount`/`cellKey`/`parseCellKey`. The script and the client both consume it, so the map and the map's caption can never disagree.
- **Aggregation rules.** ONE ROW PER PLAYER per date (day-of wins over an archive replay, then earliest timestamp) so one person's two sittings can't read as two people struggling. `n_players` counts SOLVERS, not hitters, so it is the honest denominator for the rate scale. Brute-force probe rows (`isBombHitCheat`, most of the board's mines found by detonation) are dropped ENTIRELY, from both the cell counts and the denominator, that player is messing around, not playing (his ruling, 2026-07-18). Coordinates are bound-checked against the canonical board's `rows`/`cols`.
