- **Shading is a RATE, not a rank (Christopher's ruling, 2026-07-18).** A square's level comes from the SHARE of that board's solvers who detonated it, on the fixed `HEAT_BANDS` scale (≤5% / ≤10% / ≤20% / above), so the same shade means the same thing on every board and the legend can state real percentages. The first implementation scaled against the board's own busiest square, which ranks rather than measures: a board where three people had a bad moment and nobody else did lit up as though it were brutal, because those three WERE the maximum. On a rate scale one hit among thirty reads as the 3% it is, which is why a single hit is safe to draw and why there is no per-square minimum. A square's count IS a distinct-player count (one row per player, and a struck mine stays revealed, so a player can hit a given square at most once), so the share is a genuine proportion of people.
- **The THREE gates (this is the feature's ethic, do not relax).** (1) AUDIENCE: nothing renders until `MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_HEATMAP` (30) distinct players have SOLVED a board. Below that the exhibit does not exist, no map, no waiting copy, no card (his ruling: keep it hidden until we regularly have 10+ players and probably closer to 30). (2) SPOILER: a board is only drawn for a player who has already completed it, the map lights only cells that held mines, and past dailies are replayable through the Daily Archive, so an unplayed board would ship as a partial mine map. Unknown history (signed out / failed fetch) resolves to 'none', never to a guess. (3) ALIGNMENT: the canonical board is REQUIRED, not preferred, if it cannot be fetched, or its shape disagrees with the rolled-up grid, the caption renders and the grid does not. In gates 1 and 2 the pure layer WITHHOLDS the payload rather than merely masking it, so a rendering bug cannot leak a map.
- **Rollup** `scripts/rollup-board-heatmap.mjs` + `.github/workflows/rollup-board-heatmap.yml` (15:30 UTC daily, after the 14:00 refit window, plus `workflow_dispatch` with `dryRun`/`refresh`/`date` inputs, all passed as ENV VARS rather than interpolated into the shell). Only dates strictly before today in ET are rolled up, so a map is built on settled scores. **`boardHeatmap/{date}` is writable by NOBODY at the rules layer** (`".write": false`), the sibling `cruxes`/`dailyBoard` blocks grant `auth != null && !data.exists()` because they have a legitimate first-client fallback writer, but this node has none, and copying that grant would let any anonymous visitor pre-claim a date and permanently publish a fabricated per-cell mine map with a forged `n_players` that sails past the honesty gate (the #114 poisoned-canonical class, with no signature layer and no coverage in the nightly sweep). The service-account script bypasses rules and is the only writer. **Every run RECOMPUTES the trailing `REFRESH_WINDOW_DAYS` (45, matching the client's fetch window)**: a date becomes eligible the moment it is past in ET and the cron runs at 11:30 ET, so a freeze-on-first-write would pin `n_players` at its day-after value and gate 1 could never open on its own. Dates older than the window are skipped unless `--refresh`. A date with no canonical `dailyBoard` is skipped rather than published unanchored.
- **Client** `src/ui/journalHeatmap.js` (lazy-imported by `journalView.js` into a slot after the queue line, so a Firebase outage never holds up the notes) reads the bounded `loadRecentBoardHeatmaps()` in `dailyBoardSync.js`, the player's own `dailyHistory`, and the canonical board for the authoritative grid. In-app only: the logged-out `?report=` page has no play history to check the spoiler gate against.

## Adaptive Experimental Design
- EVERY daily generates 10 candidate seeds (`${dateString}:trial0`..`trial9`), solves each, scores each by its assigned mission, and picks the highest-scoring candidate. ~500-800 ms of CPU on first daily load.
- **Multi-objective per-slot missions.** Slot 0 = the PRIMARY mission, force-injecting the high-CV target's gimmick (chosen by the R refit) and allowed to roll a second gimmick at the natural ~10% rate. Slots 1-9 = COVERAGE missions, each force-injecting a different undersampled gimmick from the ranked `coverage_targets` list (also produced by the refit) and constrained to single-gimmick only via `getDailyGimmick(seed, rng, forcedGimmick, singleOnly=true)`. Slots map ONE-TO-ONE onto the coverage list and do NOT cycle: slot N takes `coverage[N-1]`, and a slot past the end of the list has no mission and is SKIPPED, so the effective candidate count is `1 + coverage.length` (with today's 8-entry list, 9 of the 10 slots are evaluated) unless a decorrelation mission claims the tail (next bullet). Wrapping would hand the top-deficit features double slots and silently halve the sampling rate of everything below them. **Four things are single-sourced in `experimentDesign.js` and must never be re-implemented anywhere**: the slot arithmetic (`resolveMissionForSlot`), the score formula and its cap (`missionCandidateScore` / `COUNT_CAP`), the candidate count (`resolveCandidateCount`), and the payload mission stamp (`missionStamp`). Both the client (`getMissionForSlot` → `selectDailyRngSeed`/`parResolve`) and the Node precompute (`daily-board-pipeline.missionForSlot` / `candidateCountFor`) call them. The slot mapping used to exist as two copies and DRIFTED (the pipeline wrapped, the client did not), so precompute could pick a `:trial8/9` seed the client would never choose and `parResolve` could quote a par for a different board than the date's canonical (fixed 2026-07-18; pinned by `test/missionSlots.test.mjs`); the other three followed it into one place before they could do the same. `TARGET_TO_GIMMICK` likewise now exists once, the pipeline's inline duplicate is gone.
- **Decorrelation missions (PR F1, 2026-07-18).** A third mission type, occupying the slots PAST the coverage list on nights the refit emits one. Primary missions chase a coefficient's uncertainty and coverage missions chase a gimmick's sample gap; neither breaks COLLINEARITY, which is what actually blocks a confounded coefficient, more boards of the same correlated shape cannot separate a feature from its confounder. A decorrelation mission aims at the RESIDUAL instead. The refit emits an optional `decorrelation_mission: { feature, confounder, slope, intercept, residualSd, weight }` (the fitted line between the worst-confounded PAIR it can act on), and the client scores a candidate by the MAGNITUDE of its residual, `|features[feature] − slope × features[confounder] − intercept| / residualSd`, capped at `COUNT_CAP` like every other mission. **Both tails count** (Christopher's ruling, 2026-07-18: "both tails should be sampled"): what reduces the correlation is residual VARIANCE, so a board two SDs BELOW the line is worth exactly as much as one two SDs above. An earlier cut scored a signed residual toward whichever tail the fit judged thinner; that discarded half the reachable boards for no gain in precision, and a one-sided design is also what would bury the inverted-U the clue-ambiguity hypothesis predicts. A board sitting ON the line scores 0 and loses the day to coverage, which is correct. All the statistics stay R-side; the client does one subtraction and one divide, so every player resolves the same board. Deliberately PAIRWISE rather than a multi-term VIF, because the client's scorer takes exactly two features and a mission it cannot act on is worse than none. `getTargetGimmickName` returns null for the target (there is no gimmick to force), so a decorrelation board rolls the NATURAL lottery and SELECTS rather than constructs, which makes candidate count its only reach knob: `resolveCandidateCount` adds `DECORRELATION_SLOTS` (10) past the coverage list on those nights, linear cost, paid only then. **With no mission emitted, selection is byte-identical to the pre-F1 behavior** (`resolveMissionForSlot` with a null/malformed mission returns exactly what it returned before), and `normalizeDecorrelationMission` rejects anything malformed so a corrupt file degrades to an ordinary day rather than a NaN score. **Frequency is a CADENCE, not a weight** (`DECORRELATION_INTERVAL` = 7 days, anchored to `lastDecorrelationDate` like the revalidation clock, advancing only on a night that shipped a mission). A confound bad enough to deserve a mission is bad every night, so a weight tuned to actually win takes the whole calendar: measured at 11 of 12 days, which starves the coverage slate. The weight itself is DERIVED R-side, solving for the residual depth at which a decorrelation board ties the strongest coverage mission, so it recalibrates when the deficits move; a flat guessed weight made the mission lose 12 of 12 days and ship as dead code. Measured reach (`scripts/measure-decorrelation-reach.mjs`, 400 generated boards, seeded and reproducible): the generator's natural confound is r = 0.76 / R² = 0.58 between `clueShare3` and `density`, and its residuals are symmetric (15.3% beyond 1 SD on each side); best-of-19 (a live decorrelation day with an 8-entry coverage list) reaches ≥1 residual SD 100% of the time and ≥2 SD 63%, landing above the line 59% of the time (so both corners really are sampled), and adds spread around the line without dragging either marginal far (3-share +0.46 SD, density +0.17 SD). The high-clue tail is NOT reachable by selection at all (60% of boards have no 5+ clue, median 0), which is the surviving case for F2's constructive placer. Pinned in `test/decorrelationMission.test.mjs`.
- **The clue-digit shares are MEASURED but never MAXIMIZED.** `clueShare2/3/4/5plus` are computed client-side by `clueShares` in `dailyFeatures.js` (a straight port of `clue_digit_counts` / `digit_shares_from_boards` in the R refit, same exclusions: mines carry no clue, mystery hides its number, a plate shows a timer; and since 2026-07-27 the same uncapped digit range on both sides, see Board Topology) so the digit axis is scorable at SELECTION time. The R refit keeps deriving them from the canonical boards for the fit, which is authoritative and covers every date back to `DIGIT_ERA_START`. They are listed in `OBSERVATIONAL_TARGETS` and the COUNT scorer returns 0 for them: every board feeds every digit study, so a "threes study" needs no threes-heavy board, and chasing a high 3-share would actively deepen the confound (3-share runs r ≈ 0.80 with density, so the boards that maximize it are the dense ones we already have too many of). Before the port this held by accident, because `features.clueShare3` was undefined; it is now explicit and regression-tested. The refit's `digit_df` join drops any meta-carried copies of the shares BEFORE joining the board-derived ones (`select(-any_of(DIGIT_FEATURES))`), without that, dplyr suffixes both sides to `.x`/`.y`, `digit_df$clueShare2` becomes NULL, `sd(NULL)` is NA, and the eligibility `if` (which sits OUTSIDE its tryCatch) kills the WHOLE nightly refit, not just the digit block.
- **Scoring, and the winner is a LOTTERY, not an argmax (2026-07-30).** Each candidate's score is `target_count_in_features × deficit_weight`, computed by the shared `missionCandidateScore` in `experimentDesign.js` (which both selection paths call, see the mirror-pair note below), where the target and weight come from `getMissionForSlot(i)`. Slot 0's weight is fixed low (`PRIMARY_WEIGHT = 0.1`). The day's winner is then drawn by `selectMissionWinner`, a weighted lottery over the scored slots (P ∝ score), seeded from the date alone (`${date}:missionDraw`, disjoint from the `:trialN` namespace), so every client and the precompute resolve the same winner. The argmax it replaced was a monoculture machine: deficit weights move slowly (`1/(n_boards+1)`, n growing ~1/day), so the top-deficit mission won EVERY day until saturation, worm took 10 of 12 shipped dates and the whole precomputed horizon (Christopher's report, 2026-07-30), because `min(wormLoad, COUNT_CAP) × 0.143 ≈ 0.71` against every other slot's ~0.36 ceiling. Same lesson as PR F1's weight tuning from the other side: a weight sets who wins a contest, never how often a mission runs, sampling ∝ score IS the frequency mechanism, and it self-corrects nightly as the refit moves the weights. Probed with the live 2026-07-29 spec over 30 days: worm 33%, mystery 20%, locked 13%, the rest 3-10%, primary ~7%, the ~1-in-10 primary intent lands on its own. Two deliberate edges: DECORRELATION slots keep argmax supremacy (top score outright → wins outright; below it → out of the draw entirely, their weight is R-derived to "tie the strongest coverage at depth" under exactly that contest, and their frequency is already owned by the 7-day cadence), and an all-zero pool draws uniformly rather than freezing on slot 0 (∝ band weight since the par bands landed, see Par Bands). Since 2026-08-02 the lottery is additionally PAR-BANDED: each candidate's score is multiplied by its closeness to the day's target par (`parBand.js`), which shifts mission-win shares ≤ ~4 points while ending out-of-band boards. Pinned in `test/missionWinner.test.mjs`, including a grep guard that neither path regrows a private argmax.
- **Force-injection rationale.** Without forcing, the natural per-seed gimmick-inclusion rate of ~6.6% means ~50% of seeds have zero of the targeted feature, making maximization meaningless. Force-injection drops miss rate to 0% so the 10-way contest competes on cell COUNT and DEFICIT WEIGHT instead of mere presence. All four call sites of `getDailyGimmick` (`selectDailyRngSeed`, `gameActions.js`'s play path, the on-demand par calc in `main.js`, and the precompute script) pass the same `(forcedGimmick, singleOnly)` resolved from `getMissionForSeed(seed)` so the chosen seed and the regenerated board match.
- **Primary-target selection.** After each fit, the coefficient with the highest posterior coefficient of variation (SD / |mean|) from a whitelist of push-able features wins, EXCLUDING any feature targeted in the last 3 days (`recentTargets` in `experimentTarget.json`). Stops three liar-heavy dailies in a row while still pushing data toward the most uncertain coefficient. Client fetches via `loadExperimentTarget()` in `src/logic/experimentDesign.js`. **Two additions ride this candidate pool (PR D, 2026-07-17):** (1) the four `clueShare*` digit shares (from the secondary fit, see Refit Workflow) are merged into `target_candidates`, so a digit share can WIN the primary slot and become a journal study; a digit target is OBSERVATIONAL (not a gimmick, so no force-injection, `getTargetGimmickName` returns null and it falls through to the natural lottery; `selectDailyRngSeed` scores it 0 via `OBSERVATIONAL_TARGETS` and a coverage slot wins the actual board, which is correct: every board feeds every digit study, so a "threes study day" needs no threes-maximized board. **That zero used to be accidental**, the shares were just not computed client-side. PR F1 ported the histogram so the decorrelation residual is scorable, so the refusal is now an explicit list; see the Adaptive Experimental Design section). (2) Every `REVALIDATION_INTERVAL = 14` days the primary target is instead the STALEST SETTLED feature (see Refit Workflow's revalidation bullet), stamped `reason: "revalidation"`.
- **Coverage-target selection.** The R refit counts unique-date occurrences of each gimmick feature, drops any gimmick at >= 20 boards (COVERAGE_SATURATION_BOARDS coverage throttle - saturated features still appear via the primary target or the natural lottery) in `df_fit`, computes `deficit_weight = 1 / (count + 1)` per gimmick, excludes the chosen primary target, and emits the result sorted descending by deficit. So `liarCellCount` with 1 board (deficit 0.5) ranks above `compassCellCount` with 5 boards (deficit 0.17). Lives in `experimentTarget.json` as `coverage_targets: [{feature, n_boards, deficit_weight}, ...]`.
- **Double-gimmick rate.** Default `DOUBLE_GIMMICK_PROB = 0.10` (was 0.20). The coverage missions absorb most of the "make boards more varied" goal so we don't need the natural double rate doing as much work, and lower noise = cleaner per-feature deltas. Coverage slots additionally suppress the roll entirely via `singleOnly`.
- **State plumbing.** `state.dailyRngSeed` holds the effective RNG seed (the trial variant on improvement days, plain dateString otherwise). All daily-mode RNG creation in `gameActions.js` routes through this field; `state.dailySeed` remains the plain date for Firebase keys / leaderboard joins / local-storage lookups. The play path uses `getMissionForSeed(state.dailyRngSeed)` to recover which mission won, then force-injects the matching gimmick, without this, a coverage-slot winner would get the primary gimmick force-injected on the play board and we'd ship the wrong layout.
- **Determinism.** Same date + same loaded primary + same coverage_targets list → same per-slot missions → same candidates → same winner across all clients. Cache-version drift on `experimentTarget.json` between clients can desync the chosen seed; Check-for-Updates handles most cases, and the diagnostics modal surfaces the loaded primary target + coverage list so mismatches are inspectable.

## Challenge 250 (the ladder, POOL-DRIVEN since 2026-08-08)

**THE BRAID IS NO LONGER AUTHORED.** Christopher's redesign (2026-08-07/08) SUPERSEDES the block map for L26-250, so `CHALLENGE_250_MAP.md` is no longer the last word: it remains the authority for the opener, the tier anchors, the ceilings and the caps, and its 45-block braid table is history. His design in his own words:
- **L1-25**, "supposed to get them hooked and understanding what the hell minesweeper is." A slow DEDUCTION ramp. Still AUTHORED (`OPENER_BLOCKS` in challenge250.js), because teaching is the one job a draw cannot do.
- **L26-250**, a slow DIFFICULTY ramp over "a decently wide but every increased width" band, with EMERGENT introductions: "when enough boards can contain the gimmick, it should be introduced", "when enough boards can be a different shape, the shape should be introduced", "once a board or gimmick is introduced, it gets played 5 times, then any gimmick/shape can be brought in".
- **L250+**, "all hard boards (~3.5s/move) but also needs to contain lots of diversity in number of cells, mine density, gimmicks, tilings." Difficulty is a FLOOR, not a curve (already non-scaling, PR #270).
- Throughout: ramping difficulty, feels random across every parameter, **never repeats**.

**THE LIBRARY IS THE PLAY PATH for L26-250 (2026-08-11).** A Climb level deals a PRE-GENERATED board from `scripts/data/climb-library/level-NNN.json` (~2,900 boards, 10 per level, 20 from L200, built by `scripts/build-climb-library.mjs` with best-of-28 hardness selection over a 120→420s rising par floor); the drawn braid below survives ONLY as the fallback (fetch failure, corrupt file, empty bin) behind the #285 abort contract, and the L1-25 openers plus the endless zone stay drawn. The pieces:
- **The deal** (`src/game/climbDeal.js`, called from gameActions' challenge branch BEFORE the drawn loop): fetch the level file (relative URL, so `/test/` serves its own copy; bounded 5s per the boot-hang rule; the SW's general handler runtime-caches it, so offline replays work after one visit), pick under **his seen-cycle rule** (`pickFromBin` in `src/logic/climbLibrary.js`: random among unseen, marked seen ON DEAL, all-seen resets the cycle; stored as `minesweeper_climb_seen` in statsStorage; practice never marks, `/test/` shares localStorage with prod), then **re-certify from the stored opener at the point of play** (milliseconds; a tampered or corrupt board degrades to the drawn fallback, never installs). The install is shape-identical to the drawn path's; `challengeBoardSeed` = the stored board's seed so worm traits hatch on the identity `wormLoad` was priced under, and par re-prices through the CLIENT's `predictPar` over the stored features so the pace bar agrees with every other par this client shows.
- **The dealt board IS the level** (the fieldnote-drift rule): `state.challengeSpec` takes the DEALT spec at install and, on resume, its shape from the save's own `tiling` descriptor, the braid's spec for that slot says nothing about the board on screen. Checkpoint-selector intro labels read `LIB_MOD_INTROS`/`LIB_SHAPE_INTROS`, the LIBRARY's schedule (sonar block 6, hex 7, locked 13, the sonar-forward three-cycle vs the braid's derived tables, which now describe only the fallback).
- **`src/logic/climbLibrary.js` is the LEAF home** of the schedule tables + `intakeRules`/`boardAllowedAtLevel` (the one copy of schedule legality; the build/top-up/reprice scripts re-import via build-climb-library.mjs, which cannot itself be browser-imported since it pulls node:fs) + `levelFileUrl` + `pickFromBin`. In the sw.js precache with climbDeal.js.
- **The nightly refit RE-BINS the library** (his ruling 2026-08-11: "boards shouldn't be tossed but put into the correct level bin"): `scripts/reprice-climb-library.mjs` re-prices every board from its own STORED features (seconds; boards store features since the re-bin PR, one backfill re-solved all 2,903 in 5s), in-window boards stay, movers place hardest-first into schedule-legal levels preferring under-minimum bins, the homeless wait in `reserve.json` and re-try every run, and `topup-climb-library.mjs --fill` (bounded 20 min/night) generates what re-binning left short, carrying remainders in `deficits.json`. `test/climbLibrary.test.mjs` is the drift alarm: fingerprint lockstep (a refit landing without the re-bin reddens CI with the remedy named), par-from-own-features inside each window, schedule legality, face caps ≤2, reserve honesty. `--shape <tiling>` on the top-up fills a starved shape (rhombille was on 64/191 eligible levels after the build; 143 appended boards covered all 121 gaps).
- **Test-env determinism**: `?level=N&board=I` (derivation-gated beside `?level=`, practice-lane-only via `state.climbBoardIndex`) deals bin index I exactly, what the debut-venue e2e specs (`modifierCard`, `wallResize`) pin their venues with, reading the committed level files so the venues move with the library. `tests/e2e/climbLibrary.spec.mjs` is the deal journey; `test/climbDeal.test.mjs` call-tests the deal against the real level-030 file with fetch stubbed, including the tampered-payload rejection.

**ONE PROVEN POOL, THREE DRAWS.** `src/logic/challengePool.js` (a LEAF, data only) holds `LADDER_POOL` and `ENDLESS_POOL`, both measured offline by `scripts/search-endless-specs.mjs` through the ONE builder the play path uses and regenerated by `scripts/write-challenge-pool.mjs` between file markers. `src/logic/challengeRules.js` is a second leaf holding the ceilings, caps, the endless floor and the two dedupe keys, split out because the tools that BUILD the pool must read the rules they build to, and challenge250.js assembles the whole ladder at module load and throws on an empty pool, so the search script importing it could never have run before the pool existed. That is a real cycle, not an inconvenience. challenge250.js re-exports every name, so no consumer changed.

**UNIQUENESS IS `specFace`, NEVER `specFingerprint`.** The face is shape + dimensions + mine count + sorted modifier set, what a player can tell apart. The fingerprint additionally separates `gimmickLevel`, `wallSegments` and the deduction caps, which measured on the old authored table reported 130 distinct specs over **109 distinct boards**: a ladder deduped on fingerprints still repeats 21 of them, which is what he hit at L65-70 ("I do not want a duplicate at all"). `gimmickLevel` is not even a stable property of a level, L63 (gl63) and L64 (gl64) both produce mirror intensity 2 across 300 draws, and post-intro dials take a random ±1 boost per draw. The 250-distinct-faces test carries its own non-vacuity check (a face must collapse the dials, and no single shape+modifier family may be big enough to have carried the ladder alone).

**REPRESENTATION IS A CONSTRAINT ON THE SEARCH, not a hope about its output** (his ruling: "I would like to see somewhat equal representation of gimmicks, tilings, etc. I do not want it to default to classic compass boards or something when there is a ton of space to explore here"). The shapes are two orders of magnitude apart on generation cost (4.8.8 ~21ms worst, rhombille ~2371ms), so one budget spent greedily measures whatever certifies cheapest, which is exactly what the old 39-entry pool showed (floret 10, deltoidal 9, against rect 1 and rhombille 1). The sweep is therefore ROUND-ROBIN over strata, one stratum = (shape × modifier set), every stratum getting the same number of VISITS whatever a visit costs it. Three ordering details are load-bearing and each was found by a run coming back lopsided: strata are interleaved SHAPE-MINOR (shape-major, the first smoke run measured 89 specs and every one was a rectangle); modifier sets are REPLICATED BY ARITY and interleaved (84 triples against 36 pairs, 9 singles and 1 plain would spend 65% of the budget on the arity the ladder uses least, and a 90-second run came back with 192 plain boards and none carrying mirror/sonar/compass/worm); and **the EMITTER round-robins (shape × modifier set) too**, because taking a shape's entries in arity order starves the modifiers, the low-price slices fill with plain boards, and all six shapes then debut in blocks 6-11 with the first modifier debut falling to block 24. Stratification the search does is thrown away if the last step does not preserve it.

**THE ASSIGNMENT** (`assignBraid` in challenge250.js) runs once at module load over 225 levels; it must be sequential because "nothing repeats" and "a thing joins the general draw after its debut" are both history-dependent. Each block asks the pool what it can carry at that difficulty over the same widening ladder the per-level pick walks (judging a debut on the strict band while picks may widen is what starved the first cut, introductions stalled and the assignment threw at L56). Shapes and modifiers ALTERNATE by how many of each has debuted, so they interleave instead of arriving in two runs. A debut runs exactly one block, so the lesson starts ON a checkpoint. When nothing new is viable AND the introduced material cannot fill the block, something is introduced anyway: the alternative is repeating a board. `MOD_INTRO_BLOCKS` and `SHAPE_INTRO_BLOCKS` are OUTPUTS now, not inputs, anything that pins a debut position must read them (test/worms.test.mjs and test/modifierCopy.test.mjs both had to). `UNINTRODUCED` reports what the pool could never place; empty is the contract, and a non-empty one is a POOL problem to fix by searching wider, never something to route around in the assignment.

**The band** ramps geometrically from 0.55 to the T12 summit 3.60 s/cell with a half-width widening 0.18 → 0.45. The old authored band was ±9/11%, tighter than the par model's own accuracy, and its only real property was that adjacent tiers did not overlap, the wrong thing to protect, since the ramp lives in the band's CENTER, nobody can feel a 10% par difference, and overlapping bands still climb monotonically on average.

**`BOARD_WIDTH_CAP` is the rectangular width rule** (difficulty.js, held by test/difficulty-and-stats.test.mjs) and the search reads it rather than restating it. The first cut guessed 13 columns on the reasoning that rect has no `boardFitsPhone` rule, true, and beside the point, because the cap exists elsewhere. The search cache is RESUMABLE and so outlives a rule change, which is why legality is re-checked at EMIT as well as at draw: nothing but that filter would have stopped the already-measured 13-column entries from shipping.

**A REFIT RE-PRICES THE WHOLE POOL.** Every entry's `ppc` is a measured median at the par model of the day, and it is what the band matches against and what the validator's acceptance band centers on. Re-run the search after any refit that moves a shape's equation. `scripts/validate-challenge250-specs.mjs` is the 10-seed gate and is NOT in CI, which is how 22 pre-existing failures once sat on main unnoticed, run it by hand.

Older engine facts (the spec tables, the one builder, and the offline validator landed in PR #225; the ENGINE PR replaced the sawtooth): challenge mode now draws every board from `challengeSpecForLevel` through `buildChallenge250Board` at newGame, a FROZEN certified board with the marked Start-here opener, exactly the daily contract. `getDifficultyForLevel`, `GIMMICK_INTROS`, `getRequiredTechnique`, `getMaxZeroCluster`, `getGimmicksForLevel`, and `MAX_LEVEL` (120) are DELETED; `CHALLENGE_MAX_LEVEL` (250) in challenge250.js is the ladder length every consumer reads (main.js checkpoint selector + `?level=` clamp, winLossHandler advance, headerRenderer progress, titleScreen percent). Engine facts:
- **Challenge is a pre-resolved frozen mode now**: `modifiersPreResolved` returns true for 'normal', the first-click branch in gameActions serves TIMED + CHAOS only, and challenge joins the daily/weekly frozen first-click path (timer starts on the first reveal; a first-click mine is a CLASSIC LOSS with lifelines, never a strike, `mineIsStrike` never routes challenge). Death returns to the block start via the existing `getCheckpointForLevel` (CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL 5 == CHALLENGE_BLOCK_SIZE, parity-pinned in test/challenge250.test.mjs so the two copies of the formula can never drift).
- **Every attempt is a fresh draw** (reset button included): the play seed carries per-draw entropy (`challengeBoardSeed(level, redraw, salt)`), so no memorize-through and no cross-player board sharing. The draw's seed persists with the save (`challengeBoardSeed` in gamePersistence) because worm traits key on it (timerManager's hatch identity chain: dailyRngSeed → weeklyRngSeed → challengeBoardSeed → bare level) and `challengePar` rides along for the expected-time surfaces. `state.challengeSpec` re-derives from currentLevel on restore, never persisted.
- **First-encounter modifier popups fire on the frozen path** (full card, unseen only); the compact recap line stays daily/weekly-only, on the ladder a known modifier appears level after level and a recap every level is noise.
- **The Start-here label now renders for challenge** (boardRenderer's suggested-start class + label gates include 'normal'); the checkpoint selector's rows label the intro blocks from `LIB_MOD_INTROS` / `LIB_SHAPE_INTROS` (the LIBRARY's schedule, since 2026-08-11 the one a player experiences; shape rows use the player-facing `tilingLabel` names).
- **`getIntensity`'s ramp anchor is FROZEN at 120** (`INTENSITY_RAMP_MAX_LEVEL`, local to gimmicks.js): spec `gimmickLevel` dials are authored in old-ladder units and the proven T12 specs were measured on that scale, re-anchoring to 250 would silently re-price every authored spec.
- **Share cards read the PLAYED board's dims from live state** (shareActions), the ladder has no level→dims function anymore.
- **THE ENDLESS ZONE (blocks 51+, 2026-08-04).** His ruling: past L250 the ladder is endless and UNBOUNDED above the T12 summit (3.6 s/cell), any certified spec at or past it, mixed board lengths, checkpoints every 5 banked forever, max level as the brag stat, the par ceiling lifted to TEN minutes (`ENDLESS_PAR_CEILING_SECONDS`), the 2-second generation cap unchanged. Built as a **PROVEN POOL plus a deterministic per-level draw**, not as more authored blocks and not as a generator: a level number has no upper bound, so an authored table cannot reach it, and a free-parameter generator would hand a player at level 1,000 a (shape, size, density, stack) combination nobody has ever generated. Same architecture as `TILING_BAND_CONFIGS`, same reason. `ENDLESS_SPECS` in challenge250.js is the pool; `challengeSpecForLevel` dispatches past 250 and `blockStartLevel` no longer clamps (checkpoints land every 5 forever, which is why `users/{uid}/challenge250.maxCheckpoint` carries no upper bound). The escalation is `TIER_PPC[12] × ENDLESS_PPC_GROWTH^block`, clamped to what the pool holds, so the climb reaches the ceiling around L336 and then the hardest material cycles. **The spec is deterministic per level while the BOARD stays per-attempt random**, that split is what keeps "max level" comparable between players and therefore a brag stat at all.
  - **The pool floor is 3.5 s/cell and is NOT the summit the escalation aims at** (his ruling 2026-08-04: drop it so more boards fit). `ENDLESS_PPC_FLOOR` says which boards may enter the pool; the escalation still STARTS at `TIER_PPC[12]` (3.6), so the zone opens exactly where the authored ladder ended and the first endless block is never easier than the crown before it. Petals also took **+1 minute** (660s) on the same call, for the same squeeze that gave Classic and Paving Stones +2: its 72-cell boards price 7.6-8.3 s/cell at ~550s and were the pool's entire top end until the par-headroom rule cut them. Net effect of the two changes: the pool went 36 -> **57 entries** and its ceiling 6.6 -> **8.3 s/cell**, which stretched the climb from 91 to **126 levels** past the crown.
  - **Re-admission unions the PROVEN pool with fresh candidates rather than replacing it.** Rebuilding from search output alone measured a fresh crop of bigger boards the raised ceilings had opened, and those generate SLOWER, Classic's new candidates ran 1.8-3.7s against the 1500ms budget, so Paving Stones came back with zero entries and Classic with two, a worse pool than the one it replaced. Entries already hardened stay valid by construction (a HIGHER ceiling cannot disqualify a board that cleared a lower one, and the floor only moved down), so the union is both safe and necessary.
  - **A REFIT RE-PRICES THE POOL, and the stored prices must be re-measured with it** (2026-08-04, the first time it happened). The 34 recovered weekly rows were the first real data the STACKED-modifier coefficients had ever seen, and the fit moved hard: `secPerMirrorPair` +162%, `secPerWormholePair` +82%, `secPerLockedCell` −38%, `secPerCell` −12%. Every stored `ppc` in the pool had been measured under the old coefficients, so two entries fell below the 3.5 floor and the rest were mislabeled. Re-running `scripts/harden-endless-pool.mjs` over the shipped pool re-measures and re-admits in one pass (57 → 41 entries, ceiling 8.3 → 7.8). **The boards did not change, only the model's opinion of them**, so this is a labeling correction, not a difficulty change. Expect it after any refit that moves a coefficient materially, and re-search rather than merely re-price once the model settles: Classic and 3D Cubes each thinned to a single entry, which is why the block-variety bar is now "at least four distinct shapes" rather than always five.
  - **The ladder validator is NOT in CI** (it times real generation; a full run is minutes). That is why this drift was invisible until it was run by hand. After any refit that moves the equations, run `node scripts/validate-challenge250-specs.mjs`, the authored ladder's tier bands drift the same way the pool's prices do.
  - **Admission needs HEADROOM, not a passing measurement, and that lesson cost four swapped entries.** Both readings that gate a spec vary by seed sample: generation time is heavy-tailed (one rhombille entry measured 1216ms in the search and 9843ms in validation on different seeds) and median par swings about ±30s. So a spec measured AT a boundary lands either side of it depending on which seeds ran, and the pool then fails intermittently with nobody having changed it. `scripts/harden-endless-pool.mjs` is the admission pass: worst-of-N over three salts against `ENDLESS_GEN_BUDGET_MS` (1500ms, a self-imposed margin under his 2000ms cap) and a par bar at 93% of the ceiling, while `validate-challenge250-specs.mjs --endless` re-proves every entry against the REAL rulings. `scripts/search-endless-specs.mjs` is the sweep that finds candidates in the first place.
  - **PER-SHAPE ALLOWANCES got all seven shapes in (his rulings 2026-08-04).** The first pool came back with only four, and the exclusions were all one squeeze between two of his rulings, "unbounded above 3.6 s/cell" and "the ceiling lifts to ten minutes", which for a gently-priced shape intersect in a sliver. He ruled on both halves rather than accepting the loss. **Classic and Paving Stones** price so gently that reaching 3.6 s/cell takes ~150 cells, and 150 cells at that rate IS ten minutes (their boards measured 557-601s against a 600s ceiling), so `ENDLESS_PAR_CEILING_BY_SHAPE` gives each of them **720s**. **3D Cubes** was never a ceiling problem at all, its qualifying boards price 222-464s, comfortably under, but a TIME one: its certifier has no Pass B and leans on Pass C enumeration for every board, so it measured 2.1-9.8s. `ENDLESS_GEN_CAP_BY_SHAPE` gives it **3500ms**. Raising its ceiling would have done nothing, and raising theirs would have done nothing for it; the two allowances are not interchangeable. Both are pinned as applying ONLY where ruled, so an allowance cannot spread by accident.
  - **Every bound needs margin on BOTH sides, and the floor side was learned second.** Par headroom went in after four entries were swapped one at a time against the ceiling. The floor then bit the same way from below: two rect entries stored at 3.69-3.70 (42-draw medians) read 3.56-3.60 on the validator's 5-seed sample and failed the summit floor. The fix is to put each check at the precision its own sample supports, admission owns the tight floor (`PPC_FLOOR_MARGIN`, 3% above 3.6, measured over 42 draws) while the validator's floor check carries a 10% tolerance and leans on the stored-vs-measured drift check, which is the sharper instrument for a moved equation. Verified stable at 5, 8 and 12 seeds.
- **MINE SHIFT MOVES LIKE THE WORM (his ruling 2026-08-04, opening Chaos to the shapes).** `performMineShift` walked the rectangular 8-neighborhood by coordinate, which had never met a lattice because the modifier is Chaos-only and Chaos has always been rectangular. On a tiling `(row±1, col±1)` indexes the CONTAINER (an arbitrary exact factorization of the cell count), so a mine would have "shifted" to a cell it does not touch and usually is nowhere near, with the numbers around both moving for no visible reason and nothing throwing. It now steps through `buildWormCrawlTopology`, the SAME builder the worm crawls on, side-sharing on a tiling and orthogonal on a rectangle, because two crawl rules would be two chances to disagree about what a step is. Note this also changes RECTANGLES: mines used to drift diagonally and no longer do. Two dials moved with it, both his: the interval is **2 to 20 seconds** (was 30-45), and **how many mines move is the difficulty dial** (`getIntensity`, capped at `MINESHIFT_MAX_MOVERS`), where it used to be a flat 1-or-2 forever. Pinned in `test/mineShift.test.mjs`, whose tiling regression carries a CONTROL proving the lattice really does offer non-edge cells to move to wrongly. **The shifter's LIFETIME was the other half, closed the next day (issue #238).** Its cadence lived in a MODULE variable that only `stopTimer` cleared, so it outlived its own board: leaving a Chaos round through the title screen paused the interval but kept the memory, and the next `resumeTimer`, a tab return, an unlocked phone, a dismissed bomb popup, restarted it against whatever game had been loaded since. Reaching another mode needs only the resume branch of `switchMode` (`tryResumeGame` never calls `stopTimer`), so a player with an in-progress Daily who dipped into Chaos and came back had mines relocating inside a CANONICAL board mid-play: the certificate voided, the numbers rewritten, and a submitted score whose stored feature vector describes a board that no longer exists. The cadence now rides GAME state (`state.mineShiftPlan`, cleared by `stopMineShift` and by `tryResumeGame`) and BOTH the restart and the tick ask `mineShiftIsActive(state)`, is this a live Chaos board that actually rolled the modifier, which is how the worm heartbeat has always resumed (`state.worms.length > 0`) and is why worm never had this bug. The 2026-08-04 retune is what made it urgent rather than theoretical: 30-45s became 2-20s and one mover became the difficulty dial.
- **The PROGRESSION RESET (epoch marker).** Everyone replays from L1, no memento of the old 120 climb: `applyChallenge250Reset` (statsStorage, called FIRST in main.js init, one-time via `stats.challengeEpoch === CHALLENGE_250_EPOCH`) wipes maxLevelReached/checkpoints/per-level bestTimes and zeroes the challenge power-up inventory; career counters (wins/losses/streaks/feats) and the first-encounter modifier seen-set survive (popups do NOT re-show). Cross-device, challenge progression syncs through the atomic **`users/{uid}/challenge250` node** (`{epoch, maxCheckpoint, powerUps}`, rules-whitelisted with NO upper bound on maxCheckpoint, the endless zone banks checkpoints past 250, and an upper bound would re-create the 866683d silent-drop class at the boundary): `saveProgress` routes maxCheckpoint + the challenge pool into it (epoch stamped on every write), and `applyCloudProgress` adopts challenge progression ONLY from an epoch-matched node, the legacy top-level `maxCheckpoint` and `powerUps.challenge` fields are pre-reset history by definition (old clients keep writing them; they're whitelisted so those clients' writes don't drop, and ignored so they can't resurrect the climb on either merge path). Non-challenge pools (chaos) still ride the legacy `powerUps` node. The reset deliberately does NOT push to the cloud, the first post-reset checkpoint advance writes the node, which closes the two-device reset race (a reset push of `{1, epoch}` could stomp a faster device's post-reset progress via the overwrite listener). **What the reset did NOT reach, closed 2026-08-05 (issue #239).** It cleared the stats object, the checkpoint and the power-up pool, but the in-progress challenge SAVE is a storage family keyed by MODE rather than by epoch, and the level-advance handler had been writing its checkpoint under a SECOND key (`'normal'`) that only `getModeKey`-style normalization resolves. So a returning player was back at Level 1 while the same `init()` that reset them background-resumed their old-ladder game, and the checkpoint modal offered "Resume Game · Level 100" outright, and finishing that board re-stamped `maxLevelReached` into the epoch-matched cloud node, past the reset's own guard forever. Three parts: (1) `challengeSaveIsCurrent` (resumeEligibility) refuses any challenge save above `maxLevelReached + 1`, which is an INVARIANT of how the ladder advances (one level at a time, every win recorded, the selector only unlocking at or below that, `?level=` never persisting) rather than an epoch check, so it also covers every unstamped save already in the wild, works cross-device, and cannot refuse a real in-progress game; it gates both `isSaveResumable` and the checkpoint modal's Resume button, which used to read the slot raw. (2) `saveCheckpoint`/`loadCheckpoint` normalize through `getModeKey`, so `'normal'` and `'challenge'` are one number again. (3) A third migration marker, `challengeArtifactEpoch`, beside `challengeSeenEpoch` and for the same reason, drops the stale save and the orphaned legacy key, reaching players whose progression reset ALREADY ran. It runs AFTER the progression reset in the same call, because the position it judges a save against is the post-reset one. Pinned in `test/challenge250Reset.test.mjs` + the reworked `test/powerup-sync.test.mjs`.
- **SHAPE INTRO CARDS** (`src/logic/shapeIntro.js` pure + `test/shapeIntro.test.mjs`; his ruling 2026-08-04: "I want a card for them and the symbol can be one repeated pattern of that version… make the minimum repeated unit-ish"). The modifier cards had a first-encounter explainer from the start; the shapes never did, so a player reaching L26 met a hexagonal board with no word about what had changed. The card fires once per shape ever (its own `minesweeper_seen_shapes` set, deliberately NOT the modifier seen-set and NOT suppressed by "skip all modifier explainers", since opting out of modifier cards says nothing about the board's shape), and it shows BEFORE any modifier card on that board because the shape is the frame the modifiers sit inside. **The symbol is REAL geometry**, built from the same `buildTiling` the board uses, one cell plus everything it touches, cropped to its own bounding box, so it is a picture of one cell's NEIGHBORHOOD, which is the one question the card answers, and it can never drift from the lattice (a hand-drawn icon would have gone stale the moment `OCT_CUT` moved). The hub is the HIGHEST-VALENCE cell near the patch center: a no-op on the five uniform lattices, load-bearing on the 4.8.8, whose center cell is an interstitial square and drew a four-petal diamond showing none of the interlock (regression-pinned). Copy is ONE description and no flavor line (his ruling: "only the description is necessary, not the italics flavor"), a second, softer sentence was either restating the first or reaching for a characterization to fill the slot, which is how the Kites card ended up calling itself "rich". It answers "what counts as next to what" and nothing else, and takes no em-dashes (guard-tested).
- **MODIFIER CARDS WORK ON EVERY SHAPE** (2026-08-04). The venue rule debuts SIX of the nine ladder modifiers on a tiling (locked and worm on Honeycomb, wormhole and compass on Octagons, mirror on 3D Cubes, sonar on Paving Stones), and the cards described a rectangle while doing it: sonar promised "a 5×5 area centered on the cell" and debuts at L76 on Paving Stones, where its reading is a depth-2 graph ball on a valence-7 lattice; compass promised "every mine in that direction across the full row or column" and "4 mines to the left in that row" and debuts at L91 on Octagons, which has eight ray directions and no rows. Both were the FIRST thing the player read about the mechanic. Two halves, both fixed:
  - **Prose**: every sentence in `desc` (the permanent Modifiers help reference) and `longDesc` (the card) must now be true on every shape, with the Classic case named as the concrete anchor where a shape genuinely changes the answer, rather than forking into per-shape strings. Sonar is "every mine within two steps", which is EXACT on both readings because the depth-2 closure of the rectangular 8-neighborhood IS the 5×5 block; compass is "a straight line the way it points, out to the edge", with the direction counts stated as four (Classic) and six or eight (the others). Both cards point at the region highlight, which is the honest universal answer to "what does this one count". Every stated count is MEASURED against the shipped geometry by `test/modifierCopy.test.mjs` (the shapeIntro discipline), including a naked-rectangular-claim scan that was break-tested against the old sonar wording.
  - **Diagram**: `src/logic/modifierExample.js` (pure) draws the example on the board's own lattice, and every region in it comes from the mechanic's own helper rather than by hand: the sonar ball from `sonarScanCells`, the compass ray from `computeCompassRay` with an arrow out of `COMPASS_DIRS_BY_TILING`, the wall from a `buildWireframe` edge (which is also WHY a corner-only link can never be walled, since it appears in no wireframe entry), the wormhole partner at the graph distance 3 placement actually uses, and the worm's body along `buildWormCrawlTopology`'s side-only step graph. It renders as SVG polygons plus an absolutely-positioned HTML marker layer, so a mark can carry the real sprite, the italic-underlined liar number, or a worm segment while the polygons stay exact. **`modifierExampleHTML` returns null for a rectangle**, so Classic debuts and the Modifiers help tab keep the authored square markup verbatim; that compatibility half is pinned too. DOM-level coverage in `tests/e2e/modifierCard.spec.mjs` (also break-tested).
- **Expected time is DISPLAYED** (his ruling; `src/logic/expectedTime.js` pure + node-tested, `test/expectedTime.test.mjs`): the level's `personalPar` (Greg's par for the drawn board × the player's handicap ratio) shows on the **pre-level card** (the existing level-info toast, which on the ladder also names the SHAPE and cell count, a tiling container's rows×cols is storage, not geometry) and as a **quiet pace bar** (`#pace-bar`, updated on the timer tick by `updatePaceBar` in headerRenderer) that fills as the clock runs. **`paceState` CLAMPS the fill at 1**, that clamp IS the no-punishment rule ("go go go, but no real punishment"): an overrun has nothing left to show, so a slow solve never becomes a scolding meter. Challenge only (every other mode reports against par at the END, where the comparison belongs), and nothing here gates or records, challenge has no par-fit submission path. Layout note: the row is FLEX with the label beside the track, not an absolutely-positioned label, the first cut clipped the text off on a phone, caught by screenshot, and `tests/e2e/paceBar.spec.mjs` now pins label width + non-clipping alongside the fill/absence behavior.
- **Power-up earns are BANDED BY LEVEL and guaranteed** (his ruling 2026-08-04, after playing to L8 and earning nothing): `powerUpAwardCount(level)` gives **1 through L100, 2 through L250, 3 in the endless zone**, plus a bonus lifeline at `LIFELINE_BONUS_CHANCE` (0.33) on top. This REPLACED a tier-scaled expectation (tier/6 per win) that made the openers a near-dead zone, at T1 it paid about one power-up every six wins, so eight levels of honest play could easily produce zero, which is exactly what happened. **The lesson: an expectation is not a reward; a player experiences the draw, not the mean.** `awardPowerUps` takes a count; the old `LIFELINE_WIN_REWARD_CHANCE` (0.30) is gone. His stated bands leave 101-199 unnamed, over a 250-level ladder plus endless the only self-consistent reading is 1-100 / 101-250 / 251+, which is what ships.
- **The L1-10 RAMP and `maxDeductions`** (his ruling 2026-08-04: "I want the first 10 levels to be significantly easier… when I meant lvl 1 is a few clicks, I meant just a few clicks"). The first authoring read the map's "3 to 5 real deductions" as a FLOOR and sized the openers at 7×7-8×8, which measured **15-39 deductions**, because **a floor cannot make a board short, only stop it being trivial**. The ramp levels therefore carry `maxDeductions`, the builder's new accept-gate dial, and are sized down to the deduction count itself: L1 is a 5×5 with 3 mines capped at 5 deductions (measured median **4**), loosening by roughly two per level to L10's 8×8 (median **16.5**), after which the ordinary floor-only regime resumes. Only the ramp sets a cap; the validator reports the deduction RANGE and median for uncapped opener specs so the sizing stays honest. Pinned in `test/challenge250.test.mjs` (monotone caps, monotone size, no cap past L10).

- **A level is a PAR RATING, not a fixed puzzle**: spec = {par-per-cell tier, shape, modifier set}; every attempt draws a FRESH certified no-guess layout with the marked Start-here opener. 250 levels = 50 blocks of 5 (5 Classic opener blocks, then the 45-block braid; block = checkpoint = survival unit). Tier ladder T1 0.55 → T12 3.60 s/cell; absolute par ceiling 8 minutes; **THE 2-SECOND GENERATION CAP** (no spec ships whose measured worst-case generation exceeds 2s in the validator's own run, as measured, no margin); **STRICT load-bearing on ladder specs** (no relax-to-ship, unlike the daily's budgeted escape). Opener blocks are sized by the deduction floor (`minDeductions: 3` per draw, the map's "3-5 real deductions" reads as the floor plus sizing prose: at the map's own named sizes and T1 par anchors, accepted draws measure 15-39 solver deductions, so 5 cannot be a hard cap). Pressure plates and mineShift never appear on the ladder.
- **`src/logic/challenge250.js`** (leaf, imports nothing), `TIER_PPC`, the 50-block table, `challengeSpecForLevel(level)` (clamps past 250 to the L250 crown until the endless zone lands), `blockStartLevel`, `ppcBandFor` ([0.93, 1.11]× the block's authored target, reproduces prove-t12's [3.35, 4.0] at the summit), `specFingerprint` (validator dedupe). Intro-dip blocks (6/9/12/15/21/38) carry the shape's gentlest proven config as their `ppc`, the map's parenthesized tiers are narrative, the floor configs are the spec. Tiling specs pin `cells` inline (verified against `buildTiling` in the test, so the leaf stays import-free). Structure pinned in `test/challenge250.test.mjs` (venues, reprises, gauntlet orders, 3-stack debut at block 40, dip list, vocabularies).
- **`gimmickLevel` is an INTENSITY DIAL in OLD-LADDER units (11..120), never a C250 ladder position.** `getIntensity`'s ramp is anchored on the old `GIMMICK_DEFS` intro positions and difficulty.js's `MAX_LEVEL` (120), the knob PR #224 threaded into `generateTilingBoard`, and the unit the proven Paving T12 spec (level 115) was measured in. **When the engine PR retires the 120-level ladder, the 120 anchor inside `getIntensity` must NOT move with it**, re-scaling it re-prices every authored spec and the proven T12 specs silently. Specs with rect walls carry an explicit `wallSegments` (the pre-generated wall-aware base, gameActions' pattern) rather than deriving from the dial.
- **`src/logic/challenge250Builder.js`**, `buildChallenge250Board(spec, seed)`, the ONE builder (play path + validator, the buildParLabBoard precedent). Rect path: pre-walls → `generateBoard` → gimmick re-rolls on the same base (25/base, 60 bases) with **constructive mystery** (`placeMysteryConstructive`, extracted verbatim from gameActions into `boardGenerator.js` and shared, random mystery on a dense board burns whole bases and blew the 2s cap at 5-11s) → certify → strict `findDecorativeGimmicks` (empty or reject). Tiling path: `generateTilingBoard` with `loadBearingBudget: Infinity` + the result's **`decorative` stamp** (additive field: `[]` = measured strict, non-empty = named types decorative, `null` = budget expired unmeasured, lets strict callers judge the OUTCOME rather than the code path; existing callers' behavior unchanged, pinned in the test). Result carries `features` + `par` (rngSeed threaded, so worm specs price on the same seed the live hatch must use, the engine PR wires that identity).
- **`scripts/validate-challenge250-specs.mjs`**, times and certifies every authored spec through the builder (dedupe by fingerprint, 10 seeds each, `--blocks`/`--seeds`/`--quick` for iteration): every draw certified+strict, worst gen ≤ 2000ms, median par ≤ 480s, median ppc in band (openers: the deduction floor instead). Re-run after ANY change to the tables, the builder, the generators, or a refit that moves a shape's equation materially (the map's ~20%-drift rule). Wall clock is the one non-deterministic reading, boards themselves are seed-deterministic.
- **Retired with the authored braid**: the summit-block cost findings and the three beat-text reconciliations (blocks 30/33/43/46) described specs that no longer exist. The measurement behind them still holds and is worth knowing before authoring anything by hand again, on a 0.40+ density classic, constructive-mystery placement solves carry 3-4s per-seed tails and sonar-strict strip-solves 3.6-10.8s, both cap-breakers, while walls+locked+liar at 51 mines runs under 0.75s worst.

## Challenge Difficulty Curve (Challenge 250)
The ladder is AUTHORED for L1-25 and DRAWN for L26-250, see the Challenge 250 section above for his 2026-08-08 redesign, the proven pool, the emergent introductions and the widening band. `CHALLENGE_BLOCKS` is now a DERIVED summary (tier, target rate, shape or 'mixed', and a beat line naming the block's introduction) rather than a hand-written table; nothing on the ladder rolls a random secondary modifier, pressure plates and mineShift never appear, and every spec is proven offline by `scripts/validate-challenge250-specs.mjs`. Levels past 250 draw from the endless pool.

## Modifier (Gimmick) System
11 types defined in `src/logic/gimmicks.js`:
- walls (L11), liar (L21), mystery (L31), locked (L41), wormhole (L51), mirror (L61), pressurePlate (L71), sonar (L81), compass (L91), worm (L101), mineShift (chaos-only)
- 10-level intro blocks (intro to intro+9): primary gimmick always present
- Daily-safe subset: mystery, locked, walls, liar, wormhole, mirror, sonar, compass, worm (the bar is "board DATA static and canonical", worm's eggs are static cells; only its render-time overlay moves)
- **First-encounter cards are tracked BY REVISION, not by name** (his ruling 2026-08-04: "anytime a card is changed, it should be marked as not seen"). `minesweeper_seen_gimmicks` stores `{gimmick: revision}` where the revision is an FNV-1a hash DERIVED from the card's own player-visible content (name + desc + longDesc + exampleHtml); `minesweeper_seen_shapes` does the same over the shape card's title, copy and drawn patch, so a builder retune that changes the motif re-shows that card too. Editing a word therefore re-teaches the card on the next board that has it, with nothing to remember to bump, which is the whole point, since a declared version number is one somebody forgets during a copy edit. Why it matters: a player who learned the old sonar card ("a 5×5 area centered on the cell") has NOT seen the card that replaced it, and a correction has to reach exactly the people carrying the wrong idea. The legacy flat-array shape reads as UNSEEN once (an array entry genuinely does not record WHICH version was read) and migrates on the next mark.
- **The Challenge 250 epoch migration ALSO clears the modifier seen-set outright**, under its own `challengeSeenEpoch` marker. The ladder was rebuilt from level 1 for everyone, so walls at L6 and liar at L11 are first encounters again even for a returning player. The first cut of the reset deliberately left the set alone ("popups do NOT re-show") and produced exactly the report that killed that reasoning within a day of shipping: "I'm not getting popups on first wall or first liar." A SEPARATE marker is what reaches players whose progression reset already ran, folding it into the epoch guard would miss them, and bumping the epoch to catch them would wipe the climb they have built since.
- Popup can be disabled via `minesweeper_modifier_popup_disabled`
- **Persistent active-modifier reminder:** the `#active-gimmick-bar` (rendered above the board) shows icons for all currently-active modifiers in daily / weekly / challenge modes. Hidden when no modifiers are active or in chaos (which has its own bar, carrying the static "No guarantees" chip). The ✓ Certified chip does NOT live here, it rides the existing `#game-info-bar` row so gimmick-free boards (timed, most dailies) never pay an extra row for it on phones. `updateActiveGimmickBar()` in `src/ui/headerRenderer.js` updates the bar AND toggles the chip on `state.boardCertificate` (the same call sites settle both: end of `newGame()`, the post-generation stamp for challenge/timed, resume).
- **Liar visual cue:** liar cells use rose-pink background tint + italic + underlined number (CSS in `global.css` `.cell.liar-cell`). The pink background is the primary cue, italic+underline alone disappears at small mobile font sizes. Color avoids conflict with sonar cyan, compass gold, wormhole amber/magenta/green, and mirror blue/purple/green.
- **Per-cell stacking rules** (enforced in `applyGimmicks` via `hasBaseValueGimmick` / `hasDisplayBlockingGimmick`):
  - Base-value gimmicks (wormhole, mirror, sonar, compass) are mutually exclusive with each other, only one number can be displayed per cell.
  - Liar (±1 offset) stacks freely on any base-value gimmick. `recomputeDisplayedMines` computes the base value first, then applies the liar offset.
  - Locked stacks with any base-value gimmick and with liar, it's a temporary gate that reveals whatever the base/liar layers dictate once unlocked.
  - Mystery and pressure plate are fully exclusive (mystery masks the number, plate shows a timer instead of a count).
  - Worm eggs (`isWormEgg`) go only on PLAIN numbered safe cells, no mine, no other gimmick on the cell, so the post-hatch cell shows an ordinary number. `applyWorm` runs LAST in `ORDER` so its candidate filter sees every other gimmick already placed. Hidden eggs render as normal cells (a telegraphed safe-only egg would leak "this cell is safe").
- **Worm Tiles (L101, 2026-07-16):** revealing an egg hatches a worm (2-5 segments) that crawls one orthogonal step onto REVEALED cells at a SET per-worm cadence, `WORM_MOVE_BASE_MS` (1750ms, the old 0.5-3s roll's mean, so average speed and wormLoad are unchanged) × its PACE trait, a per-egg roll in [0.8, 1.3] (`WORM_PACE_MIN/MAX`), so a board has metronome-steady quick and slow worms (1.4s vs ~2.3s per move) that every player sees identically (his set-pace ruling 2026-08-03: "worms moving at a set pace, but their pace can vary as it has before", only the walk DIRECTION stays luck) (self-overlap allowed, spawns coiled on the egg, stays put when boxed in but still spends the move) and burrows after its OWN move budget, `wormLifetimeFor(seedIdentity, r, c)`, a per-EGG roll in [30, 80] (`WORM_LIFETIME_MIN/MAX_MOVES`) seeded like the length, so one daily worm can squat near-forever while another is a short cameo, yet every player sees the identical budgets (Christopher's ruling: per-worm variety, zero cross-player variability). Worms hatch at different times and each counts down alone, they never bury in sync. The step is NOT uniform, two layers: (1) MOMENTUM, with `WORM_PERSIST_PROB` (0.5) the worm first tries to continue its last heading when the cell ahead is walkable (`lastDir`, persisted; a pure random orthogonal walk backtracks so much its net travel grows like √moves, worms paced in place, Christopher's report); (2) otherwise candidates are roulette-weighted by `WORM_NUMBER_AVERSION^adjacentMines` (0.7, his "lightly weighted away from large numbers, toward lower ones"; a 0 beats a 4 about four-to-one, nothing is forbidden, and the worm reads TRUE counts, not liar-displayed values, a side effect is that big-number clue cells are covered less often), with the REVERSE of the heading additionally suppressed to `WORM_BACKTRACK_WEIGHT` (0.05) of its weight, ~5% reversal in a corridor, under 2% in open field, and a dead end still resolves by turning around since the tiny weight is then the whole roulette (his "5% or if it's stuck"). It's a memory tax, not a deduction mechanic: information is DELAYED, never destroyed, so **the solver is entirely worm-blind**, boardSolver never reads `isWormEgg`, certification/receipts are untouched, and that's the whole reason worm is daily-safe. **On a tiling the crawl is SIDE-ONLY (his ruling 2026-08-03, from the challenge-250 interview: "worms shouldn't cross through corners, only through sides like in the classic tiling")**: `buildWormCrawlTopology` (worms.js, the ONE builder timerManager's heartbeat calls) intersects the wireframe's side-sharing pairs with the live `_cellNeighbors`, the wireframe supplies geometry, the live adjacency supplies walls (a severed side stays uncrossable), so a Cubes rhombus offers 4 exits, not the corner-inclusive 10. A proven no-op on 4.8.8/honeycomb (trivalent: zero vertex-only pairs, crawl lists equal adjacency verbatim, test-pinned) and unreachable on rectangles (null topology keeps the dr/dc walk byte-identical). Side lists rebuild from `board._tiling` via the pure `buildTiling` (the renderer's own rebuild pattern; the crawl graph is mechanical, never certificational, so no stored-contract risk) and memoize per board. Pinned in `test/worms.test.mjs` with built-in controls: the lattice must HAVE corner links and the raw adjacency walk must CROSS one, or the gate fails as vacuous; break-tested (bypassing the side filter fails exactly the REGRESSION pin). Anatomy: pure logic in `src/logic/worms.js` (tunable constants; worm LENGTH is deterministic per egg via `createDailyRNG(seedIdentity:worm:r:c)` so every player on a canonical board hatches identical worms, only the walk is luck); hatch trigger is `hatchWormEggs` (timerManager) called from every player reveal batch (reveal cascade, chord, Reveal Safe) AFTER `revealWormholePairs`; the 250ms heartbeat lives beside the mine-shift interval and is torn down by `stopTimer` (which also clears worms, it only runs at true teardown), paused/resumed by `pauseTimer`/`resumeTimer`; segments + movesLeft persist in the auto-save and rehydrate with fresh clocks (`rehydrateWorms`, the plate re-arm precedent); rendering is `src/ui/wormRenderer.js`, a board-anchored sibling overlay (walls pattern) at z-index 4 with element reuse so the CSS left/top transition tweens the crawl, segments center on the cell's VISUAL center via `wormCellCenterUnitOffsets` (worms.js: cellPos minus the polygon bbox center, in pitch units, zero on every centrally symmetric cell, ~0.25P on a floret pentagon and ~0.21P on a deltoidal kite, the same asymmetry `_anchorPadding` corrects for the number; without it the pitch-sized segment centered on the BOX read off-center and oversized on petals/kites, his report 2026-08-03), `pointer-events: none` (covered cells stay clickable/chordable, no special interaction rules), repositioned on worm ticks + window resize + theme refit; each worm carries a seeded per-egg TONE (0..1, `wormToneFor`, cosmetic only, persisted with the worm) that the renderer interpolates between the theme's `--worm-tone-dark`/`--worm-tone-light` endpoint tokens (brown→cream on the base design; shading self-derives from the mixed base via `SHADE_ANCHOR`), mixed in JS (`mixHex`, node-tested) NOT CSS color-mix, a var-dependent color-mix failing on an older engine computes to no background at all, an invisible worm. A theme recolors its whole brood by overriding just the two endpoints (Christopher: theme-based worm families later). Worm is the first **sprite-only modifier**: no `icon` field in its def, no emoji anywhere, every icon surface renders `assets/sprites/mod-worm.svg` (`modWorm` has no `defaultEmoji`; the gimmick sprite chain resolves by key) and every `def.icon` consumer carries a missing-icon guard (the text share line just omits it). Par feature **`wormLoad`** = Σ per-egg (length × lifetime × pace) / `WORM_LOAD_SCALE` (100), pace belongs in the exposure measure because a slow worm covers numbers longer per move and lives longer in wall-clock. That is the SCHEDULED (maximum) dose; the FIT uses each row's REALIZED dose instead: every hatch is logged to `state.wormEvents` (`{t, r, c, len, life, pace, moves, tEnd?}`, traits embedded so R never re-derives the seeded RNG; `moves` = exact realized count, stamped `life` at burrow and `life − movesLeft` at stopTimer's finalize for worms alive at game end), submitted with daily/weekly/archive scores exactly as bombHitEvents are (rules-whitelisted per-field in both row families, pinned in `test/rules-contract.test.mjs`; extras contract pinned in `test/winSubmissionPlan.test.mjs`; persisted/restored with the save). Fitting on the schedule would ATTENUATE the coefficient, fast solvers and late hatches realize less of it, systematically. The refit computes `wormRealized = Σ len × moves / 100` per row (falling back to scheduled for old-client rows without events) and ships `secPerWormLoad` bridged by the play-weighted realization ratio, since predictPar can only know the schedule pre-play, pre-programmed segment-moves in hundreds (~0.6-12, the count features' numeric range), fully structural (derives from egg positions + the payload's `rngSeed`, which every recompute site now threads into `computeDailyFeatures`, client state carries `dailyRngSeed`/`weeklyRngSeed`, canonical recomputes pass `rngSeed`), in the verify sweep's hard-fail keys; egg COUNT alone can't carry the burden once lifetime varies 30-80. The R refit gates the `wormLoad` term on real data existing (`add_worm_term`, the archivePlay zero-variance pattern) AND zero-guards the shipped `secPerWormLoad` below 20 nonzero plays. Tests: `test/worms.test.mjs`.
- **Displayed-number source of truth:** `recomputeDisplayedMines(board)` in `gimmicks.js` is the single function that writes `displayedMines`. Called at the end of `applyGimmicks` and after any mine-layout change (`defuseMine`, `shieldDefuse`, `magnetPull`, `performMineShift`).
- **Adjacency source of truth:** `src/logic/adjacency.js` owns the whole question of what touches what, `wallKey`, `hasWallBetween`, `buildNeighborCache`, `defineCellNeighbors` (see the Board Topology section). It is a LEAF module importing nothing, which is what lets `gimmicks.js` and `boardSolver.js` both depend on it; the cycle between those two is why the primitive used to be split across them. `countAdjacentMines(board, r, c, neighborCache?)` (per-cell) and `recalcAllAdjacency(board)` (whole board) in `gimmicks.js` are the ONE mine counter and now read their neighbors from `buildNeighborCache` rather than re-deriving them; `boardGenerator` and `powerUps` call them rather than re-implementing the loop. **Pass a cache to `countAdjacentMines` from any loop**, bare, it derives the whole board's neighbor lists per call, turning an O(cells) sweep into O(cells²). **The convention: a mine carries no number, so its `adjacentMines` is ALWAYS 0.** Four copies used to exist and they disagreed on exactly that branch, `calculateAdjacency` (now deleted) SKIPPED mine cells, so a cell `swapMines` promoted from safe to mine kept the count it held while safe; that stale value serialized into a canonical and the nightly verify sweep flagged it (`dailyBoard/2026-07-16`, 2026-07-10). The deliberate scoped exceptions (each honoring the mine=0 convention) are `boardGenerator`'s incremental `placeMineIncremental` / `removeMineIncremental` (O(8) `neighborCache` deltas) and `cruxExtract`'s teaser mini-board recompute; the sonar radius-2 / compass ray / wormhole-sum scans in `recomputeDisplayedMines` are different geometry, not this counter. Pinned by `test/adjacency.test.mjs`.
- **Mirror is a 2-cell adjacent swap** (not a 2x2 zone). Pair count scales 1-3 with intensity. Cells store `mirrorPair = { row, col, pairIndex }`.
- **Walls:** `applyWalls` ALWAYS calls `recalcAllAdjacency` at the end, even when its isolation check forces walls to be cleared. Without this, cells retain stale wall-aware counts from a prior pass (shown-too-low vs. actual mines). `applyGimmicks` skips re-rolling walls when `board._wallEdges` is already populated (challenge mode pre-applies walls via `preWallEdges` so the constructive generator can build a wall-aware layout; re-rolling would invalidate it).
- **Wall rendering:** `renderWallOverlays()` is called at the end of `newGame()` so daily mode's pre-generated walls are visible before first click. Challenge and chaos modes also call it inside their first-click handler (gimmicks apply on first click in those modes). Wall lines are pixel-anchored to live cell rects (like the worm overlay), so any path that moves the cells must re-render them: the window-resize handler and `applyThemeLive`'s post-CSS refit both call `renderWallOverlays()` (idempotent, drops and rebuilds the overlay container). Pinned by `tests/e2e/wallResize.spec.mjs`.

## Achievements (skill-feat rebuild, 2026-06-10)

Value-derived categories in src/logic/achievements.js (5 tiers each, bronze/silver/gold/emerald/diamond, one drawn medal per tier; platinum was dropped 2026-06-23 so the ladder matches the 5-medal set, no stored unlock state, tiers recompute from stats counters). **Challenge Climber re-based for Challenge 250** (thresholds 25/100/200/250/300): the ladder's own landmarks, opener capstone, the L100 and L200 milestones, the L250 crown, with diamond at 300, fifty levels PAST the crown, so it can only be earned in the endless zone. Because medals recompute from `maxLevelReached`, which the progression reset zeroes, everyone re-earns them on the new ladder with no migration. The 2026-06-10 rebuild cut the two pure-grind categories (Survivor = duplicate total-wins, Dedicated = total games played) and added four SKILL FEATS, each honestly detectable from the click timeline + the board's CERTIFIED solve, never heuristics: **Flagless** (timeline has no flag action), **No Wasted Clicks** (player reveal+chord count <= certified click count, reconstructed from stored move-type counters via the solver invariant), **Tank Commander** (board provably required search reasoning: advancedLogicMoves >= 1), **Lie Detector** (provably required disjunctive liar reasoning). Feat booleans are computed in winLossHandler at win time and passed to saveGameResult({skillFeats}) which increments flaglessWins / efficientWins / searchWins / liarWins in stats (backfilled to 0 for existing saves). Feature-based feats only fire where a feature vector exists (daily / weekly / timed); challenge wins can still earn Flagless; chaos earns nothing.

## Theme System
- **26 kept themes** unlocked by level progression (trimmed from 50 in 2026-06: every kept theme carries the full per-theme kit, objects + contrasting grout + confetti palette + reveal choreography; the 24 cut themes live in git history and restoring one means restoring its CSS + registry entries and bringing it up to the contract). Count is FROZEN, the feel budget goes into deepening these, not adding palettes.
- **Unlock ladder rule (re-spread for Challenge 250):** classic + dark free at level 0; the other 24 themes unlock at the opener capstone **L25** (clearing the openers, the door to the shapes), then **one every 10 levels from L30 to L250**, so Legendary lands on the crown. Every unlock sits ON a checkpoint, or a player could pass it mid-block and miss the reward moment. **Themes fully relock at the C250 progression reset** (unlocks derive from maxLevelReached, which the reset zeroes, nothing per-theme is stored). Order unchanged, by IN-YOUR-FACE-NESS (quiet print-and-paper worlds first; loud, animated, high-saturation worlds as late-game rewards). `test/themeUnlockLadder.test.mjs` enforces the structure: L25 then every 10th to `CHALLENGE_MAX_LEVEL`, no gaps or doubles, every level a checkpoint multiple, the last theme on the crown, registry listed in unlock order (the Collection grid renders in registry order). Restoring a cut theme means giving it a ladder slot, which bumps everything after it.
- CSS custom properties per theme in `src/styles/themes/`
- `THEME_UNLOCKS` in `src/ui/themeManager.js` maps theme to required level. The boot path guards saved themes that are locked OR no longer exist (cut themes fall back to the highest unlocked).
- Num colors 9-20 exist for wormhole sums (9-18 for the rectangular 8+8 range; 19-20 added 2026-08-01 for tiling sums, a rhombille pair of valence-10 cells reaches 20, the ceiling `test/wormholeSumTokens.test.mjs` computes from the shipped lattices); dark themes override them with bright variants
- `--color-wall` contrast must be >= 3.5:1 against BOTH cell backgrounds (revealed AND hidden). For themes whose two backgrounds sit at opposite luminance extremes (light panes + dark fog, stained glass, apothecary, split-flap), no single color can satisfy both, so `.wall-line` supports a two-tone wall: the `--color-wall` body covers one side and `--color-wall-outline` (a 1.5px box-shadow ring, default transparent) covers the other. The floor is then: each background must be cleared by body OR outline.
- **Audio palettes**: `THEME_SOUND_PALETTES` in `src/audio/sounds.js` re-voices the four core moment sounds (reveal, flag, cascade, win) per theme. ALL 25 non-classic themes ship a voice (2026-06-10); `classic` is deliberately paletteless, the default synth IS its identity. Voice-design rules are in the file header (distinctness = timbre x register x direction; saw moments set the optional `cascade.vol`/`win.vol`; cascades may descend but `base + 7*step` must stay audible). `test/themeSoundPalettes.test.mjs` enforces shape, loudness ceilings, key integrity against THEME_UNLOCKS (a mis-keyed palette silently never fires), and a pairwise distinctness gate, new palettes must claim an unoccupied corner of the voice space.
- **Receipt-moment tokens** (`--receipt-pulse-color`, `--receipt-verdict-bg/-border`, `--receipt-crux-outline`) are reserved in `:root` so the deduction-receipt surfaces land themed per world when they ship.

### Theme Expression Tokens (the "wildly different per theme" layer)
The `:root` block in `global.css` exposes a large token contract so a theme can diverge in **motion, type, texture, cell treatment, and feedback colors**, not just palette. Every token defaults byte-for-byte to the value that used to be hardcoded, so a theme that overrides nothing renders exactly as before. Groups:
- **Motion:** `--anim-reveal-dur/-ease`, `--anim-flag-dur/-ease`, `--anim-mine-dur/-ease`, `--ease-bounce`, `--ease-standard`. Only the `animation` shorthand (duration/easing) is tokenized, `@keyframes` interiors can't read vars, so a theme changes *choreography* by shipping its own keyframes in its theme file. History note: the flag/mine tokens were declared but DEAD (`.cell.flag-pop` / `.cell.mine-chain` hardcoded their literals) until 2026-06-10; the root defaults were corrected to the effective literals (flag 0.35s, mine 0.4s) when wired, so tokenless themes render byte-identically while the ~17 themes that had set flag tokens finally animate as designed. Flag choreography overrides use `[data-theme=X] .cell.flag-pop { animation-name: ... }`.
- **Type scale:** `--fs-title`, `--fs-lcd`, `--fs-cell-number`, `--fs-mine`, `--fs-flag`.
- **Cell + sizing:** `--cell-shape-radius`; `--cell-min-size` / `--cell-max-size` / `--cell-fit-max-size` are read by JS in `boardRenderer.js` (`resizeCells`/`adjustCellSize`) because JS owns `--cell-size` at runtime, a CSS `--cell-size` override would be clobbered, so these bound tokens are the only way a theme can widen/tighten cells. `--grid-gap` also participates in board width (candy 3px, matrix 1px vs the 2px default), so BOTH fitters read the live computed gap and `applyThemeLive` re-runs `resizeCells` after the new theme's lazy stylesheet loads (`loadThemeCSS` returns a promise resolving on link.onload), without that refit a wider gap pushed the fit-content board past its container (cells spilling off the side, fixed 2026-07-12; pinned by `tests/e2e/themeCellFit.spec.mjs`). `--cell-gap-seal` = the grout color painted between cleared cells when an effect is on (see Effect layering). It defaults to `--color-cell-revealed`, but **that default is the banned "slab" look (grout == tile) and must NEVER ship on an effect-bearing theme**, see the grout-contrast HARD rule below. Every theme that registers a `THEME_EFFECTS` entry sets its own contrasting grout (forest sage, candy pink, circuit-board copper trace, nest twig weave, inferno lava crack, stained-glass dark lead, etc.).
- **Backgrounds:** `--bg-page-image` (a fixed `body::before` layer, default `none`), `--bg-page-blend`, `--bg-board` (`#board-container`). Vector only (gradients / CSS patterns / SVG data-URIs), no raster assets, so no `sw.js` ASSETS/CACHE_NAME coupling. Animated / board ambiance routes through `src/ui/themeEffects.js`, not CSS. **Effect layering:** the `.theme-fx` container is `z-index: 1`, between unrevealed cells (z-index auto/0) and revealed cells (`.cell.revealed` is `z-index: 2`), so ambient animations (rain, leaves, sparks, stars) play over the fog + background gaps but never wash over revealed numbers/markers. Walls stay on top at `z-index: 3`.
