Each theme's effect should keep particle counts modest but the particles large/visible (the original Forest spores were 2-4 sub-5px fading dots = invisible; falling leaves at 13-22px read clearly). **Gap-seal occlusion:** lifting revealed cells above the effect handles the cell *bodies*, but the 1-2px inter-cell *gaps* would still show the effect as slivers. `applyThemeEffects` adds `.fx-on` to `#board` only when a particle effect is registered (effect-less themes are byte-identical, no seal, no z-index lift). **Software-rendering gate (2026-06-17):** `applyThemeEffects` early-returns (treating every effect theme as effect-less, no `.fx-on`) under `prefers-reduced-motion` OR software compositing, `isSoftwareRendering()` in `src/logic/deviceCapability.js` probes the WebGL `UNMASKED_RENDERER` and matches SwiftShader / Microsoft Basic Render Driver / llvmpipe / software (or no GL context at all). Per-frame DOM particles are cheap on a GPU but stutter the whole game on the CPU, so a software-compositing browser (integrated/VM/remote, or a GPU-driver fallback that can hit even a strong card) keeps the theme's colors/sprites/static backdrop but loses the live particles. The pure string matcher `isSoftwareRenderer(str)` is node-tested in `test/softwareRender.test.mjs`; an unknown/blocked renderer string is conservatively treated as NOT software (real GPU with the debug ext hidden for privacy must keep its effects). As of wave 2 (2026-06-10), 24 of 26 themes register an effect; only classic and dark stay deliberately bare (restraint is their identity, like classic's default synth voice). Wave 2 effects (editorial ink flecks + headline rules, sumi-e ink motes + brush wisps, blueprint drafting ticks + measure lines, cartography route dashes + sounding rings, origami gliding paper triangles, nest gulls gliding across the sky) are VECTOR-DRAWN, styled divs with gradients / clip-paths / dash patterns, never emoji glyph particles, per the "worlds apart, less emoji" direction; prefer that style for any future effect work. Under `.fx-on`, each revealed cell gets a two-layer seal: a `::before` SQUARE solid fill of `--cell-gap-seal` over cell+gap (z-index -2; square so four neighbors tile every 4-cell junction shut, a rounded ring or box-shadow leaves a diamond there, the inherent weak spot of rounded corners), and a `::after` rounded `--color-cell-revealed` body on top (z-index -1) so each tile shows its own surface and the grout reads only in the gaps. The seal EXCLUDES the face-cue markers, sonar/compass/wormhole (translucent bg tints), locked/pressure-plate/defused (`::after` glyphs), and MIRROR (dashed border): the fx-on z-lift makes each revealed cell a stacking context, and negative-z pseudos paint ABOVE the element's own background and borders, so a sealed mirror cell's dashes were buried (found 2026-06-11, the mirror daily on chalkboard, invisible pairs in prod). The tint markers get an opaque revealed base composited under their tint on `.fx-on` so the effect can't bleed through them; a mirror PAIR is adjacent, so the seam between the two excluded cells can show a 1px effect sliver (accepted). Liar (outline, outlines paint last) and mystery (text) survive the body layer and keep the full seal. Colorblind mode (whose `::after` is a shape glyph) forces `--cell-gap-seal` back to the revealed color so the tile reads as one cleared surface. Verified by dyeing the effect a unique color and pixel-sampling every gap + junction: 0 leak. **Holistic fog art (2026-06-11):** `#board` consumes `--bg-fog-art` (default `none`) as a background-image sized 100% x 100% under the cells; a theme sets it to ONE large vector drawing (data-URI SVG or gradient) and switches its `.cell.unrevealed` gradient + `--color-cell-hidden-hover` to TRANSLUCENT rgba tints, so the drawing reads through the fog as one continuous image spanning the board (revealed cells stay opaque -- revealing tears the sheet). Compositing base is `#board`'s background-color (`--color-border`); the gallery mini-boards mirror that. Seven worlds use it: origami (crane crease pattern), cartography (worn chart), nest (open sky, clouds + a corner sun), blueprint (cyanotype sheet + white number set), chalkboard (half-erased workings + chalk-dust number text-shadow), noir (venetian-blind bands), ocean (sounding chart). Fog must STILL read as interactive tiles: keep borders/hover and tint alphas around 0.3-0.55, and drawings need bolder strokes than instinct suggests (the fog tint sits on top).
- **Special-cell markers:** `--marker-liar-outline(-width)`, `--marker-sonar-tint`, `--marker-compass-tint`, `--marker-wormhole-0/1/2`, `--marker-mirror-0/1/2(-width)`, `--marker-plate-timer/-border/-glow`. See legibility contract below.
- **Overlays / power-ups / timer / victory:** `--overlay-*` (wrong/correct flag, suggest move/start, mine-hit glow), `--pu-*` (shield, xray, lifeline, magnet), `--timer-warning/-critical/-critical-glow`, `--victory-color/-duration`.

**Special-cell legibility contract (HARD FLOOR, do not relax):** marker tokens may be re-hued slightly by a theme only to avoid clashing with its own background. Every marker must stay (a) unmistakable as its modifier, (b) mutually distinct, (c) high-contrast vs the cell bg (line markers ≥ 3:1, like `--color-wall`), (d) above the alpha floors (tints ≥ 0.12, borders ≥ 0.6). Identity is anchored in a non-color channel (📡/arrow glyph, italic+underline, dashed border vs outline vs fill) so meaning never rests on hue alone. The markers keep their `!important` so an aggressive cell-bg can't bury them.

**Grout-contrast contract (HARD RULE, absolute, do not relax):** for any theme that registers a `THEME_EFFECTS` entry (so `.fx-on` is set and the gap-seal is painted), `--cell-gap-seal` MUST be a color that visibly CONTRASTS `--color-cell-revealed`, never equal to it. Grout == tile produces a solid "slab" where cleared cells merge into one undifferentiated block; this was explicitly rejected ("I see that we might be making grout fill that's the same as the tiles. Absolutely don't do that."). Cleared cells must always read as separate tiles set in a contrasting grout (chalk line on slate, copper trace on PCB, twig weave between nest tiles, dark lead between stained-glass panes, glowing lava crack between charred inferno tiles). Practical floor: luminance delta ≥ ~50 between grout and tile, and the grout should be thematically motivated (it's the "what's between the tiles" of the world), not an arbitrary outline. The default (`var(--color-cell-revealed)`) is intentionally the slab so an *effect-less* theme stays byte-identical, but it is a bug on any effect theme. Verify by revealing a region and confirming the gap lines are clearly a different color from the tile bodies. Colorblind mode is the one exception: it forces gap-seal back to the revealed color (the `::after` shape glyph carries identity instead).

**Per-theme objects + moments contract:** every theme defines its game objects and key moments in its own idiom, not just palette. (a) **Objects** live in `THEME_UNLOCKS` (`themeManager.js`) as emoji fields: `mine`, `flag`, `strikeCell` (the detonated cell, falls back to `mine` then `💣` if unset), plus `smiley`/`smileyWin`/`smileyLoss`. `getThemeEmoji(type)` in `boardRenderer.js` resolves them (theme-owned only, emoji packs, the old per-player override layer, were cut with the 2026-06-12 Collection declutter along with the effects and titles tabs; `collectionManager.js` is deleted and Collection is themes-only). Architected so hand-drawn SVGs swap in later via the same classes with zero JS rewiring. That swap is now live as **`THEME_SPRITES` in `src/ui/spriteLoader.js`** (theme -> {mine, flag, strikeCell} -> `assets/sprites/themes/<theme>-<object>.svg`): `getSpriteUrl` consults it before the Tier 1 canonical-emoji match, and a theme sprite renders ONLY when the resolved emoji equals that theme's own object emoji (always true now; kept as the identity contract between getThemeEmoji and THEME_UNLOCKS) -- undrawn themes keep their emoji verbatim. Batch 1 (2026-06-11) ships editorial / sumie / blueprint / cartography / chalkboard / splitflap / galaxy / circuitboard / nest (the egg / omelette / feather set, replacing the retired comic 2026-06-25) full sets plus noir's strikeCell (the crime-scene chalk body outline). All 28 files are in the sw.js ASSETS precache. Ship future worlds by adding files + one registry line. (b) **Reveal moment:** each theme overrides `.cell.num-pop, .cell.revealing { animation-name: <theme>Reveal }` with its own `@keyframes` in its theme file (editorialPrint, sumieBleed, candyPop, chalkScrawl, noirFlicker, glassBloom, flapFlip, circuitPower, nestHatch, …). (c) **Win moment:** per-theme confetti palette in `effectsRenderer.js` (the `themeColors` map inside `showConfettiBurst`, keyed by `state.theme`, beware: a palette under a stale key silently never fires; sakura's sat under its pre-rename key 'cherry-blossom' until 2026-06). (d) **Explosion moment:** `showExplosionBurst(originX, originY, count)` in `effectsRenderer.js` fires a radial, theme-colored detonation at the hit cell from `chainRevealMines` (challenge/timed loss). It reuses `showConfettiBurst`'s particle engine via physics `opts` (the confetti defaults are unchanged). A new theme must supply, at minimum, its objects + a contrasting grout (if it has an effect) + a confetti palette entry.

**Regression invariant + how to verify:** because every default equals the old literal, all themes are byte-identical until one deliberately overrides a token. The frozen computed-style probe is a COMMITTED TOOL: `scripts/theme-style-probe.html` (serve the repo with `python -m http.server 8080`, open the page, it hashes synthetic marker/overlay/timer/cell elements across all 26 themes; capture `window.__probeResults` on the BASELINE checkout, then paste into the changed checkout's page and Diff). Implementation notes baked into the tool: animations are frozen via `animation-play-state: paused` at delay 0, NOT `animation: none`, which would erase `animation-name`/`duration` from computed styles and blind the probe to choreography identity; stylesheet links are cache-busted per run (a same-browser baseline would otherwise diff cached CSS against itself and report "no differences" for real changes); `.num-pop`/`.revealing` probe elements carry reveal-choreography identity; keyframe INTERIORS remain by-eye review. Workflow for a theme wave: `git stash push` the theme edits → baseline → `git stash pop` → diff; only deliberately redesigned themes may appear.

**Grandfathered carve-outs (don't "fix" blindly):** ~15 themes use `background-clip: text` gradient titles and `.modal` uses `backdrop-filter: blur(4px)`, both pre-existing and intentional; keep them but don't add *new* gradient-clip titles or decorative glass to showcase themes that lack them. Orbitron + Fredoka webfonts are loaded in index.html and used by aurora/galaxy/candy as real `--font-display` values (not dead). Two parallel timer color systems exist (`.lcd-display.timer-*` and `.timer-*` with `!important`); both now read `--timer-warning`/`--timer-critical`, whose defaults are the effective `!important` colors (`#ff9800` / `#f44336`).

## Firebase
- SDK v10.14.1 (compat) loaded via CDN in index.html: App, Database, Auth, Messaging, App Check
- Config hardcoded in `firebaseLeaderboard.js`, `initFirebase()` with 5s connection timeout
- **App Check (abuse protection, monitor-mode scaffold):** `activateAppCheck()` in `firebaseLeaderboard.js`, called from `initFirebase()` right after `initializeApp`. No-ops until `APP_CHECK_SITE_KEY` is set (same manual-console-prereq pattern as the VAPID key: register the web app under App Check in the Firebase console with a reCAPTCHA provider, paste the site key, set `APP_CHECK_PROVIDER` to `'v3'` or `'enterprise'` to match the registration). Skipped on the test build and localhost (neither is on the reCAPTCHA domain allowlist, and their traffic must not pollute the production verified-traffic metrics that gate the enforcement decision). Activation only ATTACHES attestation tokens, nothing is rejected until enforcement is flipped on per-product in the console, and enforcement must wait until stale SW-cached clients (which lack the App Check SDK) have cycled onto a build that includes it. Rationale: the hardcoded Firebase config works from ANY origin (anonymous auth is not gated by authorized domains), so a rehosted copy of the site can read/write the production database; App Check is the layer that eventually rejects that. Note: once the key is set, production clients load Google's reCAPTCHA script, revisit the README "no tracking" phrasing at that point.
- **Licensing posture:** LICENSE at repo root (all rights reserved, view/fork only, no license to copy/modify/rehost), README License section, `<meta name="copyright">` + title-screen `.title-copyright` line + About-modal credit in index.html. The explicit notice is DMCA-takedown hygiene for the "someone rehosts the game" scenario.
- Rate limiting: 30s cooldown per submission path (daily/archive/timed each have their own clock, a timed win must never suppress a daily submission); a daily submission landing inside its own cooldown is QUEUED for retry, not dropped. The retry queues resolve the uid at FLUSH time (never the enqueue-frozen uid, which an account-link switch invalidates).
- Score validation: 5-3600 seconds
- Database paths:
  - `daily/{dateString}/{pushId}`, leaderboard score objects (`name, time, bombHits, uid, par, timestamp, rngSeed, bombHitEvents, wormEvents`). `hintEvents` ([{t, kind}]) is a RETIRED field: the Lens that produced it was removed 2026-07-18 and no build emits it, but the rules still whitelist it (see The Lens, removed) and the R refit still filters `n_hints == 0` so historical hinted rows stay out of the fit. `rngSeed` is OMITTED when it equals the dateString (plain-date seeds), reconstruct via `effectiveRowSeed` in `src/logic/scoreRowMatch.js` before comparing seeds. One row per (uid, effective board seed) is enforced client-side at submission, see Submission dedupe in the Canonical Daily Board section.
  - `dailyArchive/{date}/{pushId}`, replay rows for PAST dailies (the Daily Archive, see that section). Same shape as a `daily/` row plus a required `archivePlay === true` and an optional `cruxViewed` boolean. SEPARATE path from `daily/` by design so the live day-of leaderboard never shows replay rows. World-read, append-only, uid-owned (rules cloned from the `daily` block). Feeds the par fit as nuisance-corrected data; the live leaderboard ignores it.
  - `parLab/{pushId}`, Par Lab result rows (world-read, append-only, uid-owned, server-`timestamp`, rules cloned from the `timed` block; `$other: false` with the row-fields lockstep pinned in `test/parLab.test.mjs`). Written by `src/firebase/parLabSync.js`, the ONE deliberately un-test-gated writer, because the lab exists only on test builds and these rows ARE the dataset (see the Par Lab section). Consumed only by the offline prior-seeding analysis, filtered to Christopher's uid; nothing in the game reads it.
  - `dailyMeta/{dateString}`, per-date board features (write-once, public-read), `{ features: {...}, writtenAt }`. Read by the R refit. Written at GENERATION time by the precompute scripts (daily dates and `{weekStart}_weekly_first`) so the generator's solver counts are canonical; the first-score-submission upsert and `backfill-features.html` remain as fallbacks for dates the precompute missed.
  - `cruxes/{dateString}`, crux teaser payload for the `?crux=` share route (write-once, public-read), `{ rows, cols, cells:[{r,c,n}], answer:{r,c}, sources:[{r,c}], walls?:[edgeKey], tier, sentence, sentenceSocratic, writtenAt }`. A self-contained "find the safe square" mini-puzzle of a PAST daily's hardest step (see Crux Teaser). Written at generation by the precompute/regenerate scripts and backfilled by `scripts/backfill-cruxes.mjs`. ~0.6 KB.
  - `boardHeatmap/{dateString}`, per-cell bomb-hit rollup for a SETTLED past daily (write-once, public-read), `{ rows, cols, cells: { "{r}_{c}": hitCount }, totals, n_players, writtenAt }`. `cells` is SPARSE (only detonated cells) and therefore OPTIONAL in the rules: a board nobody detonated aggregates to `{}`, which Firebase stores as absent, so requiring it would reject exactly the cleanest boards. `".write": false`, no client may write here; the service-account rollup (`scripts/rollup-board-heatmap.mjs`) is the only writer (see Board Heatmap). ~0.2 KB.
  - `dailyBoard/{dateString}`, canonical board layout for the date (write-once, public-read), `{ rows, cols, totalMines, rngSeed, codeVersion, activeGimmicks, gatedCert?, cells: [{isMine, adjacentMines, displayedMines, isLocked, isMystery, ...}, ...], wallEdges?, writtenAt }`. `gatedCert: true` marks boards certified under the reveal-gated solver contract (see No-Guess Contract); its absence means the historical ungated contract. The source of truth for "what board does today look like", `gameActions.js`'s daily branch fetches this BEFORE local generation so every player on the same ET date plays the exact same layout regardless of cached code or experimentTarget. Written by the GitHub Actions pre-gen workflow at 00:00 UTC; the first-client fallback writer is gone for the daily (the live daily aborts on a canonical miss rather than inventing a board, see the divergence block above), while the WEEKLY keeps its first-client writer by ruling. Each cell ships with both `adjacentMines` AND `displayedMines` so future changes to wall-aware adjacency or gimmick-display logic can't retroactively shift historical numbers. ~1-3 KB per day.
  - `users/{uid}/`, cloud progress sync (dailyStreak, bestDailyStreak, lastDailyDate, `moltDay = { banked, lastUse }`, see Molt Day; `challenge250 = { epoch, maxCheckpoint, powerUps }`, the atomic Challenge 250 progression node, see the Challenge 250 section; the legacy top-level `maxCheckpoint` + `powerUps.challenge` stay whitelisted for pre-reset clients but post-reset code never reads them)
  - `users/{uid}/dailyHistory/{dateString}`, per-user completion record (`time, submittedAt`, plus `archive: true` on archive-replay rows). Written on every daily completion (and on first archive completion of a past date), read on every leaderboard-modal open for the SVG history chart and at boot for the streak reconciler (which skips archive-marked rows).
  - `users/{uid}/weeklyCompletions/{weekStart}`, per-week weekly completion record (`{timestamp}`, write-once, owner-scoped; `backfilled: true` on entries `scripts/backfill-weekly-completions.mjs` seeded from the leaderboard rows). Written by `markWeeklyCompleted` on the week's first completion, read by `fetchCompletedWeeks` for the Past Weeklies `'done'` marks and the week-streak heal's post-epoch half (`WEEKLY_COMPLETIONS_EPOCH`, see the Weekly bullets under Game Modes).
  - `users/{uid}/friends/{friendUid}`, mutual friend list entry (`name, addedAt`). Owner-only READ (lists are private). The write rule carries a deliberate exception: a stranger may write/delete ONLY the entry keyed by their own uid (`auth.uid === $friendUid`), that is why one code redemption serves BOTH sides. Self-friendship rejected in validate. Accepted tradeoff: anyone who learns a uid could self-insert into that list (visible in the Friends tab, removable, fine at this community's scale).
  - `friendCodes/{CODE}`, ephemeral friend codes (`uid, name, createdAt`), CODE = 6 chars of `A-Z2-9` (no 0/O/1/I/L). 15-minute life enforced SERVER-SIDE by the rules read gate (`createdAt > now - 900000`); multi-use within the window BY DESIGN (one code on a classroom projector serves the whole class); create-only-if-absent (no hijacking); anyone authed may delete (cleanup). The 3-hourly scrub workflow deletes codes older than 1h. test/friendCodes.test.mjs pins the TTL + regex against src/logic/friendCodes.js; test/friendsRules.test.mjs pins the rule structure.
- Anonymous auth (`firebaseProgress.js`): silent sign-in on load, no UI. **Test environments never MINT Auth users** (2026-07-06): `initAnonymousAuth` passes a no-op `signInAnon` into `bootstrapAnonymousAuth` when `isTestEnvironment()`, and `signOut()`'s re-anonymize in `firebaseAuth.js` carries the same gate, account creation is a production write like any other. The auth listener stays attached either way, so a persisted session is still adopted (auth persistence is origin-scoped IndexedDB, which is why `gregsweeper.com/test/` sees the prod session); a fresh test/e2e context just plays signed out, which every uid consumer null-guards. Pinned by the smoke spec's `accounts:signUp` network assertion. `saveProgress` calls before auth completes are coalesced into a pending-save and flushed once `_ready` flips, so fast daily completions on slow connections don't drop their cloud sync. **The uid is reactive, see Account Linking below.** Modules that depend on per-user data (push subscription, daily-residuals cache, settings UI render) subscribe via `subscribeToUidChanges` so they reload when the user signs in on a second device.
- Cloud sync: saves on checkpoint advance + daily completion, loads on init. Challenge checkpoint adopts the max WITHIN the epoch-matched `challenge250` node (see the Challenge 250 reset). Daily streak is date-anchored: cloud date > local date adopts cloud's streak AND date verbatim (even if streak went down, the most recent play has the latest info), same date takes the higher streak, cloud stale keeps local. `bestDailyStreak` is always the high-water mark. The molt-day bank + lastUse ride this same snapshot (see Molt Day) so a cross-device merge can't pair a bank with the wrong streak.
- Falls back to localStorage leaderboards if Firebase unavailable
- Security rules live in `firebase-rules.json` and are AUTO-DEPLOYED on every push to main via `.github/workflows/deploy-firebase-rules.yml`. (`firebase deploy --only database`, config in `.firebaserc` + `firebase.json`, is the manual equivalent.) The file IS the deployed ruleset; edits to it ship.
- **Timestamp fields validated `=== now` (e.g. `daily/*/timestamp`, `users/*/dailyHistory/*/submittedAt`) MUST be written with `firebase.database.ServerValue.TIMESTAMP`, never a client `Date.now()`.** The server sentinel is the only value that equals server `now`; a client number is silently rejected and the whole write dropped. This froze every stats page from 2026-05-25 to 2026-06-08, when commit 866683d swapped the sentinel for `Date.now()` on the dailyHistory write (to make the offline queue serializable) and the rule rejected every completion after.

## Account Linking (cross-device sign-in)
The default anonymous uid per device makes phone and PC look like two unrelated players. `src/firebase/firebaseAuth.js` adds Google and email-link sign-in so the user can upgrade the anonymous account on one device and sign in to the same identity on another, both devices then share the same uid and `users/{uid}/*` syncs automatically (streak, dailyHistory, weeklyAttempts, checkpoints, handicap).

- **Provider 1 (Google):** `linkWithGoogle()` calls `auth.currentUser.linkWithPopup(GoogleAuthProvider)`. On the first device this upgrades the anonymous account (uid unchanged). On a second device the credential is already attached to the first device's uid, so Firebase throws `auth/credential-already-in-use`; the module catches it, shows a confirmation modal (delegated to the `onCredentialConflict` callback supplied by `main.js`), then calls `signInWithCredential` to SWITCH the second device's session to the existing uid. The abandoned anonymous Auth record is `.delete()`d as part of the switch so it doesn't linger in Firebase Auth.
- **Provider 2 (Email link / passwordless):** `sendEmailLink(email)` → Firebase emails a one-time link → `tryCompleteEmailLink()` (called at boot in `init()` once auth has settled) finishes the sign-in or switch. The originating device stashes the email in `localStorage['minesweeper_pending_email_link']`; on a different device the boot flow calls the `promptForEmail` callback so the user re-enters their email (Firebase security requires the email to be confirmed on the completing device).
- **Apple sign-in is intentionally deferred.** It requires an Apple Developer Program enrollment ($99/yr), a Service ID in Apple Developer, and a private key uploaded to Firebase. Can be added later without disturbing the existing flow.
- **The uid is reactive at runtime.** `firebaseProgress.js`'s anonymous-auth init now subscribes to `onAuthStateChanged` rather than capturing the uid once. `subscribeToUidChanges(callback)` fires whenever the uid flips. Callbacks distinguish `isInitial: true` (first auth settle of the session, no in-memory state to clear) from `isInitial: false` (mid-session switch, old uid's queued writes are discarded, push token is re-minted under the new uid via `initPushAuthListener` → `refreshTokenIfStale`, and the daily-residuals cache is rebuilt from the new uid's Firebase history).
- **V1 scope is intentionally simple:** signing in on the second device ABANDONS that device's anonymous data, both leaderboard rows under the old uid and the `users/{old_uid}/*` subtree stay in Firebase but are no longer reachable from the player. The confirmation modal warns the user before the switch. No merge logic (no taking max across two uids' streaks/checkpoints, no rewriting old leaderboard rows). If we ever want merge, it's additive, add a pre-switch snapshot step in `firebaseAuth.js`'s conflict handler and write merged values into `users/{new_uid}/*` after the credential switch.
- **Push subscription handoff.** When the user signs in (uid switches), `firebaseAuth.js` first writes `users/{old_uid}/pushSubscription = null` while still authed as the old uid, so the hourly send-push cron stops firing to the abandoned device. `firebasePush.js`'s `subscribeToUidChanges` listener then calls `refreshTokenIfStale` on the new uid, if that uid has `notificationPrefs.enabled === true`, a fresh FCM token is minted and written under the new uid (overwriting any existing token), so pushes follow the player to the device that just signed in. If the new uid hasn't enabled notifications, the device stays unsubscribed until the user toggles it on.
- **Manual Firebase Console prerequisites (one-time, before this feature works):**
  - Authentication → Sign-in method → enable **Google** (no extra cost).
  - Authentication → Sign-in method → enable **Email link (passwordless sign-in)**. The action-URL is computed dynamically (`?emailLink=1` on the current origin) so test and prod both work without further config.
  - Authentication → **Settings tab** → Authorized domains → add `gregsweeper.com` (the production custom domain) and `christopherwells.github.io` (the GitHub Pages host). Anonymous auth doesn't need this (server-side credential issuance) but Google sign-in's popup-return DOES, otherwise the popup throws `auth/unauthorized-domain`. Firebase auto-adds `localhost` and `<project>.firebaseapp.com`; the serving domain has to be added manually.
  - No security-rule changes needed for V1 (the existing `users/$uid/*` and `daily/$date/$entry` rules continue to work, sign-in just changes which uid the device authenticates as).
- **Verifying via diagnostics:** the Diagnostics modal (`?debug=1` then Settings → Show Diagnostics) shows a "Signed in" row reporting provider + email. If two devices report the same uid and email, the link worked.

## Leaderboard Name Filtering (hate speech)
Two-layer filter so slurs don't reach the daily/weekly leaderboards. Scope is **hate speech only** (racial, ethnic, homophobic, transphobic, misogynistic slurs), NOT general profanity, to keep false positives near zero.

- **Client reject** (`src/logic/nameFilter.js` + `hateSpeechTerms.js`): `setPlayerName` (statsStorage.js) calls `containsHateSpeech`, which normalizes (lowercase, leetspeak map `4→a 3→e 1→i 0→o @→a $→s …`, strip non-letters, collapse 3+ repeats to 2) then substring-matches the **client-safe** term subset (`HATE_SPEECH_TERMS_CLIENT_SAFE`, only terms that don't collide with innocent words, so a dumb `includes()` won't reject "Nigeria"/"Pakistani"/"RaccoonKing"/"SanDiego"/"Fagan"). `setPlayerName` returns `{ ok, reason }`; the Settings name input toasts "Please choose a different name" on the `change` event (not per-keystroke) and reverts to the last good name. Rejected names are never saved.
- **Server sweep** (`scripts/scrub-leaderboard-names.mjs` + `.github/workflows/scrub-leaderboard-names.yml`, cron `0 */3 * * *` + `workflow_dispatch` with a `--dry-run` input): the authoritative backstop for names written before the filter shipped, clever evasions, or direct-to-Firebase writes. Uses the **union** of two detectors, `obscenity` (boundary- + whitelist-aware matching of the **fuller** `HATE_SPEECH_TERMS` list, so colliding terms like coon/spic/paki are caught WITHOUT flagging raccoon/Nigeria/Pakistan; whitelist enumerated in the script) and the client normalizer (aggressive leetspeak obscenity's transformer misses, e.g. `tr4nny→tranny`). Scrubs offending names in every world-readable leaderboard path, `daily/{date}/{pushId}`, `weekly/{weekStart}/{uid}`, `timed/{pushId}`, and `dailyArchive/{date}/{pushId}`, to **"Anonymous"** (`test/scrubCoverage.test.mjs` derives that path set from the rules file, so a new name-bearing path can't ship unswept) (replace, not delete, the score/streak isn't the problem, the name is). The user can set a clean name anytime.
- **Term lists are in `src/logic/hateSpeechTerms.js`**, a deliberately separate, content-warned blocklist module. Two exports: `HATE_SPEECH_TERMS_CLIENT_SAFE` (low-collision, for naive client substring) and `HATE_SPEECH_TERMS` (fuller, server-only, safe because obscenity is boundary-aware). NEVER feed the fuller list to a naive substring matcher.

## Commit Convention
- No "Claude" or "Opus" or AI attribution in commits
- Git identity: Christopher Wells <c.wells@bowdoin.edu>

## Version
Current: **v1.10** (`CURRENT_VERSION` in `main.js`, drives the What's New NEW badge), cache `gregsweeper-v1.10.N` in `sw.js` where N is the live per-deploy counter (read it from `sw.js`; it moves on nearly every PR, so a number written here goes stale within a day and is deliberately not pinned). The two are kept in lockstep on `MAJOR.MINOR`, see the versioning rule under Deploy. **v1.10 is the Coastline + Challenge 250 release (2026-08-04)**: the daily shape rotation flipped live and the 250-level ladder replaced the sawtooth, bundled as ONE release because they are one arc and because the ladder is what walks a player through all seven shapes. **The weekly card additions (week streak, past weeklies, par) shipped on the `N` counter with NO What's New entry at all, his two rulings, 2026-08-05, each correcting the last: "this is not worth a whole .1 bump, these are really small increments", then "these aren't what's new worthy either."** The work is player-facing, which is the bar as written for a MINOR, and being player-facing turned out to be necessary and nowhere near sufficient, for either surface.
  - **The MINOR bar is the size of a RELEASE**, and v1.10 set that unit at six tilings plus a 250-level ladder. Three additions to one card is a different class of thing. Ask **would this headline a release next to the last one?** before reaching for a bump.
  - **WHAT'S NEW IS NOT A CHANGELOG.** That is the mistake underneath both rulings. Its entry is attached to a badge that interrupts a player, so the bar is not "did something change" but "is this worth stopping them for", and a card that explains itself the moment they look at it is not. The Past chip is visible, the streak and par read as what they are, so nothing needed announcing. Work of this kind ships silent: it lands on `N`, it goes in CLAUDE.md, and the player finds it where it lives.
  - Rolling a shipped bump back is cheap but not free: `CURRENT_VERSION` drives the NEW badge off `lastSeenVersion` inequality, so anyone who had already opened What's New under the withdrawn version sees the badge once more. v1.10 is also the standing example of the rule working in the other direction: six tilings, the per-shape par model, the Par Lab and the par bands all landed on the `N` counter first, because none of them was a thing a player could feel until this commit made them reachable.
